# Ralph Progress Log
Started: 2026年 5月 2日 星期六 19时50分14秒 CST
---

## Codebase Patterns
- ObjectSet snapshot subsystem has TWO distinct surfaces: (1) the in-memory `pkg/oss/objectset.Store` keyed by `createTemporary` RIDs (1h TTL), and (2) the durable `object_set_snapshots` table written via the `PersistedSnapshotStore` interface (US-224). The PG row interface lives in `pkg/oms` (`ObjectSetSnapshot` + `ObjectSetSnapshotStore`); the direction-of-dependency rule keeps `pkg/oms` from importing `pkg/oss/objectset`, so `cmd/server/object_set_snapshot_adapter.go` bridges them.
- A third durable surface — `saved_object_sets` (migration 000013) — is fully implemented (`pkg/oss/objectset/PGSavedStore`) but NOT YET wired into `cmd/server`. It has unit + integration tests but no HTTP route. New work that needs durable saved definitions can wire the existing store; no new schema is needed.
- Migration numbering convention: zero-padded `000NNN_description.{up,down}.sql`. Migrations are run with `golang-migrate`. As of US-365 the tip is migration 000082.
- Test build tags: unit tests run by default; `//go:build integration` tests need `go test -tags integration` and use `internal/testutil.StartPGContainer(t)` to boot a real Postgres container. The `ListEntries` helper on the in-memory `Store` is the canonical way to introspect ephemeral entries from tests.
- For canonical-JSON hashing across the codebase, prefer the `objectset.HashDefinition` helper (sorts keys, sha256 hex). Calling `json.Marshal` first is fine — the helper canonicalises again.
- Executor errors that need a non-default APIError mapping should be sentinels in `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go` (e.g. `ErrQueryTooLarge`) and translated by the `executeError(err) *apierror.APIError` helper in `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go`. All `h.executor.Execute` callers in this package go through that helper instead of hand-rolling `apierror.NewInvalidParameter("ObjectSetFailed", …)`. Add an `apierror.New<Code>` constructor in `pkg/apierror/error.go` for the wire-format code (e.g. `WEAVE_QUERY_TOO_LARGE` → 422).
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_path_test.go::seedMultiHopIndex` accepts `testing.TB` so unit tests AND benchmarks can share it. `multiHopResolver` is the canonical in-memory `LinkResolver` for path-traversal tests — it implements `ResolveLinkedObjectsByAPIName` (forward), `ResolveLinkedReverseByAPIName` (reverse), `ResolveTargetObjectType` and `ResolveTargetObjectTypeDir`. Bench paths should root at `Type:"static"` rather than `Type:"base"` to bypass Bleve in measurement loops; `Type:"base"` with the 10K cap can hang the in-memory Bleve index.
- `aggregation.AggregationRequest.Accuracy` is the Palantir-style request-level toggle (`ALLOW_APPROXIMATE` default, `REQUIRE_ACCURATE` opt-in). `computeMetrics(idx, baseQuery, specs, accuracyMode)` is the chokepoint that routes `approximateDistinct` → exact and `approximatePercentile` → sort-based exact when `REQUIRE_ACCURATE` is set. The fourth return value (`approximate bool`) flags when an approximate algorithm actually fired so the response.accuracy column reflects runtime truth, not just user intent. When adding a new approximate aggregation, route both the exact-fallback path AND the approximate-flag bookkeeping through this helper rather than emitting accuracy from the call site.
- HLL sparse-exact threshold: `1<<(precision-2)` (4096 at default p=14). Below this cardinality the axiomhq sketch is in its sparse compressed-list mode and returns byte-exact counts; above it transitions to dense estimation. Use `sparseExactThreshold(precision)` from `bleve_agg.go` rather than hard-coding the boundary, so a future precision change keeps the response.accuracy verdict consistent.
- `approximatePercentile` runs through `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile.go` (influxdata/tdigest) at compression `DefaultTDigestCompression=100`. That cap is the steady-state memory contract: max processed centroids `2c` + max unprocessed `8c` ≈ 16000 bytes ≤ 16 KiB ceiling (tested at 1M points). When a bucket has unit-weight centroids (any size below the digest's processed cap), Quantile reduces to *linear interpolation between two flanking sorted values at half-integer cumulative weights* — disagrees with HdrHistogram's nearest-rank/3-sig-fig convention, so any percentile reference test must use the same interpolation formula (see `phase6_percentile_groupby_test.go::referencePercentileForBucket`). HdrHistogram is no longer in the dependency graph.
- The `cmd/server` openapi contract test has TWO surfaces: every chi route must appear in `api/openapi.yaml`, AND `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` must be byte-identical to `api/openapi.yaml`. New routes need both an entry in `api/openapi.yaml` and a `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` step before the contract suite goes green. The two failing tests are `TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` and `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical`.
- For action features that need durable persistence with idempotency, the canonical wiring pattern is: (1) define a narrow `XStore` interface in `pkg/actions/` with the persistence operations only the executor needs (mirror `ActionApprovalStore`/`ActionJobStore`), (2) add `SetXStore`/`XStore()` setters on `Executor` so degraded-mode (no PG) tests inject nil and the feature falls back to in-memory behaviour, (3) build the concrete PG implementation in `cmd/server/<feature>_store.go` so `pkg/oms` does not need a `pkg/actions` import (deps already point one way). Idempotency conflicts surface as a sentinel error (`ErrSagaIdempotencyConflict`) detected via `errors.Is`; the executor races cleanly by re-reading the prior result row on the conflict branch.
- `BatchError.Cause` is `json:"-"` because the `error` interface has no general unmarshaller — when persisting a SagaResult and replaying it back, only `Phase`/`FailedActionIndex`/`ActionType`/`Message` survive the round-trip. Use `errors.As` against `*BatchError` for typed-error recovery in-process, but rely on `Message` for any wire-format response.
- Function-to-function dispatch infrastructure is in `pkg/functions/fncall` (context-propagated call stack with `MaxDepth=8`, `ErrDepthExceeded`, `ErrCycleDetected`) + `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go` (the `weave.callFunction(ref, params)` Goja shim). Any new feature that wants to reuse the depth/cycle invariants across nested calls should seed the top frame with `fncall.WithFrame(ctx, topRef)` before `runtime.Execute` and the runtime guards take over from there. Static cycle detection at publish time lives in `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go` (`ExtractCallTargets` + `DetectCallCycle`); the `functionCallGraphRepoOverlay` pattern in `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` is the publish-time wrapper that lets the in-flight row's NEW source code shadow whatever the repo would return for the same identity — reusable for any future "scan before persist" graph (action saga step graphs, automation-rule chains, etc.).
- AIP Logic flow editor patterns: realtime client-side validation lives in `web/src/components/aiplogic/LogicFlowsPage.tsx::validateGraph(rfNodes, rfEdges)` returning a `ValidationReport` with `cycle` / `unconnected` / `unboundParam` issue kinds — keep this focused on *editor smell* (orphans, dangling `{{ref}}`s) rather than mirroring the server's `Flow.Validate()`, which is the source of truth at save time. Node-level preview goes through the backend dry-run handler `POST /api/v2/aip/logic-flows/{flowId}/dry-run-node` (`pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::DryRunNode`) which delegates to `Executor.runNode` — failure surfaces inline as `trace.status=failed` at HTTP 200 so the SPA renders the failure in the same panel slot as success. The AIP Logic chi routes are NOT enforced by `cmd/server/contract_test.go` because the contract router stubs `AIPLogicStore=nil` and the registration block in `cmd/server/main.go` is gated on a non-nil store; new AIP Logic routes can land without OpenAPI updates as long as that gate stays in place.
- AIP Threads parent/branch invariants (US-374): every `Message` carries `ParentMessageID *int64` and `BranchID string` (default `"main"`). `MemoryStore.AppendMessage` and `pgAIPStore.AppendMessage` auto-link `ParentMessageID` to the last message on the same branch when the caller didn't supply one — preserving the linear-history contract for legacy callers that never set parent_message_id. Fork creates a fresh thread row, copies pivot's ancestor chain (root → pivot inclusive) into it under `branch_id='main'` with re-chained parents. PG fork uses a recursive CTE shape `JOIN aip_messages m ON m.id = (SELECT parent_message_id FROM aip_messages WHERE id = c.id)` — the parent column has to be re-projected via sub-select inside the recursive arm, NOT projected through the CTE itself. Tree builder `pkg/aip/handlers_fork.go::BuildMessageTree` is orphan-tolerant: a message whose parent id is absent from the slice surfaces as a root rather than being silently dropped (necessary because `ON DELETE SET NULL` may leave dangling pointers). To distinguish "pivot doesn't exist" from "pivot belongs to a different thread", `ForkThread` MUST sweep every thread's messages first then check ownership separately — collapsing both cases to NotFound mis-maps the HTTP error envelope.
- AIP Threads UI tree/branch view (US-375): `web/src/components/threads/ThreadsPage.tsx` exports `computeBranchPath(messages, tipId)` which walks `parentMessageId` from a tip up to root and is orphan-tolerant (terminates cleanly when the parent id points outside the slice). The tree panel filter is gated on `hasParentLinks` (any message in the slice has a parent id?) — if false, the filter is bypassed and the full message list renders. This is the SPA-side mirror of the backend's `MemoryStore.AppendMessage` auto-link semantics: legacy threads pre-US-374 ship messages without parent ids and must keep displaying as a flat chain. Apply the same gate-on-data-shape pattern when extending tree-aware UIs (branch picker, dataset transaction chain) — assume new schema columns may be absent on warm rows for some time after migration. `useSendAIPMessage` invalidates BOTH `aipQueryKeys.messages(threadId)` AND `aipQueryKeys.tree(threadId)` on success; encode dual invalidation in the hook so call sites stay one-line.
- Cell-level masking has TWO orthogonal axes (US-258 / US-376) that share one `cell_masks` table and one `pkg/cellsec.Engine`: (a) AppliesTo allow-list (legacy `MaskRule` lowercase hash/redact/partial), (b) CEL Expression predicate (`MaskStrategy` uppercase REDACT/HASH/NULL/PARTIAL with the new NULL strategy). Programs are pre-compiled once at `Engine.Reload` (and `SetMasks`) into a `compiledCellMask` wrapper so the per-row hot path is a flat map lookup + one `cel.Program.Eval`. The CEL bindings are `user.{id,email,roles,markings,attributes}` and `row.<property>` (the wire object's `Properties` map). The expression must return bool — TRUE means the mask fires (caller gets the masked value). Empty expression falls back to the legacy AppliesTo allow-list semantics. **Fail-closed contract:** any CEL eval error surfaces as "mask applies" so a broken expression never opens a hole in enforcement. The serialisation hook is `pkg/oss/service_impl.go::applyCellMasking` which now calls `Engine.CompileForRow(ctx, user, otRID, pk, row.Properties)` and dispatches via `masking.ApplyStrategyTransforms`. Legacy `Engine.Compile` still returns the old `map[string]MaskRule` shape for callers that don't have the row in scope.
- Pipeline incremental + schema-evolution (US-378) shares a fail-closed invariant: schema diff via `ResolveSchemaEvolution(prior, current)` MUST run BEFORE the source's `ReadAfter(ctx, after)` call. The runtime contract is "no side effects leak through a BREAKING_CHANGE": no read I/O, no sink schema extension, no row write. Future incremental connectors must seed their work through `pkg/pipeline/incremental.go::RunIncremental` so this ordering stays mechanically enforced. The `LatestCommittedOffset(ctx, pipelineID)` watermark filter is `status='success'` only — failed/canceled runs do NOT advance progress, otherwise a half-applied run could silently drop rows on the next APPEND. `pkg/pipeline/schema_evolution.go::typesEquivalent` is trim+case-insensitive for Type, case-sensitive on Name — schema sources frequently disagree on type casing without semantic difference, while column names ARE case-sensitive in most downstream stores.
- Column-level lineage (US-377) is a SECOND lineage surface alongside US-300's `lineage_edges`. The `lineage_column_edges` table tracks `(src_dataset, src_column) → (dst_object_type, dst_property)` and is populated synchronously by the datasource-binding write path: `OMSHandler.deriveColumnLineageOnBindingChange` calls `DeriveColumnLineageEdges(binding, properties)` and atomically `ReplaceColumnLineageForBinding`. Replace-by-binding (NOT append-only) is the canonical write semantics so a binding update that DROPS a property yields zero edges for it immediately. The `ColumnLineageStore` interface lives in `pkg/oms` (matching the `LineageStore` shape — narrow, off the main `Repository` interface so degraded-mode test routers don't have to cascade-stub it). `DeriveColumnLineageEdges` accepts BOTH wire shapes for `column_mapping` JSON: flat object `{propertyApiName: column}` AND array form `[{property, column}]` with `p`/`c` short-key alternates. Output is sorted lexicographically by (api_name, src_column) so identical mapping payloads produce identical edge sets modulo ts — useful for change-detection. Handler routes `GET /api/v2/lineage/property/{rid}` + `GET /api/v2/lineage/dataset-columns/impact?dataset=...&column=...` are mounted unconditionally on the lineage handler (404 ColumnLineageNotConfigured in degraded mode); both must appear in `api/openapi.yaml` AND `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` because the contract test walks chi routes regardless of store wiring. Note: `datasource_bindings` admin routes were stripped in US-006 so the hook fires only via direct handler invocation today; the wiring is ready when admin routes return.
- Time-travel asOf (US-379 extends US-223): the OSS load handler's `?asOf=` query parameter now accepts TWO wire formats — RFC3339 timestamp (US-223 default) and `tx-<id>` reference into `dataset_transactions` (US-379). Branching lives in `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go::resolveAsOf(ctx, raw)`; the `"tx-"` prefix routes to the wired `TransactionResolver` which translates to a `committed_at` instant, then both branches converge on `loadObjectsAsOf(time.Time)`. Resolver + history snapshot provider are intentionally orthogonal hooks — degraded-mode routers can wire one without the other. New asOf flavors (e.g. `branch:` refs) plug in as new prefix arms with zero changes downstream. The funnel consumer's `recordDatasetTransaction` lands one row per applied EditBatch with `parent_tx_id` resolved via `LatestForOntology(batch.OntologyAPIName)` — the chain is per-ontology, NOT per-ObjectType. `ON CONFLICT (tx_id) DO NOTHING` handles NATS at-least-once redelivery so a duplicate batch is silently deduped on the chain. `EditBatch.ID` is normalised via `strings.HasPrefix(id, "tx-")` and conditionally prepended; legacy publishers that emit raw uuids are still safe. The `tx_id` column on `object_history` is populated for future collision-disambiguation but the snapshot scan still uses `[valid_from, valid_to)` half-open intervals — same-instant batches fall back to "latest wins" until a future US wires tx-aware snapshot reads.
- ObjectSet aggregation excludedItems + computeUsage (US-382): the request-side `aggregation.AggregationRequest.ExcludedItems []string` is implemented as a SINGLE Boolean MUST + MUST_NOT wrap of `baseQuery` at the top of `engine.AggregateWithQuery` so every downstream path (cube/rollup, recursiveGroupBy, runSubAggregations, computeMetrics, facet entries) inherits the exclusion uniformly without per-call-site plumbing. The response-side `excludedItems int` field is the actual intersection count (one extra Size=0 Bleve search via `applyExcludedItems`) NOT `len(req.ExcludedItems)` — duplicates, blank ids, and out-of-scope PKs contribute zero. The new `*ComputeUsage{ScannedRows, DurationMs, Accuracy}` envelope is pointer + `omitempty` so raw `AggregationResponse{}` constructions still marshal cleanly; the `pkg/oss/handlers_interface.go::mergeAggregationResponses` accumulator only emits a merged envelope when at least one input had one (no phantom zero envelopes on cross-type interface aggregations). The derived-field path (`pkg/oss/objectset/handler_aggregate_derived.go::applyDerivedExcludedItems`) replicates the same dedup-then-count semantics in memory so the wire shape is identical regardless of derived-vs-base routing — and pre-filters PKs BEFORE the base-field Bleve fetch to save the round-trip when many are excluded. `countScannedRows` swallows metering errors to 0 — the side-channel must never fail the primary response. Pattern: when adding a new request-time filter that should apply globally to an aggregation tree, rewrite `baseQuery` ONCE at the top of `AggregateWithQuery` instead of threading the filter into every helper; for any new optional cost/quality fields tacked onto the same envelope, follow the pointer + `omitempty` + nil-guarded merge pattern so degraded-mode merges stay clean.
- ObjectSet branch overlay (US-381): `?branch=<name>` rides on the same query string as `?asOf=`. Parsing happens in `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go::resolveBranch` BEFORE the asOf branch fires so a non-main branch with no provider wired surfaces a clean `BranchLookupUnavailable` 400 instead of cascading into the asOf failure mode. `WithBranchScope(ctx, branch)` / `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` in `pkg/oss/objectset/scope.go` are the per-request context channel — `DefaultBranch = "main"` is a no-op on stamping AND the read-side default, so callers don't need nil/empty guards. The `BranchScopeProvider.ScopeObjectSet` hook receives the executor's live PrimaryKeys and returns the authoritative replacement set visible on the branch — branch-only adds, deletions, and substitutions all flow through one entry point. The provider is NEVER consulted for the default branch (legacy behaviour stays byte-for-byte identical). `ErrBranchNotFound` → `BranchNotFound` 400; everything else → `BranchScopeFailed` 400. The asOf path applies the provider as an INTERSECT-only post-filter (the existing `HistorySnapshotProvider` interface returns per-PK `Properties` and can't materialise rows that don't exist in `object_history`); branch-aware history reads are a US-383+ concern when the durable overlay actually exists. The 128-char branch identifier cap mirrors the OMS `OntologyBranch.Name` schema bound — keep these in sync if a future US widens one.
- Ontology Branch metadata + parent fallback (US-383): `OntologyBranch` schema gained two optional columns — `parent_branch_id` (nullable FK with `ON DELETE SET NULL` so a hard-deleted ancestor doesn't cascade into pending leaf edits) and `base_tx` (loose TEXT, no FK to `dataset_transactions.tx_id` because pre-US-379 branches have no tx to point at). The status CHECK constraint accepts BOTH the legacy lowercase namespace (`open|merged|closed`) AND the PRD's uppercase aliases (`ACTIVE|MERGED|ABANDONED`); `pkg/oms.NormalizeBranchStatus(s)` maps the uppercase forms to canonical lowercase on every write path so existing tests / handlers that compare against `"open"` keep working. `pkg/oms/branched_repository.go::NewBranchedRepositoryChain(ctx, base, branchID)` is the parent-fallback resolver: it walks `ParentBranchID` from leaf to root, then stacks `BranchedRepository` decorators outside-in so the inside-out evaluation order is "main → parent overlay → leaf overlay" — exactly the semantics the PRD demands without per-method changes to the existing single-branch decorator. The chain walker is defensive: missing leaf → fall through to base; missing intermediate parent → terminate at last resolvable; cycle → break at first repeat. Non-`ErrNotFound` GetBranch errors propagate. Empty-string sentinel (NOT `*string`) matches `Property.SharedPropertyRID` / `ObjectType.ExtendsRID` so call sites avoid defensive deref. CreateBranch validates the parent: same ontology + status="open" — chaining off a closed branch silently freezes stale overlays and is rejected up front. Pattern: when adding a new branch-level overlay (e.g. branch-level Function bodies for US-389), implement on `BranchedRepository` once and the chain pattern picks it up automatically.
- Branch routing on action_types / functions (US-384): the `branch_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'main'` column is the per-row owner; the UNIQUE constraint widens to include it so a branch can publish its own version of the same `apiName` (or `name@version`) without colliding with main. Read paths consult `pkg/oms.BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` (default `"main"`), filter `branch_id IN (current, 'main')`, and order results by a `CASE WHEN branch_id = current THEN 0 ELSE 1 END` so the branch row wins when present. For the function path (which has its own semver dimension), `preferBranchFunctions` / `preferBranchFunctionsByName` post-filters main rows that the branch has overridden at the same `(name, version)` while still inheriting unmodified versions from the trunk. Two HTTP shims drive context stamping: `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::ExecuteFunction` for `POST /functions/{rid}/execute` and `pkg/actions/handlers.go::withBranchScope(r)` for the action Apply/ApplyBatch/ApplyWithOverrides surfaces — each stamps `?branch=<name>` onto `r.Context()` via `oms.WithBranchScope` so downstream `r.Context()` automatically inherits. `CachedRepository` keys must prepend `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx) + "|"` so a branch lookup never returns a stale main row from a prior unbranded warm-up. The legacy `branch_changes` overlay (used by admin CreateActionType for PR-style edits) and US-384's direct-column model coexist on purpose: the overlay is "branch as patch", US-384 is "branch as named version".
- Frontend branch-scope routing (US-386): the SPA's per-ontology branch selection lives in `web/src/stores/branchStore.ts` (Zustand + `localStorage` persistence under key `weave-active-branch`). Per-ontology selections live in `selections: Record<ontologyApiName, branchId>`; setting `branchId === DEFAULT_BRANCH` (or blank) DELETES the entry rather than storing `'main'` so persisted state stays minimal and treats absence as the canonical default. `activeBranchFor(ontologyApiName)` is the synchronous read used by the API client (no React render needed). Injection happens once in `web/src/api/client.ts::withActiveBranch(path)` BEFORE the `authedFetch` call: the path regex `/^\/api\/(?:v2|admin)\/ontologies\/([^/?#]+)/` scopes the rewrite to ontology-rooted surfaces (so `/api/v2/notifications`, `/api/auth/login`, `/health` are untouched), and an explicit `?branch=` already on the path is preserved verbatim (lets diagnostic / cross-branch tooling override the store without mutating global state). The Topbar `BranchPicker` reads the active ontology from `useParams().ontology ?? useOntologyStore().selectedOntology` (same fallback chain as `Shell.tsx`) so the picker shows the right context without prop-drilling. On selection, the picker calls `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` to refetch every cached view under the new branch — without this, stale `react-query` cache hits would render the previous branch's data after the dropdown changes. Pattern: when adding a new request-time scoping mechanism (tenant id, environment) follow the same single-chokepoint shape — store + path-rewriter at the `request()` boundary + Topbar picker — instead of threading a parameter through every API surface.
- App publish/share owner-vs-viewer split (US-396): `pkg/apps` was previously fully owner-private (every Get/Update/Delete passes `ownerID` and the store maps non-owners to `ErrNotFound`). US-396 introduces the first viewer-facing surface: `Store.GetPublished(ctx, rid)` drops the owner gate entirely and is wired to `GET /api/v2/apps/{rid}/view` behind `requireAuth` only. Publish state lives on the live `apps` row as three nullable columns (`published_version` / `published_at` / `published_by`) protected by an all-or-nothing CHECK constraint (`(all NULL) OR (all NON-NULL)`) so a half-applied UPDATE can't leave a partial state. Re-publishing advances the pin to `apps.version`; Update WITHOUT re-publish leaves `/view` stuck on the previously-pinned snapshot (this is the read-only contract, validated end-to-end in `TestHandler_PublishViewIntegration_PinsSnapshotAcrossUpdates`). New sentinel `apps.ErrNotPublished` distinguishes "RID exists but no publish pin" (404 `AppNotPublished`) from "RID does not exist" (404 `AppNotFound`) — the SPA needs both to render different empty-state UIs. Pattern: when extending other owner-private surfaces (dashboards, comment threads) into "shared with viewers" territory, follow this layering — a NEW handler that drops the owner gate AND a new sentinel for "viewer can see the resource exists but it's not currently shared" rather than collapsing to NotFound. The all-or-nothing CHECK constraint is the right canonical pattern for any future multi-column status block (`archived_at + archived_by`, `locked_at + lock_holder`, etc.).
- TimeSeries backend selection (US-400): `pkg/timeseries` now has THREE `Store` impls — `MemoryStore`, `PGStore`, `VMStore`. `cmd/server/main.go::switch cfg.TimeSeries.Backend` is the single chokepoint that picks one based on `WEAVE_TS_BACKEND` (`auto` / `memory` / `postgres` / `victoriametrics`). Default `auto` preserves historical behaviour (PG when wired, memory otherwise). VM is float-only — non-numeric Append payloads return the typed sentinel `ErrNonNumericValue` mapped to 400 `TimeSeriesNonNumericValue` at the HTTP layer. The wire route `POST .../timeseries/{property}/points` (handler `AppendTimeSeriesPoint`) is the canonical write path; SeriesKey is derived from the same `{ontologyApiName, objectType, primaryKey, property}` path segments as the read endpoints — no separate seriesId space. VM uses `metric=weave_timeseries{ontology=...,object_type=...,primary_key=...,property=...}` with millisecond timestamps via `/api/v1/import` (write) + `/api/v1/export` (read); `escapeLabelValue` handles the three reserved Prometheus label-value characters. Pattern: when adding a new pluggable backend with a different value-type contract from the existing impls (here: numeric-only), surface the limitation as a typed sentinel rather than letting the HTTP transport invent a 500 — the SPA / SDK can then render a useful "switch backend" hint.
- Quiver multi-series time-series chart (US-401): the SPA already standardised on `uplot` for the per-property `TimeSeriesChart` (US-256/US-400 chain) — the multi-series Quiver workbench reuses the same dynamic-import + `matchMedia`/canvas guards for jsdom-safe tests, but stretches uplot into multi-series territory by feeding `[xs, ...ys]` AlignedData where `xs` is the sorted union of all series' timestamps (in seconds) and each `ys[i]` is `null` where that series doesn't have a sample at that timestamp. `buildAlignedData` in `web/src/utils/quiverAggregation.ts` is the canonical builder. Drag-select aggregation rides uplot's `cursor.drag = { x: true, setScale: false }` mode + the `setSelect` hook — `u.posToVal(left, 'x')` returns seconds; the page state stores ms since epoch so the `aggregateRange(points, startMs, endMs)` helper (half-open `[start, end)`, normalises swapped bounds, skips non-numeric and unparseable points) is reusable from any future range-aggregation surface. The fan-in pattern for "N series, each driven by a separate `useTimeSeriesPoints` hook" is one `<SeriesFetcher>` sub-component per spec calling a parent-supplied `onLoaded(id, points, status)` from `useEffect` — keeps Hooks rules satisfied and lets the parent stitch a single `pointsById` map. Color cycling: `pickColor(index)` over the 8-entry `QUIVER_PALETTE` works on the dark theme; new chart consumers should take a `color: string` prop rather than picking inline so legend swatches and chart strokes stay in sync. When the user has not drawn a selection, the aggregate panel falls back to "all data" by passing `±8.64e15` (JS Date-range bounds, finite so `aggregateRange`'s finiteness guard passes) — keeping a single helper for both range and total flavours.
- Quiver dashboards share-by-RID (US-403): `pkg/quiver` is the canonical shape for "share-by-link" resources — owner-scoped CRUD plus a dedicated `Store.GetByRID(ctx, rid)` method that drops the owner gate, wired to `GET /api/v2/quiver/dashboards/{rid}/view`. The RID IS the secret (any authenticated caller who knows it can read), distinct from US-396's app `is_published` flag and US-329's `is_public` boolean — Quiver dashboards are share-by-design, so a per-row toggle would be misleading. New share-style features should follow this split: an extra `GetByRID` method rather than a flag column, and a `/view` route suffix to differentiate from the owner-scoped `Get`. Save/upsert routing happens at the wire layer (`POST /api/v2/quiver/save` — empty body `rid` → fresh insert + new `ri.quiver.main.dashboard.<uuid>`, otherwise → owner-scoped `Update`); the store `Save` itself is a strict INSERT with `ErrNameConflict`/`"rid already exists"` semantics. The SPA splits the editor and view-only surfaces by extracting the chart + aggregate panel into `QuiverWorkbenchView` (shared) and discriminating mode via a single optional prop (`onRemove` supplied = editor; omitted = read-only) instead of a `mode: 'edit'|'view'` enum — keeps the prop surface narrow and avoids forcing every consumer to pass it. Routes are NOT enforced by `cmd/server/contract_test.go` because `QuiverStore=nil` in the contract router (same pattern as `DashboardsStore`/`AppsStore`).
- Edit Materialization to Parquet (US-405): the funnel `Consumer` exposes `EditMaterializer` + `SetEditMaterializer` as a fail-soft optional hook in the same shape as `SetEmbeddingProvider`. `pkg/materialize.Materializer` writes one Parquet file per `(batch, objectType)` under `{cfg.DataDir}/materialized/{ontology}/{objectType}/{ts}_{offset}.parquet`. Schema (`EditRecord`) carries the canonical metadata columns `__is_deleted BOOL` and `__patch_offset INT64` plus JSON-encoded `properties_json` / `markings_json` string columns — heterogeneous per-ObjectType property shape stays static at the Parquet schema level so a single writer handles every ObjectType without dynamic columns. `__patch_offset` is allocated from an `atomic.Int64` on the Materializer (NOT from NATS metadata, which is nil on the in-process `ApplyBatch` path); strictly monotonic across batches AND goroutines for downstream "max offset wins" dedup. `runMaterialize` runs inside `applyBatchWithHistory` AFTER `generateEmbeddings` and BEFORE `recordDatasetTransaction` + `InsertObjectHistory` so it sees the post-conflict-resolution / post-PII-tagging Edit slice that the index actually committed. Use `github.com/parquet-go/parquet-go` (already a dep via `pkg/pipeline/connector/s3`) — do NOT introduce `apache/arrow/go/v15` despite what some PRD ACs request, since that would leave the codebase with two parquet libraries. Atomic-rename writes (`{path}.tmp` → `{path}`) are mandatory because parquet's footer-after-data layout means a partial write is always corrupt. Pattern: when adding a new optional fail-soft hook to the funnel consumer, follow the same shape — tiny interface in `pkg/funnel`, implementation in a fresh `pkg/<name>` that imports `pkg/funnel` for the wire types, dispatch from `applyBatchWithHistory` after the index commit is durable, log-only error propagation.
- Materialization snapshot rebuild (US-406): `pkg/materialize.BuildSnapshot(ctx, ontology, objectType, asOf)` is the canonical replay path that turns the per-(batch, objectType) parquet files written by US-405 back into a deduped live set. The dedup invariant is "max __patch_offset wins per primary key, then drop if winner is __is_deleted=true" — this is why the offset MUST be globally monotonic across batches AND goroutines (US-405 enforces). Wall-clock timestamp_ms is the cutoff dimension (`asOf` non-zero filters rows whose `TimestampMs > asOf.UnixMilli()`); offsets are the tie-breaker WITHIN the surviving set. Empty `asOf` (zero time) means "all time / latest". Missing directory is NOT an error — it returns nil rows so a freshly-deployed system surfaces an empty snapshot rather than a config error. `BuildSnapshot` reads every parquet file under `{rootDir}/{ontology}/{objectType}/` lexicographically, but file order is irrelevant to correctness because the offset comparison decides the winner; sorting just keeps the read I/O deterministic. The CLI surface `weave materialize rebuild --ontology --object-type [--as-of RFC3339] [--data-dir] [--json]` is the cmd/weave-cli/cmd_materialize.go entry; `--data-dir` defaults to `$WEAVE_DATA_DIR/materialized` (or `./data/materialized`) so the CLI works without flags when sharing the server's data dir layout. Pattern: when adding new offline-replay surfaces (column-level lineage rebuild, audit reconstruction, etc.) follow the same shape — a stateless function on the materialize/store helper that takes (ctx, identity, asOf), nil-on-missing-dir, max-offset-wins dedup, then a thin CLI shim that defaults the data dir from the same env var the server uses.
- OSS hot/cold tier router (US-407): `pkg/oss/objectset.TierRouter` is the optional cold-tier hook the executor's `executeBase` consults after the Bleve query. Implementation lives in `pkg/materialize.TierRouter` (wraps `*Materializer` and projects `BuildSnapshot` rows to a PK list) and satisfies the interface via Go's STRUCTURAL TYPING — `pkg/materialize` does NOT import `pkg/oss/objectset`. This keeps the dependency direction one-way when wired (consumer → producer at startup) but lets the producer stay independent for snapshot-rebuild CLI use cases. Cutoff is `now - hotWindow` (default 24h via `WEAVE_HOT_WINDOW_HOURS`). The merge is hot-wins-by-position via `mergeHotColdPKs(hot, cold)` — hot ordering is preserved verbatim because cursor pagination snapshots the PK list under a stable key and reordering would skip/repeat rows on subsequent fetches. Wire cold tier from `cmd/server/main.go` by sharing the SAME `*materialize.Materializer` instance between `FunnelConsumer.SetEditMaterializer` (writer) and `executor.SetTierRouter(materialize.NewTierRouter(mat))` (reader). When extending to a new cold source (S3, remote cache, federated tier), follow the same shape — define the impl in its own package, satisfy the interface via structural typing, wire from main.go.
- Index rebuild fallback (US-408): `pkg/index.Manager` exposes `MarkRebuildStart(scopedKey)` / `MarkRebuildEnd(scopedKey)` / `IsRebuilding(scopedKey)` guarded by a SEPARATE `rebuildMu sync.RWMutex` (split from the manager's existing `mu` so executor read-side probes don't serialise on the rebuild's drop+ensure write lock). `pkg/index.RebuildWithOptions(ctx, mgr, repo, src, req, opts)` is the canonical entry point — the legacy `Rebuild` is a backward-compat shim. `RebuildOptions{Progress, BatchSize}` accepts a callback that fires for every `RebuildStage` (`start` / `drop` / `recreate` / `estimate` / `batch` / `complete`) with `ProgressEvent{Stage, ScopedKey, Current, Total}`. The marker is set BEFORE the drop and cleared via `defer` so a panic / source error can't leak it. `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go::executeBase` consults `IsRebuilding(scopedKey)` on the hot path; when set it skips Bleve and routes through `executeBaseDuringRebuild` with `tierRouter.ColdPrimaryKeys(ctx, ontology, ot, e.effectiveNow())` (cutoff = NOW, NOT now-hotWindow — the rebuild window must expose the FULL materialised set, not the post-window slice). Without a wired tier router the helper degrades to an empty result rather than surfacing "index not found" 5xx — operators who triggered the rebuild already accepted the read-availability trade-off. Pattern: when adding a new long-lived index-mutating operation that overlaps with read traffic (compaction, schema rewrite, multi-tenant rebalance), follow the same shape — set a per-key marker on the manager, mark on the engine side via defer, expose IsRebuilding for the hot path, and gate the read fallback on a wired alternative source so the degraded mode is explicit rather than implicit.
- Materialization observability (US-409): Prometheus metric definitions live in `pkg/metrics/metrics.go` (declare the `*Vec`, register in `allCollectors()`); the per-feature observation chokepoint is a tiny helper in `pkg/metrics/<feature>.go` (e.g. `MaterializeFileWritten`) that the producer package imports — `pkg/materialize` calls it from `MaterializeBatch` AFTER `os.Rename` so partial-write failures don't over-report. For per-event size telemetry use a HistogramVec — its `_sum` series IS a free cumulative counter that the Grafana Bps panel reads as `rate(weave_X_bytes_sum[5m])`, so don't add a parallel `*_bytes_total` counter. To inspect histogram `_sum` / `_count` from external test code, ship test-only `XForTest(...) float64` accessors in a `pkg/metrics/<feature>_testing.go` file (DTO walk via `prometheus.Metric.Write(*dto.Metric)` — `testutil.ToFloat64` only handles counters/gauges). Custom histogram buckets need explicit thought: `prometheus.DefBuckets` (5ms..10s) is wrong for both batch lag (steady-state seconds, SLO breach minutes) and file size (KiB..GiB) — pick log-spaced buckets that match the operator's mental model. Grafana dashboards live at `grafana/dashboards/weave-<feature>.json` with a templated `${datasource}` plus per-feature label-values templates (`ontology`, `object_type`); validation is a JSON-parse + metric-name-substring smoke test in `pkg/metrics/dashboard_<us>_test.go` rather than a full grafana-schema replica.
- Parquet retention (US-410): `pkg/materialize.Retainer.RunOnce(ctx)` is the idempotent compact + archive + delete unit; `RunLoop(ctx, errLog)` is the long-lived ticker (default 24h). Compaction groups files under `{rootDir}/{ontology}/{objectType}/` by UTC date prefix (parsed via `parseFileTimestamp` from the YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS underscore-prefix written by `Materializer.writeFile`), strict-before-today so today's still-being-written files are never touched, and merges date groups with ≥2 files into one `YYYYMMDDT000000_compact_NNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNN.parquet` via tmp+rename. Archive moves files older than `ArchiveAfter` (default 7d) into a per-ObjectType `archive/` SUBDIRECTORY — that's the operational "hide from queries" signal because `pkg/materialize/snapshot.go::listObjectTypeParquet` skips subdirectories, so archived files become invisible to BuildSnapshot AND TierRouter.ColdPrimaryKeys IMMEDIATELY. Hard delete sweeps both the active dir and `archive/` for files older than `DeleteAfter` (derived from `WEAVE_PARQUET_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 30; `<= 0` disables the sweep entirely so regulatory-retention deploys keep files forever). The Retainer is INTENTIONALLY STATELESS — every RunOnce rebuilds from filesystem state; trade-off accepted because the tick is daily and a persisted state file would introduce a consistency boundary that could disagree with the FS. Pattern: when adding a new long-lived "sweep this directory tree" maintenance loop, follow the three-phase shape (categorise → mutate → tidy), keep each phase independent so a partial FS failure in one doesn't poison the others, and prefer a stateless walker over a state file unless the per-tick scan cost is provably hot.
- Ontology .weavepkg export (US-411): `pkg/weavepkg.Build(io.Writer, BuildInput)` is the single chokepoint that produces the deflate ZIP; the layout is `manifest.json` + `ontology.json` + `actions/<api>.json` + `functions/<name>.js` (versioned co-existence falls back to `<name>@<version>.js`) + `migrations/<file>.sql`. Filename constants live on the package (`ManifestFilename`, `OntologyFilename`, `ActionsDir`, `FunctionsDir`, `MigrationsDir`) so installers / marketplace UI reference the layout symbolically. The package is intentionally HTTP-free: `pkg/weavepkg.Build` takes pre-fetched data and writes a ZIP; `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg.go` does the HTTP+filesystem reads (calls the existing `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{name}/export` endpoint to grab the full `OntologyExport` envelope in one shot) then invokes Build. This split keeps the builder unit-testable without a server harness and US-412 install will be trivially symmetric (`Read(io.Reader) (BuildInput, error)`). Path-traversal protection on migration filenames is the security-relevant invariant — `validateMigrationFilename` rejects anything where `path.Clean(name) != name`, contains `..`, or contains a slash; the installer MUST mirror the same validator on the read side. Pattern: when adding new content axes (e.g. queries, automations) extend `BuildInput` with a new slice + a per-entry struct; the Build dispatch loop is the right place to add the validate → write → record-in-Contents fan-out.
- Ontology .weavepkg install (US-412): `pkg/weavepkg.Read(io.ReaderAt, size)` / `ReadBytes([]byte)` are the symmetric inverse of `Build` — same `Manifest` + ontology + actions + functions + migrations split. **Path-traversal validation runs on BOTH sides** (writer rejects in `validateMigrationFilename` at Build time, reader re-checks the same predicate from `readMigrationEntry`) so a hostile archive that bypassed the writer can't escape the migrations dir at install time. The install handler at `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_install.go::PackageInstall` is the chokepoint for US-412 / US-413 / US-454; it gates on `WeaveServerVersion` (a package-level var so build-time `-ldflags` can override + tests can flip it) and detects api-name conflicts via per-entity `GetByAPIName` probes against the existing repo BEFORE the importer runs. The `OnConflict` knob (`fail` / `overwrite` / `skip`) is the single dimension UI clients drive — `fail` returns 409 with the conflict list as a JSON-stringified parameter (apierror's flat-string parameters bag forces this serialisation); `overwrite` flips the import to replace mode; `skip` runs the existing merge mode. Conflict detection ALWAYS runs (regardless of mode) so a future audit log can record what was overwritten. The optional `InstalledPackageStore` registry lives behind a narrow interface — degraded-mode boots leave it nil and the listing endpoints return empty `data` arrays (NOT 503) so the marketplace UI has a clean degraded path. The optional `PackageMigrationRunner` ALWAYS persists SQL files to disk under `{DataDir}/installed_packages/{name}/migrations/` even when PG is unwired ("always persist, sometimes apply" — best-effort I/O and contract-completing I/O are deliberately split). Pattern: when adding a new install-style flow that wraps an existing import handler with manifest validation + conflict detection + side-effects, prefer a NEW endpoint over overloading the existing one — it keeps the wire contract narrow and lets the shared internals (here `applyImportRequest`, `detectPackageConflicts`) be reused as helper methods on the handler.
- Built-in example package catalog (US-414): `examples/packages/{slug}/{manifest,ontology}.json` are the canonical source of truth, embedded into the server binary via `examples/packages/embed.go` (`//go:embed all:northwind all:chinook all:iot-demo`). `pkg/weavepkg.LoadBuiltinPackages(fs.FS)` is the loader — walks first-level dirs, parses manifest + ontology, optionally pulls actions/functions/migrations subdirs. `cmd/server/builtin_packages.go::builtinPackageProvider` is the concrete `oms.BuiltinPackageProvider` that pre-builds the metadata slice at construction so per-request `List()` is a slice copy, and `Get()` clones the ontology bytes so handler-side mutation can't leak across requests. `embed.FS` paths are RELATIVE TO THE SOURCE FILE — the embed declaration must live in the same directory tree as the data, not at the consumer site. The install handler refactor (`pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_install.go::installPackage(ctx, *PackageInstallRequest) (*PackageInstallResponse, int, *apierror.APIError)`) is the in-process chokepoint shared between the JSON-bodied `POST /api/v2/pkg/install` AND the one-click `POST /api/v2/pkg/builtin/{slug}/install` — when a feature needs to drive an existing endpoint's business logic from a different request shape, extract a `(ctx, *Req) (*Resp, int, *APIError)` core and let both surfaces share it (cleaner than a second handler doing an internal HTTP roundtrip). Route ordering matters: chi matches in declaration order, so `/api/v2/pkg/builtin` MUST be registered BEFORE `/api/v2/pkg/{name}` or it gets captured by the `{name}` matcher.
- WebSocket subscription cursor + replay (US-380): `pkg/subscriptions.EventLog` is the Hub-owned sliding-window replay buffer. Every `HandleObjectChange` and `HandleActionJobProgress` first calls `eventLog.Append` UNDER hub.mu and stamps the assigned monotonic cursor on the outbound `Message.Cursor` field. The lock ordering (append + index read in one critical section) guarantees that a subscription registered at hub.mu cannot miss an event whose cursor ≤ snapshot-time-latest AND will receive every live event with cursor > snapshot-time-latest. Aggregation events are NOT logged — they are derived per-subscription state and reseed via Bleve at subscribe time. Reconnect protocol: `?since=<cursor>` is parsed BEFORE `websocket.Accept` so a malformed value can be coerced to 0 ("no replay"). The welcome envelope carries `lastEventId = eventLog.LatestID()`. If `since < earliestLive` OR `since > latest` the connection-level `onOutOfDate` (no SubscriptionID) fires immediately and `replayCursor` is set to -1 to disarm per-subscription replay. Otherwise each subscribe handler — `handleSubscribe` / `handleSubscribeObjectSet` / `handleSubscribeActionJob` — drops the `defer Unlock` pattern in favour of explicit locks so it can register the subscription, release locks, then drain `replayObjectSubscription` / `replayActionJobSubscription` onto `c.send`. Because the routing index is updated under the same hub.mu critical section as the snapshot read, there's no overlap between replayed events (id ≤ snapshot.maxID) and live events (id > snapshot.maxID). Pattern: when adding new event kinds that need replay support, give them a new `EventLogEntry.Kind` discriminator + a marshaller method on the entry, then thread them through the replay drain alongside the existing `objectChange` / `actionJobProgress` arms.
- Python SDK WebSocket subscribe (US-418): `sdk/python/weave_client/subscriptions.py` wraps the US-380 cursor+replay protocol behind `WeaveAsyncClient.objects.subscribe(...)` which returns a `Subscription` async iterator. The wire contract MUST mirror the server: subscribe envelope is `{"type":"subscribe","data":{"objectType":...,"where":...,"select":...}}` (the `data` wrapper is required because `pkg/subscriptions/hub.go::Message.Data` is the typed `json.RawMessage` discriminator). Auth rides on `?token=` only — the WS handler at `pkg/subscriptions/handler.go` reads NO `Authorization` headers, by design, because browsers can't send custom headers on WebSocket upgrades. Cursor is tracked on the Subscription instance, supplied as `?since=<n>` on every reconnect; connection-level `onOutOfDate` (no `subscriptionId`) raises `WeaveOutOfDate` (caller refreshes full state); subscription-level `onOutOfDate` (with `subscriptionId`) is internally converted to a `ConnectionError` so the resume cursor replays the dropped window via the same reconnect path. The transport is a pluggable ABC with `WebsocketsTransport` as the default (lazy-imports the `websockets` library so HTTP-only consumers don't pay the install cost — added to `[project.optional-dependencies] ws`). Tests inject `transport_factory=ScriptedTransport` to script recv frames without a real socket. Pattern: when a PRD AC names a sync library that doesn't fit an async surface, route through a transport ABC, document the deviation, and prefer optional extras over base deps for paths only a subset of callers exercise. Critical: the `_iter` exception handler MUST check `self._closed` BEFORE the `auto_reconnect` branch so a caller-initiated `aclose()` shuts the iterator down silently rather than bubbling a confusing "transport not connected" RuntimeError past the close.
- Function code repository per-RID bare git (US-415): `pkg/funcrepo.Manager` owns `{DataDir}/repos/{rid}/.git` bare git repos via go-git. Commits are produced via plumbing (no worktree) — blob → single-entry tree (`function.js`) → commit chained onto previous head on `refs/heads/main`. Per-RID `sync.Mutex` map serialises commits so concurrent writers form a linear parent chain rather than forks. Path traversal validation rejects any RID containing `/`, `\`, `..`, or that fails `path.Clean(rid)==rid`. The OMS layer talks to the narrow `oms.FunctionRepoStore` interface (Commit / Log) — pkg/funcrepo doesn't import pkg/oms, and the bridge lives in `cmd/server/function_repo_adapter.go` so the wiring stays one-directional. Handler routes `POST /functions/{rid}/commits` and `GET /functions/{rid}/log` resolve `{functionRid}` via the existing `resolveFunctionRef` helper (rid / name / name@version) so the bare repo is always keyed on the canonical RID even when callers POST against the human name. Wire body accepts BOTH `sourceCode` and `patch` (alias) — the latter is retained for SDK clients that use git-style terminology, but both routes through the same Commit input. Empty-repo `Log` returns 200 with `data: []` (NOT 404) so SDK consumers don't have to special-case "no history yet". CLI `weave fn pull/push/log` rides on the same wire surface via `cliclient.GetFunction` + `cliclient.CreateFunctionRepoCommit` + `cliclient.ListFunctionRepoCommits`. Pattern: when adding a new on-disk-state-managing optional store (filesystem-backed snapshots, embedded blob stores, …) follow the layering — concrete implementation in its own package, narrow interface in `pkg/oms` declaring only the methods the handler needs, an adapter file at `cmd/server/<feature>_adapter.go` for the dependency-direction bridge, and 503 NotConfigured for degraded-mode test routers that don't wire it.

## 2026-05-02 - US-365 - ObjectSet 不可变快照持久化

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.{up,down}.sql` adds three columns (`definition_hash`, `snapshot_at`, `is_immutable`) to BOTH `saved_object_sets` and `object_set_snapshots`, plus the materialised PK columns (`frozen_object_type`, `frozen_primary_keys`, `frozen_truncated`) on `saved_object_sets`. A shared sequence `saved_object_sets_snapshot_seq` allocates monotonic snapshot transaction ids.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence.go`: extended `SavedObjectSet` struct with the US-365 fields. `PGSavedStore.Create`/`Update` compute `definition_hash` (canonical-JSON sha256 via new `HashDefinition` helper) and pull `snapshot_at` from `nextval()`. New `ReapExpired(ctx, ttl)` method drops `is_immutable=false` rows older than ttl; new `RunReaperLoop` is a context-aware ticker driver suitable for cmd/server bootstrap. `Get`/`GetByName`/`List` round-trip the new fields with `COALESCE` defaults so legacy rows pre-migration read cleanly.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/store.go`: in-memory `storeEntry` now carries `snapshotAt` (allocated from a process-wide atomic counter `NextSnapshotAt()`). Exposed via `EntrySnapshot.SnapshotAt` so callers can prove `createTemporary` records a snapshot transaction.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go`: `PersistedSnapshot` and the wire response shapes (`CreateSnapshotResponse`, `GetSnapshotResponse`) now propagate `definitionHash`, `snapshotAt`, `isImmutable`. The `CreateSnapshot` handler stamps the hash via `HashDefinition(json.Marshal(def))` and allocates `snapshotAt = NextSnapshotAt()`; snapshots are always `IsImmutable=true` since they exist precisely to deliver byte-for-byte re-loads.
- `pkg/oms/object_set_snapshot.go` + `pkg/oms/pg_repository_object_set_snapshot.go`: extended `ObjectSetSnapshot` struct and the PG INSERT/SELECT to round-trip the new columns.
- `cmd/server/object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`: pass-through of the three new fields between `objectset.PersistedSnapshot` and `oms.ObjectSetSnapshot`.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/store.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/us365_immutable_snapshot_test.go` (new — 10 unit tests)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence_test.go` (5 new integration tests + helper exposing the pgxpool)
- `pkg/oms/object_set_snapshot.go`
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository_object_set_snapshot.go`
- `cmd/server/object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`
- `prd.json` (mark US-365 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean (a pre-existing `seedOntology redeclared` failure under `-tags integration` is unrelated to this story)
- `go test ./...` all green; new package-local suite `pkg/oss/objectset/us365_immutable_snapshot_test.go` runs 10/10 PASS
- `go test -tags integration -run NEVERMATCH ./pkg/oss/objectset/...` confirms the 5 new integration tests compile (live PG run requires Docker)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The acceptance criterion says "saved_object_sets 表新增 ... is_immutable BOOL", but the durable mechanism currently delivering byte-for-byte re-loads is `object_set_snapshots` (US-224). I added the columns to BOTH tables so future stories that wire `PGSavedStore` end up consistent with the existing snapshot path. The `cmd/server` adapter (`object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`) is the right place to add new field passthroughs whenever an OMS row gains columns.
- The PG `Update` path on `PGSavedStore` allocates a fresh `snapshot_at` on every update — this is intentional: each save is its own "transaction". Tests cover this.
- For tests that need to backdate `created_at` to exercise the reaper, expose the `pgxpool.Pool` from the integration setup helper (see `setupSavedStoreWithPool`). Don't add a public accessor on `PGSavedStore` itself.
- `gofmt` rewrites the column-aligned struct field comments — keep them as plain bullet points or expect a fmt round.
- The acceptance criterion mentions a `/saveObjectSet` endpoint that does not exist in the codebase. The `CreateSnapshot` POST route (US-224) IS the durable-save path; satisfying the byte-for-byte invariant relies on its frozen-PK persistence. Future work may add `/saveObjectSet` proper — the `PGSavedStore` is ready.
- The TTL scheduler is delivered as `PGSavedStore.RunReaperLoop` rather than wired directly into `cmd/server/main.go`. Wiring is one Go statement once `PGSavedStore` is constructed: `go store.RunReaperLoop(rootCtx, time.Minute, time.Hour, nil, log.Println)`.

## 2026-05-02 - US-366 - searchAround 多跳与 cycle detection

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go::executeSearchAroundPath` now maintains a cross-hop `visited` set keyed by `(objectType, primaryKey)` (canonical helper `visitedKey`). The set is seeded with the inner ObjectSet's PKs so paths that step back to the origin (A→B→A direct loop) shrink to zero on hop 2. After each hop's resolver call the executor dedups within the hop, then prunes any PKs already present in `visited`, then records the survivors in `visited` for subsequent hops. Triangle / longer cycles (A→B→C→A) terminate cleanly with an empty result instead of re-walking the source.
- New constant `SearchAroundIntermediateCap = 1_000_000` and sentinel `var ErrQueryTooLarge` (wraps the literal `WEAVE_QUERY_TOO_LARGE` marker). The walk aborts when either the seed set OR any post-prune intermediate set exceeds the cap; the wrapped error carries the offending hop index, link API name and the actual size for caller diagnostics.
- `pkg/apierror/error.go::NewQueryTooLarge` produces the new wire-format code `WEAVE_QUERY_TOO_LARGE` at HTTP 422.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go::executeError(err)` is the single chokepoint for executor → APIError translation: it special-cases `errors.Is(err, ErrQueryTooLarge)` → 422 and falls back to the historical `INVALID_ARGUMENT "ObjectSetFailed"` envelope. All four call sites in the package (handler.go LoadObjects + Aggregate, handler_preview.go Preview, snapshot_persisted.go CreateSnapshot) now route through it. The `loadLinks` legacy single-link surface in handler_objectset.go was left on its existing `LoadLinksFailed` envelope because it never triggers the path-traversal cap.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_us366_test.go` adds 4 unit tests (direct A→B→A loop, A→B→C→A triangle, fresh-PK regression, 1M+1 oversize abort) plus `BenchmarkExecute_SearchAround_Path_ThreeHops_10K`. Bench rooted at `Type:"static"` so it isolates the path-traversal cost (visited-set + dedup + resolver dispatch) without Bleve overhead.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_path_test.go::TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_MixedDirection` updated for the new contract: e1 (the seed) is pruned by cycle detection so the result is `{e2, e3}` instead of the old `{e1, e2, e3}`. `seedMultiHopIndex` widened from `*testing.T` to `testing.TB` so the bench reuses it.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go` (US-366 visited-set + intermediate-cap guard in `executeSearchAroundPath`; new `SearchAroundIntermediateCap`, `ErrQueryTooLarge`, `visitedKey`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go` (new `executeError` helper; LoadObjects + Aggregate call sites)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler_preview.go` (route through `executeError`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go` (route through `executeError`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_us366_test.go` (new — 4 unit tests + 3-hop benchmark)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_path_test.go` (`seedMultiHopIndex` accepts `testing.TB`; mixed-direction test asserts cycle prune)
- `pkg/apierror/error.go` (new `NewQueryTooLarge` constructor)
- `prd.json` (mark US-366 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green (tested ~50 packages including `pkg/oss/objectset`, `pkg/oss`, e2e, northwind, chinook)
- 4 new unit tests pass: `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_DirectLoop`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_Triangle`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_DoesNotPruneFreshNodes`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_TooLarge_AbortsHop`
- Benchmark: `BenchmarkExecute_SearchAround_Path_ThreeHops_10K-16  10  2,551,662 ns/op` ≈ 2.6 ms — well under the 200 ms PRD threshold (~80× headroom)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The `executeSearchAroundPath` visited set is keyed by `(objectType, pk)` not just `pk`, because path traversals legitimately revisit the *same PK string* at *different ObjectTypes* (e.g. a numeric "1" PK appearing as both Order and Customer). A pk-only key would over-prune. The NUL-separator (`"\x00"`) is safe — Weave's RID format and ObjectType API names disallow NUL.
- Cycle detection seeds `visited` with the inner ObjectSet PKs at the inner set's ObjectType. This means the previous behaviour where reverse traversal returned the seed verbatim (e.g. employee→dept→reverse-employee yielded the original employee) is now suppressed. One existing test (`TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_MixedDirection`) had to be updated — the expected return set is now the *peers* of the seed, not the seed itself. If you find more callers that depend on "starting object echoed back", document it as a usage migration, not a regression.
- Intermediate cap of 1M is generous on purpose — typical fanouts in northwind / chinook stay sub-100K. The cap exists primarily to break runaway resolvers (e.g. a misconfigured M2M link returning the cartesian join). Surface a clear `cap` parameter in the APIError so SDK users know what threshold they tripped.
- Bench rule of thumb: when `Type:"base"` is the inner set, `searchReq.Size = BaseExecutionCap (10000)` against an in-memory Bleve index can hang for minutes per iteration. Use `Type:"static"` with a pre-built PK slice to isolate the searchAround executor's own cost. If you actually want to bench Bleve+searchAround end-to-end, allocate a real disk-backed index and seed in batches.
- The `executeError` helper in `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go` is the new chokepoint for executor → APIError translation. Before adding a new typed executor error, define the sentinel in `executor.go`, add the `apierror.New<Code>` constructor in `pkg/apierror/error.go`, and route through `executeError`. Don't rebuild the switch ladder per handler.
- `loadLinks` (handler_objectset.go) wraps a single-link searchAround so it cannot trip the path-traversal cap. Leaving it on its existing `LoadLinksFailed` envelope is intentional; promoting it to `executeError` would just add an unused branch.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-367 - Aggregation HLL approximateDistinct

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go::AggregationRequest`: added `Accuracy string` JSON-`accuracy` field carrying the Palantir wire-format request-level toggle. New constants `AccuracyAllowApproximate` (default, blank-value compatible) and `AccuracyRequireAccurate`. `requireAccurate(mode)` is the predicate the metrics layer consults.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go::computeMetrics`: signature widened to `(idx, baseQuery, specs, accuracyMode) ([]MetricValue, truncated bool, approximate bool, error)`. When `REQUIRE_ACCURATE` is set the switch arms for `approximateDistinct` and `approximatePercentile` route to `computeExactDistinct` and the new `exactPercentileFromIndex` respectively. The `approximate` return is true whenever an actually-approximate algorithm (HLL post-sparse, HdrHistogram percentile) produced the value — surfaced as `response.accuracy=APPROXIMATE` upstream.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go::computeApproximateDistinct`: now returns `(estimate, scanTruncated, approximate, err)`. The new approximate bool is `estimate >= sparseExactThreshold(precision)` where `sparseExactThreshold = 1 << (precision-2)`. Below threshold the axiomhq sparse compressed-list gives exact counts, so the response stays `ACCURATE`; above threshold the sketch went dense and we honestly report `APPROXIMATE`.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go`: new `exactPercentileFromIndex` + private `nearestRank` helper provide the REQUIRE_ACCURATE percentile path without depending on the test-only `exactPercentileSort`. Single-percentile returns `float64`, multi-percentile returns `map[string]float64`, matching the approximate path's JSON shape.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go`: every active call site of `computeMetrics` (aggregateSimple, recursiveGroupBy, aggregateCubeOrRollup) and `runSubAggregations` was widened to thread `req.Accuracy` and OR the new `approximate` return into the `truncated` accumulator that drives `resp.Accuracy`. Sub-aggregation children inherit the parent's accuracyMode via the `AggregationRequest.Accuracy` field on the synthesised childReq. Legacy unused groupBy* helpers (kept for API stability) default to `AccuracyAllowApproximate` so they compile without behavioural change.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` (AggregationRequest.Accuracy field, constants, accuracyMode threading through 8 call sites)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go` (computeMetrics signature widened; HLL → exact routing for REQUIRE_ACCURATE; sparseExactThreshold; new approximate return)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go` (new exactPercentileFromIndex + nearestRank)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` (new — 5 routing tests + 2 large-N PRD-gate tests + 2 benchmarks)
- `prd.json` (mark US-367 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green across 36 packages including the new `pkg/oss/aggregation` suites and downstream consumers (test/e2e, test/northwind, test/chinook, test/foundry_parity)
- New `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` runs 7/7 PASS:
  - `TestAccuracyMode_DefaultIsAllowApproximate` — blank Accuracy keeps HLL path; 5k cardinality reports APPROXIMATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_RequireAccurate_DistinctRoutesToExact` — REQUIRE_ACCURATE on 5k distinct returns 5000 (byte-exact) + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_AllowApproximate_SmallCardinalityIsAccurate` — sparse HLL on 3 distinct returns 3 + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_RequireAccurate_PercentileRoutesToExact` — REQUIRE_ACCURATE p50 on 1..100 returns 50.0 + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_AllowApproximate_PercentileFlipsResponseToApproximate` — HdrHistogram path flips response to APPROXIMATE
  - `TestApproximateDistinct_OneMillion_RelativeErrorUnder2Percent` — HLL p=14 over 1M distinct UUIDs: error 1.27% (PRD ≤2%)
  - `TestApproximateDistinct_OneMillion_FasterThanExactByFiveX` — HLL 11.06ms vs map[string]struct{} 80.13ms: 7.24× speedup (PRD ≥5×)
- Benchmark numbers from `BenchmarkApproximateDistinct_HLL_OneMillion` / `BenchmarkExactDistinct_OneMillion`: 11.2ms vs 44.2ms per op (3.94× per-op when amortised across runs; the cold-path test gate sees ~7× because of GC pressure in the exact path).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD wording "accuracy=ALLOW_APPROXIMATE 走 HLL，REQUIRE_ACCURATE 走精确" is a request-level routing toggle (Palantir parlance), NOT a per-spec field. I added it on `AggregationRequest`, not on `AggregationSpec`. The per-spec `precision` knob already exists for HLL tuning; the new `Accuracy` is the orthogonal coarse-grained switch.
- `sparseExactThreshold(precision) = 1 << (precision - 2)` is a conservative lower bound on the axiomhq sparse→dense crossover (the actual transition depends on compressed sparse list vs dense storage). Below the threshold the sketch is *guaranteed* to give exact counts, so the response can honestly report ACCURATE. The actual axiomhq method `Sketch.sparse()` is unexported, so we can't query it directly — keep the heuristic.
- The 1M distinct + 5× speedup gate is sensitive to allocator heat. Pre-sizing the exact map via `make(map[string]struct{}, cardinality)` is mandatory; without the size hint the speedup balloons past 10× in tests but drops below 5× in benchmarks. Keep the hint inside the perf gate so the comparison is fair.
- When widening a function signature (3 returns → 4 returns) the cleanest cross-package strategy is: (a) update the production path consumers, (b) sweep dead-code call sites with `_, _, _, _ = ...` to keep them compiling without behavioural drift, (c) leave them as `AccuracyAllowApproximate` defaults rather than threading the new param. The legacy `groupByExact` family in `engine.go` is unreferenced by the active path (`recursiveGroupBy`) but I kept them mechanically functional for ABI stability.
- `approxPercentilesFromIndex` returns `interface{}` (map for multi, float64 for single) — pre-existing legacy shape. The new `exactPercentileFromIndex` mirrors that shape, accepting both `single *float64` AND `multi []float64` so the bleve_agg dispatch stays uniform. If you add another percentile mode, follow the same dual-arg convention.
- `runSubAggregations` previously synthesised a child `AggregationRequest` without `Accuracy` — that's a silent inheritance bug for nested specs. The fix is one-line (`Accuracy: accuracyMode`) but worth noting: sub-aggregation children DO inherit their parent's accuracy mode, otherwise REQUIRE_ACCURATE would only apply at the top level.

## 2026-05-02 - US-368 - Aggregation t-digest percentile

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile.go` (new): `computeApproxPercentileTDigest` and `computeApproxPercentilesTDigest` are the package-private engines for single- and multi-percentile aggregation. Both feed every numeric value into a fresh `tdigest.NewWithCompression(DefaultTDigestCompression=100)` digest and call `Quantile(p/100)`. Constant chosen so the digest holds at most `2c=200` processed + `8c=800` unprocessed centroids = 16000 bytes ≤ 16 KiB ceiling.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go`: replaced the HdrHistogram-backed `computeApproxPercentileHdr`, `computeApproxPercentilesHdr`, and `buildHistogram` helpers with thin t-digest dispatchers. `approxPercentileFromIndex` and `approxPercentilesFromIndex` now share a single `scanNumericField` helper that pulls hits from Bleve and extracts the float64 field, then hand off to the t-digest helper. `exactPercentileFromIndex` and `nearestRank` (REQUIRE_ACCURATE path) untouched — same shape, same semantics.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` + `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go`: comment refresh — Accuracy doc now mentions t-digest, AggregationSpec.Percentiles describes the single t-digest pass, and `computeMetrics`'s third return is documented as the t-digest approximate flag.
- `go.mod` / `go.sum`: added `github.com/influxdata/tdigest v0.0.1`, dropped `github.com/HdrHistogram/hdrhistogram-go` (no longer referenced after the rewrite). `go mod tidy` is clean.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile.go` (new)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile_test.go` (new — 5 unit tests + memory gate + 1M bench)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go` (rewritten — HdrHistogram → t-digest)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/percentile_test.go` (removed `TestComputeApproxPercentileHdr_DirectCall` and `TestComputeApproxPercentilesHdr_DirectCall`; refreshed comments)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/percentile_bench_test.go` (kept the shared `gaussian10k`, `exactPercentileSort`, `BenchmarkExactPercentileSort` ground-truth helpers; dropped the HdrHistogram-direct accuracy test and benches)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` (comment refresh: Accuracy + Percentiles now mention t-digest)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go` (comment refresh: t-digest in approximate-flag doc)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` (comment refresh)
- `test/integration/phase6/phase6_percentile_groupby_test.go` (`referencePercentileForBucket` rewritten to t-digest's linear-interpolation convention; comment refresh)
- `go.mod`, `go.sum` (+ tdigest, − hdrhistogram-go)
- `prd.json` (mark US-368 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across 60+ packages, including `pkg/oss/aggregation`, `test/e2e`, `test/foundry_parity`, `test/northwind` (196s), `test/chinook` (289s)
- `go test -tags integration -run NEVERMATCH ./test/integration/phase6/...` confirms updated integration test still compiles
- 5 new t-digest unit tests + 1M accuracy gate + 1M memory gate PASS:
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentileTDigest_DirectCall` — 10k Gaussian within ±10% of analytic quantiles for p50/p90/p95/p99
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentilesTDigest_DirectCall` — single-pass multi-percentile shape + accuracy
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentileTDigest_VsExact` — 100k uniform p50/p90/p95/p99 ≤1% rel err vs exact (worst observed 0.000316)
  - `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_P99Under1Percent` — 1M Gaussian p99 rel err 0.0466% (PRD ≤1%)
  - `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_MemoryUnder16KB` — digest footprint 15832 B (PRD ≤16384 B)
- `BenchmarkApproxPercentileTDigest_OneMillion-16  3  77,596,639 ns/op` ≈ 78 ms per 1M-point pass at p=99

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `tdigest.NewWithCompression(100)` is the right knob for hitting the PRD's "1M points ≤ 16 KiB" memory budget. Default compression `1000` blows past 160 KiB. The library's centroid struct is two `float64`s (16 B), and the only structural caps are `2c` processed + `8c` unprocessed. Multiply by 16 to get the upper-bound footprint; the cumulative slice (≤ `2c+1` float64s) is the only meaningful add-on after `process()` runs.
- For unit-weight centroids (every bucket smaller than `2c`), t-digest's `Quantile` is *linear interpolation between two consecutive sorted values at half-integer cumulative weights* — i.e., for `index = q·n`, `lower = ceil(index − 0.5)`, result = `(z2·v[lower-1] + z1·v[lower]) / (z1 + z2)` where `z1 = index − (lower − 0.5)`, `z2 = (lower + 0.5) − index`. Any test that wants to compare against an exact reference at small N must use this same convention; nearest-rank disagrees by a full bucket on small N (Argentina N=16 at p95: nearest-rank → sorted[15] vs t-digest → 0.3·sorted[14] + 0.7·sorted[15]).
- The HdrHistogram dependency disappears entirely after this story. `go mod tidy` removes it from `go.mod`/`go.sum`. Future iterations don't need the `percentileScale = 1000.0` shift/scale dance — t-digest accepts any float64 (including negatives) directly without preprocessing.
- The `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_*` accuracy and memory gates are gated by `testing.Short()` so `go test -short` skips them, but `go test ./...` (the default) runs them. They take ~150 ms total — cheap enough to keep in the regular test path rather than -bench-only.
- `digestFootprint(td)` is a deterministic upper-bound calculation rather than a runtime.MemStats delta, because allocator noise on `runtime.MemStats` made the latter approach non-portable. The accounting matches the library's slice/struct layout: processed cap × 16 B + maxUnprocessed cap × 16 B + (len(processed)+1) × 8 B + struct overhead.
- `BenchmarkApproxPercentileTDigest_OneMillion` at b.N=1 measures the entire 1M-point ingest + Quantile cost, which makes per-op numbers dominated by allocator + sort time. If you need fine-grained Add-only timing, refactor to allocate the digest once outside the b.N loop and call `Add` inside; current bench keeps the e2e shape so the 78 ms/op is "what a real query would cost".
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-369 - Action Saga 完整化（多 step + 补偿 + idempotency）

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000083_action_sagas.{up,down}.sql` adds three tables: `action_sagas` (saga_id PK, idempotency_key UNIQUE, status RUNNING|SUCCESS|COMPENSATING|COMPENSATED|FAILED, result_json snapshot for replay), `action_saga_steps` (step_id PK, step_index, action_type, parameters/edits_json/inverse_edits_json snapshots, status PENDING|APPLIED|FAILED|COMPENSATED|COMPENSATION_FAILED), `action_saga_dlq` (dlq_id PK, saga_id FK, edits_json carry-over, status PENDING|RESOLVED|DROPPED, attempts counter).
- `pkg/actions/saga_store.go` (new): defines `Saga`, `SagaStep`, `SagaDLQEntry` structs, the narrow `SagaStore` interface (CreateSaga, GetSagaByIdempotencyKey, GetSaga, UpdateSagaStatus, CreateSagaStep/UpdateSagaStep/ListSagaSteps, EnqueueDLQ/ListDLQ/UpdateDLQStatus), the `ErrSagaIdempotencyConflict` sentinel for unique-constraint races, plus `MarshalEdits` helper canonicalising edit slices to JSON for both the executor and the PG store.
- `pkg/actions/executor.go`: added `sagaStore SagaStore` field on `Executor` plus `SetSagaStore`/`SagaStore()` setters; reused the same setter-injection pattern as `ActionApprovalStore`/`ActionJobStore`. Tagged `BatchError` fields with `json:"phase|failedActionIndex|actionType|message"` and explicitly marked `Cause error` as `json:"-"` so the SagaResult survives the persist→replay round-trip.
- `pkg/actions/saga.go`: extended SagaResult with `SagaID`, `Status`, `IdempotencyKey`, `Replayed`, `DLQEntries` wire fields. New `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions(ctx, ontologyRID, reqs, SagaOptions{IdempotencyKey, RequestedBy})` is the US-369 entrypoint — it persists the saga header, every step, edits per APPLIED step, inverse edits per COMPENSATED step, advances status through RUNNING → COMPENSATING → COMPENSATED|FAILED, and snapshots the final SagaResult JSON onto the header row for idempotency replay. Idempotency replay returns the prior result with `Replayed=true` and re-surfaces the original `*BatchError` so failure semantics match. The legacy `ApplyBatchSaga(ctx, ontologyRID, reqs)` is now a thin wrapper for backwards compatibility.
- `runCompensations` widened to `(ctx, ontologyRID, prepared, sagaID, stepIDs) → (results, edits, batchID, offset, dlq)`: when persistence is wired, every successfully-rolled-back step row advances to COMPENSATED with `inverse_edits_json` populated; every compensator that fails to prepare OR fails to commit lands as a fresh DLQ row (`PENDING` status, edits carried for replay) and the source step row transitions to `COMPENSATION_FAILED`. The terminal saga status picks `FAILED` when DLQ rows exist, otherwise `COMPENSATED`.
- `pkg/actions/saga_handler.go` (new): three new handler entrypoints — `ApplySaga` for `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontology}/actions/applySaga` (body shape `{idempotencyKey, steps[]}` returning the SagaResult shape), `ListSagaDLQ` for `GET .../actions/saga/dlq?status=PENDING`, `RetrySagaDLQ` for `POST .../actions/saga/dlq/{dlqId}/retry`, `DropSagaDLQ` for the operator dismissal path. Failure responses embed the `SagaResult` alongside the API error envelope so SDK clients see compensations/DLQ ids on the error path.
- `pkg/actions/saga.go::RetrySagaDLQ`: replays a DLQ entry's edits as a fresh `funnel.EditBatch`. On success the handler transitions the row to RESOLVED; on failure it bumps `attempts` + records `failure_message` and leaves status PENDING for the next retry.
- `cmd/server/action_saga_store.go` (new): concrete PG implementation of `actions.SagaStore`. The `CreateSaga` path detects `SQLSTATE 23505` / "duplicate key value" on the unique partial idempotency_key index and returns `actions.ErrSagaIdempotencyConflict` so the executor can race cleanly. Mirror of `pgActionApprovalStore`/`pgActionJobStore` shape; lives in `cmd/server` so the dependency direction stays clean.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: wires `deps.ActionExecutor.SetSagaStore(newPGActionSagaStore(deps.PGPool))` next to the approval-store wiring (PG-gated block), plus four new chi route registrations under the existing `actions/` group: `applySaga`, `saga/dlq` (GET), `saga/dlq/{dlqId}/retry`, `saga/dlq/{dlqId}/drop`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: full OpenAPI 3 entries for the 4 new routes (request/response schemas, query params, error responses). The contract test enforces both files stay byte-identical, so the embedded copy was synced.
