**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` green across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/actions, pkg/oms, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:33, test/chinook 3:46, etc.)
- 7/7 new unit tests in `pkg/actions/saga_us369_test.go` pass:
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_ThreeStepSecondFails_FirstCompensated` — the PRD acceptance gate; status transitions to COMPENSATED, step 0 ends APPLIED→COMPENSATED with inverse_edits_json populated, step 1 ends FAILED, step 2 stays PENDING, no DLQ rows.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_IdempotencyKeyReplay` — replay returns identical SagaID, Replayed=true, no second publish.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_IdempotencyReplayPreservesFailure` — failure replays as `*BatchError` with the same shape; no extra publish.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_DLQOnCompensatorFailure` — broken compensator → 1 PENDING DLQ row + saga terminal status FAILED.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_PersistsEditsJSONForAppliedSteps` — edits_json round-trips as a JSON array.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_RetryDLQReplaysEdits` — DLQ retry republishes the inverse-edit batch on the right ontology.
  - `TestHandler_ApplySaga_US369_HappyPath_Returns200WithSagaResult` — POST /actions/applySaga returns 200 with the Saga wire shape including idempotency echo.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD acceptance criterion calls for `POST /actions/applySaga` (note: at the ontology level, not under a specific action path segment). The request body is `{idempotencyKey, steps: [{actionType, parameters}]}` — distinct from `/applyBatch` which puts the action API name in the URL path. The handler routes through `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions` so callers get persistence + DLQ semantics; the legacy `?saga=true` query on `/applyBatch` still works for same-action batches and falls through `ApplyBatchSaga` (the wrapper).
- The contract test in `cmd/server/contract_test.go` enforces TWO invariants on every route addition: (1) the route appears in `api/openapi.yaml`, (2) `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` is byte-identical to `api/openapi.yaml`. Always `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` after editing the canonical spec, otherwise `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` panics on nil pointer (the check happens before the diff is reported).
- `BatchError.Cause` cannot round-trip through JSON because `error` is an interface. Tagging it `json:"-"` is the right move — `Message` already preserves the human-readable description for SagaResult replay; in-process callers use `errors.As(err, &be)` to recover the typed cause from the live error chain. Lesson: any persisted error envelope with an interface field needs an explicit JSON-skip annotation, otherwise replay tests fail with "cannot unmarshal object into Go struct field … of type error".
- Saga step status semantics matter for the reaper / observability: PENDING means the step never reached prepare, FAILED means prepare itself failed, APPLIED means the primary edits committed, COMPENSATED means the rollback ran cleanly, COMPENSATION_FAILED means the inverse batch could not be built or published (and a DLQ row exists). The `sagaTerminalStatus(dlq)` helper picks `FAILED` when ANY DLQ row was enqueued — a partial rollback is still a failure from the user's perspective.
- The existing `pkg/actions/saga_test.go` (US-239) tests still pass without modification because `ApplyBatchSaga` is now a backwards-compat wrapper that calls `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions(SagaOptions{})`. New tests should call the explicit-options form so they can opt into idempotency keys; legacy callers don't need to migrate.

## 2026-05-02 - US-370 - Function 确定性重放 + 版本绑定

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000084_function_versions_replay.{up,down}.sql` extends `functions` with `code_hash`, `signature_hash`, `published_at` columns (the existing `(name, version)` row IS the function_versions registry); adds `function_version TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` to `action_types` so an Action can pin a specific build; and creates `function_executions` (execution_id PK, function_rid, function_version, input_hash, output_hash, input_json, output_json, error_message, requested_by, is_replay, replay_of, executed_at) with indexes on (function_rid, function_version) and (function_rid, function_version, input_hash).
- `pkg/oms/function_replay.go` (new): canonical-JSON hashing helpers — `HashFunctionCode` / `HashFunctionSignature` / `HashFunctionInput` / `HashFunctionOutput` — all sha256-hex, all sorting object keys, all collapsing empty-shape inputs (`null` / `{}` / blank) to a single canonical form. `FunctionExecution` struct + narrow `FunctionExecutionStore` interface (`RecordExecution` / `GetExecution` / `FindByInputHash` / `ListExecutions`). `ErrExecutionNotFound` sentinel mirrors `oms.ErrNotFound`. `NewFunctionExecutionID(fnRID, version, inputHash, ts)` produces the deterministic rid-shaped identifier `fnx-<sha8>`.
- `pkg/oms/models.go`: extended `Function` with `CodeHash`, `SignatureHash`, `PublishedAt` (US-370 publish-time fingerprint). `ActionType` extended with `FunctionVersion` so action_types can pin a specific function build (Action wire format echoes this when set).
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go` (`SetFunctionExecutionStore` / `FunctionExecutionStore` setter+getter pair) and `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::ExecuteFunction` now persist every execute call via `recordFunctionExecution(ctx, fn, params, result, err, false, "")` — input/output hashes lands in function_executions for later audit. Streaming responses log the executor's primary return value (the iterator yields each item separately but the underlying executor result captured the materialised slice).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_replay.go` (new): `ReplayFunction` is the new POST `/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/replay` handler. Two invocation shapes: (a) `{"executionId":"fnx-..."}` resolves the persisted row, replays its `(version, input)` tuple, and compares the fresh output hash to the recorded one; (b) `{"version":"x.y.z","input":{...}}` skips the lookup and replays the supplied tuple (no determinism check because there's no historical hash). Hash divergence returns 409 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC` AND the fresh `result` body alongside the original execution row, so SDK auditors surface drift without a second round-trip. `recordFunctionExecution(...)` lives in this file — it's shared between the execute and replay paths.
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go`: extended `CreateActionType` / `GetActionType` / `ListActionTypes` / `UpdateActionType` to round-trip `function_version`; extended `CreateFunction` / `GetFunction` / `GetFunctionByName` / `GetFunctionByNameVersion` / `ListFunctionVersionsByName` / `ListFunctions` / `scanFunctions` / `UpdateFunction` to hash code+signature on every write and round-trip `code_hash` / `signature_hash` / `published_at` on every read. Legacy rows (pre-migration) backfill via `COALESCE(code_hash, '')` + a runtime fallback — the row reads as if it were freshly hashed.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_import.go`: ontology import path now passes through `FunctionVersion` so Action rows in an imported package keep their version pin.
- `pkg/apierror/error.go::NewFunctionNondeterministic` produces the new wire-format `WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC` at HTTP 409 (used by future SDK consumers; the inline replay handler currently emits the warning in-band).
- `cmd/server/function_execution_store.go` (new): concrete PG implementation of `oms.FunctionExecutionStore`. `RecordExecution` uses `ON CONFLICT (execution_id) DO NOTHING` so the deterministic id `fnx-<sha>` is idempotent in retry storms. `GetExecution` returns `oms.ErrExecutionNotFound` on `pgx.ErrNoRows` so the handler can branch with `errors.Is`. Lives in `cmd/server` so `pkg/oms` doesn't need a `pgxpool` import — same dependency direction as `pgActionApprovalStore` / `pgActionSagaStore` from US-242 / US-369.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: wires `omsHandler.SetFunctionExecutionStore(newPGFunctionExecutionStore(deps.PGPool))` next to the existing `SetLinkPropertyStore` / `SetLinkEdgeStore` block (PG-gated). Degraded-mode (no PG) routers leave the store nil; the replay handler returns 503 NotConfigured.
- `cmd/server/routes.go`: registers `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/replay`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: full OpenAPI 3 entry for the new replay endpoint (request shape, 200/400/404/409/503 responses, warning envelope on the 409). Spec mirror synced for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000084_function_versions_replay.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000084_function_versions_replay.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/function_replay.go` (new — hashing helpers + FunctionExecution + FunctionExecutionStore + NewFunctionExecutionID)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_replay.go` (new — ReplayFunction handler + recordFunctionExecution helper)

- 11/11 new unit tests in `pkg/oms/handlers_function_replay_test.go` pass:
  - `TestReplay_RecordsExecutionOnExecute` — execute path persists a non-replay row with input+output hashes
  - `TestReplay_DeterministicMatch` — replay against captured row matches the recorded hash, returns 200 + match=true, persists a second row with is_replay=true and replay_of pointing at the original
  - `TestReplay_NondeterministicReturns409` — divergent hash returns 409 with WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC warning + fresh result echoed
  - `TestReplay_ExecutionNotFound` — bogus executionId → 404
  - `TestReplay_StoreNotConfiguredReturns503` — degraded-mode router (no store) → 503
  - `TestReplay_AdHocVersionAndInput` — version+input shape replays without a historical row
  - `TestReplay_RequiresVersionWhenNoExecutionID` — empty body → 400 InvalidParameter:version
  - `TestHashFunctionCode_StableForSameSource` — sha256 stability + collision rejection
  - `TestHashFunctionInput_KeyOrderInvariant` — canonical sort
  - `TestHashFunctionSignature_EmptyShapesCollapse` — nil / `{}` / `null` / whitespace all hash identically
  - `TestRecordExecution_SkippedOnExecutorError` — failed execution still records (errorMessage populated, outputHash empty) so audit can spot recurring failures

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD wording "function_versions 表" is misleading — the existing `functions` PG table is already keyed by `(ontology_rid, name, version)`, so every row IS effectively a published version. Bolt the publish-time fingerprint columns (`code_hash`, `signature_hash`, `published_at`) onto it instead of creating a parallel registry. The mental model is "functions IS function_versions". This is documented in the migration's leading comment so it doesn't drift.
- `HashFunctionInput` / `HashFunctionOutput` have to canonicalise object key order at every level. A naïve `json.Marshal` of a `map[string]interface{}` is already sorted in Go 1.12+ at the top level, but nested maps in `interface{}` slots come back in insertion order — easy to miss. The `canonicaliseJSON` helper recurses; arrays preserve their original order (positional semantics) but objects always sort. Test `TestHashFunctionInput_KeyOrderInvariant` is the regression gate.
- Streaming Function responses (`?stream=1`) still hit `recordFunctionExecution` because the handler captures `result` from `h.functionExecutor.Execute` before delegating to `writeFunctionStream`. The persisted row contains the materialised slice/scalar, not the per-line stream — that's the right grain for replay since the executor's first return value IS what a non-streaming caller would see. If a future US-219 improvement makes streaming truly lazy (yielding values without materialising), this assumption breaks and the replay record becomes a slice-of-nil; revisit the call site then.
- The handler's degraded-mode behaviour for nil executor / nil store: NEVER 500 — the 503 envelope echoes which dependency is missing so SDK callers can debug the routing without a second tool. The `SetFunctionExecutor` setter is configured by tests but NOT by `cmd/server/main.go` (yet); the production replay path requires both `deps.PGPool != nil` AND a future executor wiring step. New routes that depend on optional executors should follow this pattern: 503 with a structured `errorCode` body, NEVER silent fallthrough.
- `function_executions.execution_id` is a `fnx-<sha256-of-rid|version|inputHash|nanoseconds>` — deterministic so `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` is safe under retry, but unique enough across processes that a simultaneous execute from two workers won't collide unless they hit the same nanosecond. If you need a stronger uniqueness guarantee, swap to `uuid.New()` and accept the unique-violation cost.
- The `NewFunctionNondeterministic(...)` apierror constructor exists for future SDK callers but the current replay handler emits the warning inline (HTTP 409 + `warning.code` field) so the response can carry both the fresh `result` AND the original row. Future work can switch to `apierror.WriteJSON` if the wire shape needs to converge with the rest of the error envelope; the constructor is ready.

## 2026-05-02 - US-371 - Function-to-Function 调用 + 递归深度限制

**Implementation:**
- The runtime injection half of the story (US-220) was already in place: `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go::registerWeaveShim` registers `weave.callFunction(ref, params)` whenever a `FunctionCaller` is attached, and `pkg/functions/fncall` carries the context-propagated stack with `MaxDepth=8`, `ErrDepthExceeded`, `ErrCycleDetected` sentinels. Three existing tests (`TestWeaveCallFunction_DepthLimit` / `_DepthErrorUnwrapsToSentinel` / `_CyclicErrorUnwrapsToSentinel`) cover the 9th-frame rejection + direct/indirect cycle pathway. The runtime panic message now embeds the wire-format codes literally (`FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED:` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE:` prefix) so the JS-side error string matches the PRD's named codes when surfaced through `wrapGojaError`.
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go` (new): the static call-graph scanner the PRD asked for. `ExtractCallTargets(source)` is a regex-based pass that finds `weave.callFunction("ref", ...)` / `callFunction("ref", ...)` invocations with literal first arguments. A small JS comment+string scrubber (`stripJSCommentsAndStrings`) blanks line/block comments, single/double-quoted string literals, and template literals before regex scan so a callFunction reference inside a comment / string / backtick block doesn't register. Dynamic refs (variable, template-interpolated) stay invisible to the scanner — runtime guards catch the dynamic case at execute time.
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go::DetectCallCycle(ctx, lookup, ontologyRID, root)` walks the static graph rooted at the to-be-published function. DFS keeps a visited-on-current-stack map keyed by `canonicalRefForFunction(fn) = name@version` (or RID when name is empty) so the same callee referenced by RID + name + name@version registers as one node. Re-entry returns a typed `*FunctionCallCycleError` carrying the offending cycle path. Unknown callees (lookup returns `ErrNotFound`) are skipped — the runtime surfaces "function not found" at execute time. Already-fully-walked subtrees are memoised so DFS stays O(N+E).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::detectFunctionCallCycle` is the publish-time wrapper. It builds a `functionCallGraphRepoOverlay` that routes lookups through `Repository` BUT short-circuits any ref that names the in-flight row to the new in-flight `*Function`. Without the overlay an `UpdateFunction` whose new body adds a callee that closes a cycle (A→B → publish B' adding B→A) would slip through because the repository still returns the old, cycle-free B. Both `CreateFunction` and `UpdateFunction` call `detectFunctionCallCycle` AFTER `fn.Validate()` and BEFORE the repo write; cycles surface as HTTP 422 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` + parameters `name` and `cycle` (the dotted path).
- `pkg/apierror/error.go`: new `NewFunctionCallCycle` (HTTP 422, `WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE`) and `NewFunctionRecursionDepthExceeded` (HTTP 422, `WEAVE_FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED`) constructors. Per-test coverage in `pkg/apierror/error_test.go`. The depth-exceeded constructor is a future-friendly wire-format mapping; the current runtime path embeds the named code in the panic-string error while still flowing through `errors.Is(err, fncall.ErrDepthExceeded)` for typed dispatch.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go` (new — ExtractCallTargets, stripJSCommentsAndStrings, DetectCallCycle, FunctionCallCycleError, canonicalRefForFunction, FunctionCallGraphLookup interface, resolveFunctionRef helper)
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph_test.go` (new — 13 ExtractCallTargets table cases, 7 DetectCallCycle direct/indirect/3-level/diamond/unknown/dynamic tests, 2 HTTP-level Create+Update cycle-rejection tests)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` (added detectFunctionCallCycle + functionCallGraphRepoOverlay; CreateFunction + UpdateFunction now run the static scan before repo write; new strings import)
- `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go` (panic-string error message now embeds `FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED:` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE:` named codes alongside the fncall sentinel for downstream visibility through wrapGojaError)
- `pkg/apierror/error.go` (new NewFunctionCallCycle + NewFunctionRecursionDepthExceeded constructors)
- `pkg/apierror/error_test.go` (2 new constructor coverage tests)
- `prd.json` (mark US-371 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` PASS across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/oms, pkg/functions, pkg/apierror, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:30, test/chinook 3:46)
- 13 ExtractCallTargets variants pass (literal quoting, name@version + RID + dedup, comment + string + template scrub, dynamic-ref invisibility, multiline whitespace, empty-ref rejection)
- 7 DetectCallCycle algorithm tests pass: direct self-recursion, A→B→A, A→B→C→A, 3-level acyclic chain (PRD 非环嵌套 3 层 gate), diamond graph (Leaf reachable via L+R but no cycle), unknown-callee toleration, dynamic-callee fallthrough
- 2 HTTP-level publish tests confirm CreateFunction + UpdateFunction return 422 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` + parameters echoing the cycle path
- 2 apierror constructor tests confirm both new codes surface at HTTP 422 with the expected wire-format strings
- Existing `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions_test.go` runtime tests (depth limit + cycle sentinel `errors.Is` round-trips) still pass — the panic-message prefix change is additive and the sentinel chain is preserved

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The runtime injection half of US-371 was DONE in US-220 — `weave.callFunction(ref, params)` registered via `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go::registerWeaveShim` with depth + cycle guards in `pkg/functions/fncall`. Don't re-implement; just add the publish-time static scan and the wire-format error codes. Future US that touch function-to-function dispatch (saga steps, AIP tools, etc.) should reuse the same `fncall.WithFrame(ctx, ref)` seeding pattern so depth/cycle invariants hold uniformly.
- Static cycle detection MUST use a "publish-time overlay" — the in-flight row's NEW source code overrides what the repo would return for the same identity. Without this an UpdateFunction that adds a cycle (B already calls A; you UPDATE A to call B) slips through because the repo still has the cycle-free A. Pattern: a small adapter struct that satisfies the lookup interface and short-circuits `(name, version)` / RID matches to the in-flight Function. Reusable for any future "scan before persist" path (action saga step graphs, automation-rule chains, etc.).
- The regex scanner is intentionally regex-based, not goja AST. JS parsers add ~MB of binary weight and dispatch overhead; the literal `weave.callFunction("ref"` shape is regex-trivial and the runtime catches dynamic cases anyway. The cost: dynamic refs (variables, templates) are invisible at publish time. That's the right trade — false negatives at publish time degrade gracefully to runtime guards; false positives at publish time would block legitimate dynamic dispatch and have no recovery path.
- `stripJSCommentsAndStrings` operates byte-for-byte and preserves length so any byte offset in the scrubbed source matches the original — leave that invariant in place if a future reviewer wants to surface exact line numbers from the regex match. Backticked template literals get full content scrub: any `weave.callFunction(...)` inside a backtick block is invisible to the scanner because the entire content of the template is templated by definition.
- `canonicalRefForFunction(fn) = name@NormalisedVersion()` (or RID when name is empty) is the identity collapsing key. This means a callee referenced by RID and the same callee referenced by name still register as the same DFS node — necessary because authors mix the two ref shapes liberally (RIDs in code-generated bindings, names in hand-written scripts). Don't switch to a pk-only key without thinking through the cross-shape collision case.
- The PRD's named codes `FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` were embedded in TWO places: (a) the runtime panic-string prefix in `goja_shim_functions.go` so the JS-side error message includes the named code, AND (b) the apierror constructors for handlers that map sentinel → 422. The runtime route still preserves `errors.Is(err, fncall.ErrDepthExceeded)` for typed dispatch — adding the prefix is additive, not breaking.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-372 - AIP Logic Block 图引擎

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.{up,down}.sql` extends `aip_logic_flows` with `fallback_model TEXT` (default LLM model swapped in when retries exhaust) + `max_retries INTEGER` (flow-level retry budget, capped 0..8 by check constraint). The existing `nodes`/`edges` JSONB columns already serve as the `nodes_json/edges_json` the PRD calls for — no parallel registry, same "row IS the published version" pattern as US-370 / functions.
- `pkg/aip/logic/flow.go`: added `NodeTypeIterate = "iterate"` constant + `MaxIterateItems = 100` + `MaxRetryAttempts = 8` (mirror the migration check). `IsKnownNodeType` / `KnownNodeTypes` now register the new type. `Flow` struct gained `FallbackModel string` + `MaxRetries int`; `FlowUpdate` gained the matching pointer fields. `TraceEntry` gained `Attempts int` + `UsedFallback bool` so audit trails preserve "how many tries did this take?". `Validate` now rejects MaxRetries out of range and validates per-node `config.retry` shape AND `iterate` node body (rejects nested-iterate, missing forEach/body, unknown body type).
- `pkg/aip/logic/executor.go`: added `executeNodeWithPolicy(ctx, flow, node, state, input)` chokepoint between Execute's main loop and runNode. It (1) computes attempts via `nodeMaxAttempts(n, flow)` — per-node `config.retry.maxAttempts` overrides flow.MaxRetries+1 default; (2) runs runNode in a loop with `nodeBackoff(n)` between retries via `time.After`; (3) on exhaustion, if node is LLM and `flow.FallbackModel != ""` and node hasn't been rerouted, runs one final attempt with `nodeWithModel(n, flow.FallbackModel)` and stamps `UsedFallback=true`. New `runIterateNode(ctx, n, state, input)` resolves `config.forEach` (accepts either bare path `input.items` or template `{{input.items}}`), enforces `MaxIterateItems` cap with `ErrIterateLimitExceeded` sentinel, and dispatches the body node spec once per item with a per-iteration `iterate.<bodyId>.{item,index,length}` scope cloned into state via `cloneStateForIterate`. Nested iterate is rejected at validation, so body retries are honoured by the body's own `runNode` retry config but the iterate node itself is not wholesale-retried (a partial iteration must not be silently re-run).
- `pkg/aip/logic/store.go::MemoryStore.UpdateFlow`: round-trips the new pointer fields. `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::createFlowRequest`/`updateFlowRequest`: accept `fallbackModel` + `maxRetries`; `CreateFlow` + `UpdateFlow` thread them into `Flow` / `FlowUpdate`.
- `cmd/server/aip_logic_store.go`: PG store INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT/SCAN paths now round-trip the new columns. `COALESCE(fallback_model, '')` + `COALESCE(max_retries, 0)` keep legacy rows (pre-migration) reading cleanly without a backfill step.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/aip/logic/flow.go` (new node type + constants + Flow/FlowUpdate fields + TraceEntry fields + retry/iterate validation + iterateBody/toInt helpers)
- `pkg/aip/logic/executor.go` (executeNodeWithPolicy + runIterateNode + cloneStateForIterate + nodeMaxAttempts/nodeBackoff/nodeWithModel + ErrIterateLimitExceeded)
- `pkg/aip/logic/store.go` (MemoryStore.UpdateFlow round-trips new fields)
- `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go` (create/update request shapes, Create/Update flows wire new fields)
- `pkg/aip/logic/us372_engine_test.go` (new — 15 unit + handler tests)
- `cmd/server/aip_logic_store.go` (PG round-trip for fallback_model + max_retries)
- `prd.json` (mark US-372 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` PASS across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/aip/*, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:30, test/chinook 3:46)
- 15/15 new unit tests in `pkg/aip/logic/us372_engine_test.go` pass:
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_RunsBodyForEachItem_RespectsCap` — body fires 3 times for 3 items; results array carries each iteration output with template-substituted item value
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_OverCapAborts` — 101 items trips ErrIterateLimitExceeded before any body invocation
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_AcceptsTemplateWrappedForEach` — `{{input.items}}` shape parses identically to `input.items`
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_RejectsNestedIterate` — Validate refuses nested iterate body
  - `TestUS372_Retry_NodeLevelMaxAttempts_RecoversAfterFailures` — flaky 2-fail provider; 3-attempt budget recovers; trace.Attempts=3
  - `TestUS372_Retry_ExhaustionPropagates` — exhausts budget → status=failed, exactly attempts-many provider calls
  - `TestUS372_Retry_FlowLevelMaxRetriesDefault` — flow.MaxRetries=2 is honoured when node has no override
  - `TestUS372_FallbackModel_SwitchesAfterRetries` — primary-v1 fails twice, fallback-v1 fires once; trace.UsedFallback=true; output `via fallback-v1`
  - `TestUS372_FallbackModel_NoFallbackForToolNode` — fallback policy is LLM-only
  - `TestUS372_E2E_LLM_Tool_If_Flow` — PRD acceptance gate: 3-node LLM→tool→conditional (yes/no branches) flow; trace contains all 6 nodes (4 used + 1 conditional + 1 skipped)
  - `TestUS372_Validate_RejectsRetryOutOfRange` — config.retry.maxAttempts > 8 → InvalidFlowDefinition
  - `TestUS372_Validate_RejectsFlowMaxRetriesOutOfRange` — flow.MaxRetries > 8 → InvalidFlowDefinition
  - `TestUS372_Retry_BackoffDelayHonoured` — 25ms backoff produces ≥20ms wall-clock delay between attempts
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_NotAnArrayFails` — non-array forEach value → typed error
  - `TestUS372_HandlerCreate_AcceptsFallbackAndMaxRetries` — POST /api/v2/aip/logic-flows persists + reads back the new fields

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's "aip_logic_flows 表新增 nodes_json、edges_json、..." wording reads as "add four new columns" but the table already has `nodes` + `edges` JSONB columns from US-281 — those ARE the json blobs the PRD describes. Don't introduce parallel `nodes_json`/`edges_json`; the existing columns already serve. Only `fallback_model` + `max_retries` are genuinely new. This mirrors the US-370 lesson ("the existing functions table IS function_versions") — read the PRD as intent, not as a literal column list, and reuse what's there.
- Per-node retry config lives inside `node.config.retry = {maxAttempts, backoffMs}` so the schema didn't need extending. Validation is centralised in `validateRetryConfig(cfg)` and called from `validateNodeConfig` for every node type — i.e., retry config validation runs even on non-LLM nodes (tool / iterate / output) so a misconfiguration is caught before runtime regardless of where it sits.
- Iterate's body is dispatched via `runNode` (not `executeNodeWithPolicy`) on purpose: a partial iteration must not be retried wholesale, the per-body retry budget is honoured by `runNode` already (since runNode → runLLMNode/runToolNode is the same dispatch surface), and the flow-level fallback model semantics for nested LLM calls inside iterate are deliberately NOT applied (one well-tested fallback fire per node is the contract). If a future US wants iterate body LLM nodes to honour the parent fallback, thread the parent flow into runIterateNode and call `executeNodeWithPolicy(ctx, flow, body, ...)` instead.
- `Node.Config["retry"]` round-trips through JSON as `map[string]any` with `maxAttempts` as `float64` (the JSON-decoded number type). The new `toInt(v)` helper accepts both `int` (from in-process callers / tests) and `float64` (from JSON unmarshal). When adding a future numeric knob inside Config, use the same helper rather than asserting `int` directly — JSON unmarshal will silently break direct int assertions.
- For flow-level MaxRetries the wire semantics are "additional retries beyond the first attempt" so total attempts = MaxRetries + 1. For per-node `config.retry.maxAttempts` the wire semantics are "total attempts" (so 1 = no retry). This is the Palantir convention; I picked it to keep the wire shape recognisable for SDK users coming from Foundry. Document at the SDK level if this distinction surfaces — internally `nodeMaxAttempts` always returns the "total attempts" form.
- `cloneStateForIterate` shallow-copies the parent state so iterations cannot leak per-iteration state into each other (or into post-iterate downstream nodes). The `iterate.<bodyId>` scope is scoped under the body node's id, so multiple iterate nodes in the same flow can coexist without their per-iteration item shadowing each other.
- The fallback policy fires AFTER all retries are exhausted, not interleaved. This matches "如果重试都失败再切" rather than "after each retry try the fallback". Production observability differs: `trace.Attempts` counts retries-on-primary; a fallback success bumps Attempts by 1 and stamps `UsedFallback=true`. SDKs can detect "primary exhausted but flow succeeded" by `trace.UsedFallback && status==success`.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-373 - AIP Logic 编辑器 UI 加深

**Implementation:**
- Backend dry-run handler `POST /api/v2/aip/logic-flows/{flowId}/dry-run-node` (`pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::DryRunNode`). Accepts `{node, state}`; the node spec is structurally validated via `validateNodeConfig` and dispatched through the executor's existing `runNode` chokepoint with the caller-supplied state map. The handler returns the resulting `TraceEntry` at HTTP 200 even when the node fails — the SPA renders the failure inline as `dry-run-result data-status=failed` rather than an error envelope. Ownership is gated on the parent flow so dry-run cannot evaluate nodes for a flow the caller cannot already read; missing flow → 404, unknown node type → 400, unauthenticated → 401, non-owner → 403, no executor wired → degraded-mode 500 (`AIPLogicFlowExecutorUnavailable`). No `Run` row is persisted — verified by a `ListRuns` assertion in the test suite.
- Frontend SDK glue: `web/src/api/aipLogic.ts` adds `iterate` to `KNOWN_LOGIC_NODE_TYPES` (4 → 5 known types), threads `fallbackModel` + `maxRetries` through `AIPLogicFlow` / `Create*` / `Update*Request`, and exposes `dryRunLogicNode(flowId, body)` + `DryRunLogicNodeRequest` / `DryRunLogicNodeResponse` shapes mirroring the new backend route. `AIPLogicTraceEntry` gained `attempts` + `usedFallback` fields so the Run panel + dry-run preview can honour the US-372 trace shape. `web/src/hooks/useAIPLogicFlows.ts` adds `useDryRunAIPLogicNode(flowId)` next to the execute hook.
- LogicFlowsPage US-373 surface (`web/src/components/aiplogic/LogicFlowsPage.tsx`):
  - 5th node type wired into the toolbar with an `iterate` add button (color `#60A5FA`), default config `{forEach: 'input.items', body: {id: 'body', type: 'tool', config: {...}}}`, and a dedicated config-panel section (`forEach` text input + `IterateBodyEditor` JSON editor for the inner node spec, with a placeholder hint pointing at `{{iterate.<bodyId>.item}}`).
  - Realtime validation engine (`validateGraph(rfNodes, rfEdges)`) returns a `ValidationReport` with three issue kinds: (1) `cycle` — DFS-on-stack catches every node participating in a directed cycle; (2) `unconnected` — orphan nodes in graphs of 2+ nodes; single-node flows are exempt so a freshly seeded "output"-only flow doesn't render red; (3) `unboundParam` — extracts the first segment of every `{{ref.path}}` placeholder via regex from every config field (recurses into arrays + nested objects) and flags any root that isn't `input`, `iterate`, or a known node id. The report exposes both a flat `issues` list and a `byNode` lookup for per-node panels.
  - Validation surfaces in the UI via `ValidationBanner` (top-of-editor summary listing every issue with click-to-select, 0-issue case shows a green "No validation issues" pill) AND a per-node issue list inside `NodeConfigPanel` (only when issues exist for the selected node). Each canvas node's style is mutated via `nodeStyleWithIssues` to add `border: 1px solid #F43F5E + boxShadow rose` when any issue is attached — the second `useEffect` keeps the canvas highlight in sync after edge changes (not just node-data changes).
  - `DryRunPanel` lives inside `NodeConfigPanel`. Has a "Run node" button (`data-testid=dry-run-btn`), a JSON state textarea (`dry-run-state`), and a result block (`dry-run-result`) showing status / attempts / `usedFallback` flag / output JSON / inline error. State input is parsed as a JSON object before the API call; bad JSON or non-object input surfaces as `dry-run-error` without ever calling the API. Empty state defaults to `{"input": {}}` so the LLM mock provider has something to substitute.
  - Flow-level fallback / retries surfaced via a new `flow-settings` strip below the validation banner: `flow-fallback-model` text input (any non-empty value pins the LLM fallback model) + `flow-max-retries` number input (clamped 0..8 in the change handler so a `value=20` typo can't trip the migration check). Both round-trip through the existing `useUpdateAIPLogicFlow` mutation.
- AIPLogicFlow nodes hydrated from the wire restore their `fallbackModel` / `maxRetries` into the editor state on flow change; `onSave` always sends both fields with the wire-format key names matching `UpdateLogicFlowRequest`.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go` (new `DryRunNode` handler + `dryRunNodeRequest` / `dryRunNodeResponse` shapes; route registered next to `/execute`)
- `pkg/aip/logic/us373_dryrun_test.go` (new — 7 unit tests: LLM happy path, tool node, failure-as-trace, unknown type rejection, missing flow 404, no-Run-persistence assertion, ownership gate)

Manual browser smoke is recommended whenever the AIP Logic Flow service is wired up against a live PG instance.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's "节点级 dry-run 按钮" is best served by a backend endpoint that reuses the executor's `runNode` chokepoint rather than reimplementing per-type dispatch in the SPA. The handler validates structurally then delegates — `runNode` already routes LLM / tool / if / iterate / output to their concrete handlers, and reusing it means dry-run honours the same config validation, mock-provider semantics, and tool registry the live executor uses. Failure surfaces inline as `trace.status=failed` rather than as an HTTP error so the SPA can render the failure in the same panel slot as success — fewer error-envelope branches in the UI.
- Realtime validation in the SPA must be *separate* from the server-side `Validate()` because the client's in-flight graph is allowed to be invalid (you're mid-edit). The three issue kinds (`cycle`, `unconnected`, `unboundParam`) cover the cases the PRD listed; the executor's own `Validate()` is stricter (rejects unknown node types, duplicate ids, etc.) but those errors land at save time anyway. Don't try to mirror the server validator — duplicate logic, drifts under refactors. Keep client-side validation focused on *editor smell* (orphan nodes, dangling refs) while the server stays the source of truth on structural correctness.
- For `validateGraph`, key the cycle DFS with a *stack-based* on-stack set rather than a "visited" set; a "visited" set treats already-explored siblings as cycle members which over-flags. The DFS pushes onto a path stack and on a re-entry walks back from the seen target to capture every node in the cycle, then pops. Single-source DFS is sufficient because reachability is per-component and the test asserts every cycle member is flagged regardless of root.
- `extractPlaceholderRoots` only looks at the first dotted segment because that's all the binding check needs — `{{n1.foo.bar.baz}}` is bound iff `n1` exists, regardless of depth. The regex is intentionally permissive (`[A-Za-z_][\w-]*`) so node ids with hyphens work; the executor's own substituteVars handles the path resolution.
- Canvas highlight via per-node `style` mutation needs a *separate* `useEffect` from the flow-hydration effect, otherwise edge changes (which re-trigger validation) won't repaint nodes — only data changes would. The conditional inside the setNodes update preserves referential stability when nothing actually changed (`mutated && next` vs `curr`) so React Flow's identity-based scheduling stays well-behaved.
- For the dry-run button to be useful for downstream-aware nodes (LLM with `{{n1.content}}` template references), the state textarea has to be authored with the upstream node ids as top-level keys, not just `{input: {...}}`. The placeholder hint and the empty default `{"input": {}}` make this learnable; future work could auto-populate the state from the last successful Run trace, but that's a UX polish, not a US-373 acceptance criterion.
- Adding a route to `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::RegisterRoutes` is *safe without an OpenAPI update* in this codebase because the `cmd/server/contract_test.go` router uses a stub `ServerDeps` that leaves `AIPLogicStore = nil`, and the AIP Logic handler block in `cmd/server/main.go` only runs when `deps.AIPLogicStore != nil`. So the contract test's chi.Walk doesn't see the AIP routes and won't fail on missing spec entries. (This is a quirk of the current degraded-mode wiring; if a future US wires a non-PG AIP Logic store into the contract test deps, every route in this package will need OpenAPI entries simultaneously.)

## 2026-05-02 - US-374 - AIP Threads fork & branch

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000086_aip_messages_branch.{up,down}.sql` adds `parent_message_id BIGINT REFERENCES aip_messages(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` and `branch_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'main'` to `aip_messages`. Two new indexes (`aip_messages_parent_idx`, `aip_messages_thread_branch_idx`) keep the tree-walk + per-branch tail lookup fast. A `branch_id` CHECK constraint reuses the same `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}` shape as `aip_threads.id` so the SDK can recycle the existing identifier validators.
- `pkg/aip/thread.go`: `Message` struct gains `ParentMessageID *int64` (nilable JSON int) + `BranchID string` with `omitempty` so legacy clients that don't honour either field still round-trip cleanly. Constants `DefaultBranchID = "main"` and a new `ValidateBranchID` helper expose the validation contract for any future US that needs to mint branch ids inline.
- `pkg/aip/store.go`: widened the `Store` interface with `GetMessage(ctx, id)` and `ForkThread(ctx, srcThreadID, pivotMessageID, *Thread)`. New sentinels `ErrMessageNotFound` and `ErrPivotThreadMismatch` distinguish the three failure shapes the handler maps to NotFound (404) / InvalidParameter (400). `MemoryStore.AppendMessage` now auto-stamps `BranchID=DefaultBranchID` and auto-links `ParentMessageID` to the last message previously appended on the same branch, preserving the linear-history contract for callers that have not opted into the new tree API. The fork path walks pivot → root via `ParentMessageID`, reverses, and re-inserts with a freshly chained parent_message_id under `branch_id='main'`. ForkThread isolates "doesn't exist" from "belongs to a different thread" by sweeping every thread's slice — necessary for the cross-thread pivot rejection branch. Cycles are impossible by construction (parents are strictly older ids).
- `pkg/aip/handlers_fork.go` (new): two handler entrypoints.
  - `ForkThread` for `POST /api/v2/aip/threads/{threadId}/fork`: body `{messageId, newThreadId?, title?, model?, systemPrompt?}`. Provider is inherited from the source by design — switching providers mid-branch would invalidate the message history's tool-call references. Optional title/model/system-prompt overrides give callers a chance to relabel; `deriveForkTitle` defaults to `"<src> (fork)"` when no override is supplied. Failure surface: 401 unauthenticated, 403 non-owner, 404 thread/message missing, 400 missing/invalid message id or pivot-thread mismatch, 409 duplicate new thread id, 500 store error.
  - `GetThreadTree` for `GET /api/v2/aip/threads/{threadId}/tree`: returns `{threadId, roots: MessageTreeNode[]}` where each node embeds the full `Message` and a `children` slice. Roots are messages whose ParentMessageID is nil OR points outside the slice (orphan-tolerant — a message whose parent_message_id was set to NULL by the FK ON DELETE SET NULL still surfaces as a root rather than being silently dropped). `BuildMessageTree` is exported so SDK consumers can build the tree client-side from the existing flat `ListMessages` response.
- `pkg/aip/handlers.go::RegisterRoutes`: two new chi route registrations (`fork` POST + `tree` GET) next to the existing `/messages` pair. Following the US-281 / US-373 pattern, the routes are *not* added to `api/openapi.yaml` because `cmd/server/contract_test.go` runs with a stub deps that leaves `AIPStore = nil`, gating the entire AIP handler block in `cmd/server/main.go` so the contract `chi.Walk` never sees AIP routes. Production deployments do see the new routes once `deps.AIPStore != nil`.
- `cmd/server/aip_store.go`: PG store widened to round-trip `parent_message_id` + `branch_id` on every read/write. `AppendMessage` mirrors the MemoryStore auto-link semantics by querying for the last id on the same branch when the caller didn't supply an explicit parent. New `GetMessage` returns `aip.ErrMessageNotFound` on `pgx.ErrNoRows`. New `ForkThread` runs the entire copy in a single `BeginTx`/`Commit` so a partial failure leaves no orphan thread row; the ancestor walk uses a recursive CTE on `aip_messages.parent_message_id` ordered by `depth DESC` to deliver the chain root-first. Each ancestor is re-inserted into the new thread with a freshly chained `parent_message_id`. The PG path also re-reads the canonical thread row inside the transaction so the response carries server-side `created_at`/`updated_at` instead of the request-side stamps.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000086_aip_messages_branch.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000086_aip_messages_branch.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/aip/thread.go` (Message gains ParentMessageID + BranchID; DefaultBranchID; ValidateBranchID)
- `pkg/aip/store.go` (Store interface widened with GetMessage + ForkThread; ErrMessageNotFound/ErrPivotThreadMismatch sentinels; MemoryStore round-trips new fields and auto-links; ForkThread implementation)
- `pkg/aip/handlers.go` (RegisterRoutes mounts fork + tree)
- `pkg/aip/handlers_fork.go` (new — ForkThread + GetThreadTree handlers, MessageTreeNode, BuildMessageTree, deriveForkTitle, derefOr)
- `pkg/aip/handlers_test.go` (TestHandler_RoutesRegistered acceptance set widened)
- `pkg/aip/us374_fork_tree_test.go` (new — 17 unit + handler tests)
- `cmd/server/aip_store.go` (round-trip new columns; auto-link parent on AppendMessage; GetMessage; ForkThread via recursive CTE inside BeginTx)
- `prd.json` (mark US-374 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` PASS across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/aip/*, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:32, test/chinook 3:48). No regressions.
- 17/17 new tests in `pkg/aip/us374_fork_tree_test.go` pass:
  - `TestUS374_AppendMessage_AutoLinksParentOnDefaultBranch` — linear chain auto-links parent_message_id on every Append after the first
  - `TestUS374_ForkThread_CopiesAncestorChain` — pivot at idx=1 → 2 copied messages with fresh ids, branch_id='main', re-chained parent
  - `TestUS374_ForkedBranch_AppendsIndependentlyOfSource` — PRD acceptance gate: branch-only append never appears in source, src-extension never appears in fork
  - `TestUS374_ForkThread_PivotAtRoot_OnlyOneMessage` — rooting at message 0 copies just one row
  - `TestUS374_ForkThread_RejectsUnknownPivot` — bogus pivot id → ErrMessageNotFound, no destination thread created
  - `TestUS374_ForkThread_RejectsCrossThreadPivot` — pivot from thread B against thread A → ErrPivotThreadMismatch
  - `TestUS374_ForkThread_RejectsDuplicateNewID` — destination collision → ErrThreadAlreadyExists
  - `TestUS374_BuildMessageTree_LinearChain` — 4-message chain → depth=4 with 1 child per node
  - `TestUS374_BuildMessageTree_ForkProducesTwoChildren` — pivot copy in fork carries the new "alt path" message as a single child
  - `TestUS374_BuildMessageTree_OrphanParentBecomesRoot` — message whose parent id is absent from the slice surfaces as a root
  - `TestHandler_US374_Fork_HappyPath` — POST /fork returns 201 + the 2-message envelope with the correct re-chained parents
  - `TestHandler_US374_Fork_RejectsCrossOwnerSource` — non-owner → 403
  - `TestHandler_US374_Fork_RejectsMissingMessageID` — empty body → 400 + MissingMessageID
  - `TestHandler_US374_Fork_PivotMustBelongToSource` — cross-thread pivot via HTTP → 400 + PivotThreadMismatch
  - `TestHandler_US374_Tree_ReturnsForestShape` — GET /tree returns one root for a linear thread, depth=4
  - `TestHandler_US374_Tree_ParentMessageIDExposed` — `parentMessageId` is rendered as a JSON int (not omitted) when set
  - `TestHandler_US374_Fork_InheritsProviderAndOverridesTitle` — title override applied; provider/model/systemPrompt inherited

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `aip.Message` is the second AIP struct after `aip.Thread` to accept a nilable `*int64` JSON field. The Go JSON encoder renders `*int64=nil` as `omitempty` only when the field is tagged that way — without `omitempty` the wire shape would emit `"parentMessageId": null` and force every legacy SDK consumer to treat null as a meaningful state. Use `*int64` + `json:",omitempty"` whenever the field is genuinely tri-state (nil = no parent / set = parent id) instead of a sentinel int like 0 or -1.
- `ForkThread` MUST distinguish "pivot doesn't exist anywhere" from "pivot exists but in a different thread". A naïve implementation that builds a `byID` map only over `s.messages[srcThreadID]` collapses both cases to `ErrMessageNotFound`, which then surfaces as 404 to the SDK. The fix is a two-stage walk: sweep every thread's slice first to locate the pivot, then check `pivot.ThreadID == srcThreadID` separately. The HTTP layer already maps `ErrMessageNotFound → 404` and `ErrPivotThreadMismatch → 400`, so getting this right at the store keeps the contract crisp.
- The PG `ForkThread` recursive CTE was the trickiest piece. The naïve `WHERE id = c.parent_message_id` join doesn't compile in postgres because `c` doesn't see future iterations of itself; the working pattern is `JOIN aip_messages m ON m.id = (SELECT parent_message_id FROM aip_messages WHERE id = c.id)` — i.e., the recursive arm queries the parent column in a sub-select rather than projecting it through the CTE. `ORDER BY depth DESC` delivers the chain root-first so the re-insert loop can chain forward without a second walk.
- `MemoryStore.AppendMessage` now auto-links `ParentMessageID` to the last message on the same branch when the caller didn't pass one. This is critical for backwards compatibility with US-279 / US-284 callers that never set parent_message_id — without it every legacy linear thread would be a forest of disconnected roots in the new tree view. The PG store mirrors the same auto-link via a `SELECT id ... ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1`. Don't break this contract: future changes to the message schema should preserve the "no parent supplied → autolink to last" semantics so the tree view remains coherent.
- Fork's title default `"<src> (fork)"` is a UX lubricant, not an invariant — overrides via the `title` field in the request body win unconditionally. The provider is *not* overridable on fork by design (switching mid-branch would invalidate tool_call ids referenced earlier in the chain), but model and system prompt are because they're plain text the LLM treats per-turn. If a future US adds branch-id columns *to threads* (so a thread can carry multiple branches in-place rather than spawning new threads), revisit whether provider becomes per-branch — for now the "one provider per thread" invariant from US-279 is preserved.
- The `MessageTreeNode.Message *Message` pointer-embed is the right shape for SDK serialisers because it flattens the message fields onto the node JSON without a wrapper key. The alternative (named field `message: Message`) would force the SPA to write `node.message.id` everywhere — a worse UX. Document this in the SDK README when US-375 (UI tree browse) lands.
- AIP routes still don't need OpenAPI entries (per the US-373 / US-281 contract-test gate) because the contract test deps leave `AIPStore = nil`. If a future US wires a non-PG AIP store into the contract router, every AIP route will simultaneously need OpenAPI entries — plan for an "OpenAPI bulk add" PR at that point rather than incremental updates per US.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-375 - AIP Threads UI 树形浏览

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/api/aip.ts`: extended `AIPMessage` with `parentMessageId?: number | null` and `branchId?: string` so the SDK shape mirrors the US-374 wire format. Added `AIPMessageTreeNode` (extends `AIPMessage` with `children?: AIPMessageTreeNode[]`) and `ThreadTreeResponse` shapes plus a `getThreadTree(threadId)` helper that calls `GET /api/v2/aip/threads/{threadId}/tree`.
- `web/src/hooks/useAIPThreads.ts`: new `aipQueryKeys.tree(threadId)` and `useAIPThreadTree(threadId)` query hook. `useSendAIPMessage` now invalidates the tree key alongside messages so a fresh send refetches the new shape immediately.
- `web/src/components/threads/ThreadsPage.tsx`: refactored `ThreadConversationActive` into a two-column body. Left rail = new `ThreadTreePanel` rendering the parent-child forest from the tree query (recursive `ThreadTreeNode` with depth-driven indent, role badge, optional non-default `branchId` chip, message id, and content preview truncated to 48 chars). Right rail = the existing messages + composer scaffold but now driven by `branchMessages` (the active-branch slice) instead of the raw `messages` array. Tree-panel error / loading / empty placeholders use `data-testid="thread-tree-error"` / spinner / `thread-tree-empty`.
- Active-branch state: `activeBranchTipId` defaults to the latest message id when the messages list first loads (or thread changes). Clicking any tree node sets the tip; the messages pane re-filters to the parent chain ending at the clicked node. `computeBranchPath(messages, tipId)` walks `parentMessageId` from tip → root, terminating cleanly when a parent points outside the slice (orphan-tolerant). `hasParentLinks` is a fallback gate: if no message in the thread carries a parent id (legacy US-279 / US-284 fixtures) the filter is bypassed and the full message list renders, preserving backwards compatibility for pre-US-374 threads.
- Sending a new message promotes the tip to the new assistant message id so the active branch automatically extends post-send (mirrors the per-branch auto-link semantics on the backend).
- Thread switch clears `activeBranchTipId` so each conversation starts on its own latest leaf.

**Files changed:**
- `web/src/api/aip.ts` (added `parentMessageId` + `branchId` on `AIPMessage`; added `AIPMessageTreeNode` + `ThreadTreeResponse` + `getThreadTree`)
- `web/src/hooks/useAIPThreads.ts` (added `tree` query key + `useAIPThreadTree`; tree invalidation on send)
- `web/src/components/threads/ThreadsPage.tsx` (new `ThreadTreePanel` + `ThreadTreeNode` + `computeBranchPath`; two-column body layout; active-branch state + filter + post-send tip promotion)
- `web/src/components/threads/__tests__/ThreadsPageUS375.test.tsx` (new — 4 unit tests on `computeBranchPath` + 5 UI integration tests on tree rendering / default tip / branch switch on click / empty placeholder / branch_id badges)
- `prd.json` (mark US-375 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (zero TypeScript errors)
- `npx vitest run src/components/threads` PASS — 15/15 tests across the existing `ThreadsPage.test.tsx` (unchanged by the layout shift) and the new `ThreadsPageUS375.test.tsx`
- `npx vitest run` PASS — full SPA suite 906/906 across 124 files, no regressions
- `go build ./...` clean (sanity — this US is frontend-only but the API helper references types that match the Go wire shape)
- Browser verification deferred: dev-browser MCP tooling is not available in this iteration runner. The 5 UI integration tests exercise every acceptance criterion (tree panel renders all nodes, default tip = latest, click switches branch + filters messages, empty state when tree is empty, branch_id badge rendered for non-default branches). Recommend manual smoke against a live PG-backed thread once the AIP service is wired.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- Legacy threads pre-US-374 ship messages without `parent_message_id`, so a naïve `messages.filter(branchPathIds.has)` would yank every legacy message off-screen. The `hasParentLinks` gate (any message with a parent id present? then filter, else full list) preserves the existing UX for older fixtures while the new tree filtering kicks in cleanly for forked threads. Apply the same gate-on-presence pattern when extending other tree-aware UIs (e.g. branch picker for ontology, dataset transaction chain) — assume the *new* schema columns might be absent on warm rows for some time after migration and feature-gate on data shape rather than schema version.
- `computeBranchPath` MUST add to the path *after* the byId existence check, not before. The naïve "add then check" pattern pollutes the result with orphan parent ids when `ON DELETE SET NULL` on the FK leaves a dangling pointer (or the slice is filtered server-side and a parent ancestor isn't in the response). Test it with a parent id that points outside the slice as part of the unit suite — it's a quietly-wrong corner that would surface only as one extra ghost row in the conversation pane.
- The "active branch tip = latest message id" default is the lowest-friction first-paint behaviour for an LLM chat UX — the user sees the most recent turn. When a new message is sent, *promote the tip* to the assistant message id in the `onSuccess` of `useSendAIPMessage` so a refetch doesn't yank the visible chain back to a previous leaf. This mirrors the backend's `MemoryStore.AppendMessage` auto-link semantics (a new append extends whichever branch was active) and keeps client/server intuitions aligned.
- `useAIPThreadTree` *must* be invalidated alongside `aipQueryKeys.messages(threadId)` on send — otherwise the tree panel stays stale until the user re-mounts the page. A reasonable rule: any mutation that mutates messages should invalidate both the flat list AND the tree shape; encode it once in the hook so call sites stay one-line.
- The tree node UI uses inline padding-left (`paddingLeft: 8 + depth * 12`) rather than tailwind `pl-N` because depth is dynamic; tailwind's static class scanner would not pick up arbitrary computed values. Prefer the inline style for any future indentation that depends on a runtime depth count.
- `ThreadTreeNode` recurses through children rather than flattening the tree client-side because the backend already orders siblings by id asc inside `BuildMessageTree` (US-374 pkg/aip/handlers_fork.go). Doing the recursion client-side preserves that order and lets us reuse the same shape without a second sort.
- The `branchId` badge only renders when `branchId !== 'main'` so the default branch stays visually quiet. Once US-383 (Ontology Branch metadata) starts writing non-default branch ids into AIP messages, those will surface here automatically — no extra wiring needed.
- The two-column layout inside `ThreadConversationActive` collapsed the original `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` div into a wrapping `flex flex-1 overflow-hidden` parent so the tree panel can scroll independently of the messages pane. When adding more side rails (e.g. inspector, lineage), keep the message pane as the only `flex-1` child and rail-shape every other column with explicit widths — otherwise scroll containment leaks across rails.
- The conversation pane's `useEffect` for `scrollIntoView` now keys on `branchMessages` (not the raw `messages`) so switching branches triggers a scroll-to-bottom inside the new branch view rather than blindly scrolling on every refetch. This keeps the UX intuitive when the user clicks a tree node mid-thread.

## 2026-05-03 - US-376 - Cell-level Masking CEL 表达式引擎

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000087_cell_masks_cel_expression.{up,down}.sql` adds two columns to `cell_masks`: `expression TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (the optional CEL predicate) and `mask_strategy TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (the new uppercase REDACT|HASH|NULL|PARTIAL taxonomy). A partial index `idx_cell_masks_expression ON cell_masks(object_type_rid) WHERE expression <> ''` keeps the per-OT lookup cheap when CEL masks are sparse. The legacy `mask_rule` column stays as the back-compat surface; `mask_strategy` wins when both are set.
- New package `pkg/cellsec/celmask` (single-file `celmask.go`) compiles CEL expressions against the shared `celEnv` (built once at package init with `user: map<string, dyn>` + `row: map<string, dyn>` variables) and exposes `Compile(expression) (*Program, error)`, `(*Program).Eval(UserView, row map[string]any) (bool, error)`, and `Validate(expression) error`. Programs are immutable + safe for concurrent use; the runtime uses `cel.OptOptimize`. `UserView` carries `{ID, Email, Roles, Markings, Attributes}` and bridges to the CEL map shape via `AsMap()`. Compile rejects empty input (`ErrEmptyExpression`), enforces `bool` return, and surfaces parse / type-check / program-construction errors with the offending source quoted in the wrap.
- `pkg/masking/strategy.go` (new): adds the US-376 wire-format `MaskStrategy` enum + `IsKnownStrategy` / `NormalizeStrategy` (uppercases + trims; unknown → empty) / `StrategyFromRule` / `RuleFromStrategy` crosswalk + `ApplyMaskStrategy` (with the new `NULL` branch) + `ApplyStrategyTransforms` (mutates props in place by strategy). NULL collapses any non-nil value to nil; HASH/REDACT/PARTIAL reuse the existing helpers in `rules.go`.
- `pkg/cellsec/model.go`: `CellMask` gains `Expression string`, `MaskStrategy masking.MaskStrategy` (both `omitempty`). `EffectiveStrategy()` prefers the explicit MaskStrategy and falls back to `StrategyFromRule(MaskRule)` so single dispatch covers both old + new authoring shapes. `Validate()` now requires AT LEAST ONE of MaskRule/MaskStrategy (instead of MaskRule only), normalises MaskStrategy via `NormalizeStrategy`, validates Expression via `celmask.Validate` and trims it. New sentinels: `ErrUnknownMaskStrategy`, `ErrInvalidExpression`. `CellMaskUpdate` gains `MaskStrategy *MaskStrategy` and `Expression *string`.
- `pkg/cellsec/engine.go` rewritten to wrap each row in a `compiledCellMask{mask, program, compileErr}`. Reload pre-compiles every Expression at policy-load time so the hot path stays a flat map lookup + Eval. New `CompileForRow(ctx, user, otRID, pk, row map[string]any) (map[string]MaskStrategy, error)` is the US-376 entry point and evaluates CEL predicates against the (user, row) binding. Legacy `Compile(...)` keeps its `map[string]MaskRule` signature by translating the strategy back via `RuleFromStrategy` (NULL strategy → no MaskRule, dropped). **Fail-closed contract:** any CEL eval error or missing program surfaces as "mask applies" so a broken expression never opens a hole.
- `pkg/cellsec/store.go`: `MemoryStore.Update` round-trips the new `MaskStrategy` and `Expression` patch fields.
- `pkg/cellsec/handlers.go`: `CreateRequest` gains `MaskStrategy` and `Expression` (both `omitempty`); Create stamps + normalises both before Validate. The PATCH handler validates `MaskStrategy` via `NormalizeStrategy` and `Expression` via `celmask.Validate` before forwarding the update so an invalid CEL predicate is rejected at the API boundary instead of poisoning the cache at next Reload.
- `cmd/server/cell_mask_store.go`: PG store INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT/SCAN paths now round-trip `mask_strategy` + `expression`. `COALESCE(mask_strategy, '')` + `COALESCE(expression, '')` keep legacy rows (pre-migration) reading cleanly.
- `pkg/oss/service_impl.go::applyCellMasking`: replaced the `Compile(...)` + `masking.ApplyTransforms(...)` pair with `CompileForRow(ctx, user, otRID, pk, o.Properties)` + `masking.ApplyStrategyTransforms(o.Properties, transforms)` so the wire path now honours CEL predicates (with the row's properties as the binding) AND the new NULL strategy.
- `go.mod` / `go.sum`: added `github.com/google/cel-go v0.28.0` (and indirects `cel.dev/expr v0.25.1`, `github.com/antlr4-go/antlr/v4 v4.13.1`, `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3 v3.0.4`). `go mod tidy` is clean.

**Files changed:**

  - `TestUS376_PerfGate_1000Rows_10Columns_Under50ms` — **PRD perf gate**: 1000 rows × 10 cols (5 distinct CEL expressions cycled) finished in **10.97 ms** (≥4.5× headroom under the 50 ms budget)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD example `user.markings has 'PII' || row.country = 'CN'` is *descriptive intent*, not literal CEL — `has` in CEL is the field-presence macro `has(e.f)`, not infix list membership; equality is `==` not `=`. The accepted CEL shape is `("PII" in user.markings) || row.country == "CN"`. Document this in any SDK / admin-UI surface that exposes the editor: the PRD wording will trip up authors who type it verbatim.
- The "fire direction" of the predicate matters and isn't self-evident: the convention chosen here is **TRUE → mask applies** (the expression is the trigger condition, not an allow-list). This matches Foundry's "mask predicates" model and keeps the AppliesTo path semantically distinct (AppliesTo is the allow-list — callers in it see clear). If a future US wants `allow:` style expressions, add a sibling field rather than overloading the existing one.
- **Fail-closed eval is non-negotiable.** Any CEL runtime error (missing field on strict row, type mismatch in `==`, etc.) MUST surface as "mask applies" otherwise a malformed expression authored by a non-expert opens a hole in PII enforcement. The fail-closed contract is enforced inside `evaluateProgram` and exercised by `TestUS376_BrokenExpressionFailsClosed`. If you add a new code path that calls `program.Eval(...)` outside the engine, replicate the fail-closed branch.
- CEL programs are **safe for concurrent use** — `cel.Program.Eval` does not mutate the program. Pre-compiling at `Engine.Reload` time and storing one `*celmask.Program` per CellMask in `compiledCellMask` is the right shape; do NOT compile per request (the env build + parse + type-check + lower is ~ms-scale and dwarfs Eval's microseconds).
- The PG `mask_rule` column stays as the back-compat surface alongside the new `mask_strategy`. The migration deliberately does not transform existing rows: `mask_rule = 'hash' / mask_strategy = ''` on a legacy row reads cleanly because `EffectiveStrategy()` falls back to `StrategyFromRule(MaskRule)`. No backfill step needed; a future US-433 (Masking 真实算法) can treat the strategy column as canonical without breaking US-258 callers.
- `cell-go v0.28.0` pulls in `cel.dev/expr`, `github.com/antlr4-go/antlr/v4`, and (transitively) `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` — all three are non-trivial deps. `go mod tidy` after `go get` is mandatory or `go build` errors with "missing go.sum entry for module providing package …". Add `go mod tidy` to the post-add checklist when introducing any cel-go style framework that pulls heavyweight indirects.
- The `applyCellMasking` change in `pkg/oss/service_impl.go` is the ONLY oss-side wiring needed because it already runs after `applyColumnMasking` at every read path (`GetObject`, `ListObjects`, `SearchObjects`, ObjectSet `load`). No new entry-point hook required — but new read APIs (e.g., a future bulk-export path) MUST call this helper or CEL masks won't apply.
- For the PG store's COALESCE pattern on new columns: use `COALESCE(mask_strategy, '')` instead of relying on the migration's `DEFAULT ''`, because Go's nullable scan would still error on a NULL row if a future migration drops the default. Cheap defensive read; matches the existing `pkg/oms` PG-row pattern.

## 2026-05-03 - US-377 - Lineage column-level

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000088_lineage_column_edges.{up,down}.sql` adds the `lineage_column_edges` table (id BIGSERIAL PK, binding_rid, src_dataset_rid, src_column, dst_object_type_rid, dst_property_rid, dst_property_api_name, ts) plus three indexes: (dst_property_rid) for the property-lookup hot path, (src_dataset_rid, src_column) for reverse-impact lookups, (binding_rid) for cascading deletes. Replace-by-binding is the canonical write path (one binding owns N edges); the table is *not* append-only — a binding update yields a clean overwrite.
- `pkg/oms/column_lineage.go` (new): `ColumnLineageEdge` struct, `ColumnLineageStore` interface (Replace/Delete/ListUpstream/ListDownstream — narrow surface mirroring `LineageStore`), and the `DeriveColumnLineageEdges(binding, properties)` helper. The helper accepts BOTH wire shapes for `column_mapping` JSON: flat object `{propertyApiName: column}` (Foundry default) AND array form `[{property, column}]` (with `p`/`c` short-key alternates). Output is sorted lexicographically by (api_name, src_column) so replace round-trips deterministically.
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository_column_lineage.go` (new): `PGRepository` implementation. `ReplaceColumnLineageForBinding` runs DELETE + N×INSERT inside a single `BeginTx` so concurrent reads never observe a partial replacement. Each inserted row's ID + Timestamp are back-filled. The store stamps `BindingRID` from the explicit parameter so callers can't accidentally mix owners. `ListUpstreamColumnLineageForProperty` / `ListDownstreamColumnLineageForDatasetColumn` return newest-first (ts DESC, id DESC) with a default cap of 200 mirroring `defaultLineageListLimit`.
- `pkg/oms/column_lineage_memory.go` (new): `MemoryColumnLineageStore` for tests + degraded-mode bootstraps. Same contract as the PG store; clock is swappable via `WithClock(...)` so deterministic-ordering tests can pin `time.Now`.
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go`: `OMSHandler` gains a `columnLineageStore ColumnLineageStore` field + `SetColumnLineageStore(s)` / `ColumnLineageStore() ColumnLineageStore` setters mirroring the existing optional-store pattern. Nil store = derivation hook is a no-op (degraded-mode contract).
- `pkg/oms/admin_handlers.go`: `CreateDatasourceBinding`, `UpdateDatasourceBinding`, `DeleteDatasourceBinding` now invoke a private `deriveColumnLineageOnBindingChange(ctx, db)` helper. Create/Update load the ObjectType's properties via `ListProperties`, run `DeriveColumnLineageEdges`, and atomically `ReplaceColumnLineageForBinding`. Delete cascades by calling `DeleteColumnLineageForBinding`. The hook is best-effort: lineage view is a derived index, so a property-list or store failure does NOT roll back the binding write — the next successful update re-derives.
- `pkg/lineage/handler.go`: `Handler` gains `columnStore oms.ColumnLineageStore` + `SetColumnLineageStore(s)`. `RegisterRoutes` now mounts two new endpoints alongside `/api/v2/objects/{rid}/lineage`: `GET /api/v2/lineage/property/{rid}` (upstream column lineage for a property) + `GET /api/v2/lineage/dataset-columns/impact?dataset=...&column=...` (reverse-impact analysis). Both return 404 `ColumnLineageNotConfigured` when the store is nil so the contract stays discoverable in degraded mode. Wire shapes: `PropertyLineageResponse{propertyRid, upstream[{bindingRid, srcDatasetRid, srcColumn, timestamp}], truncated}` and `DatasetColumnImpactResponse{datasetRid, column, impacted[{bindingRid, dstObjectTypeRid, dstPropertyRid, dstPropertyApiName, timestamp}], truncated}`. Per-call cap = 200; `truncated=true` when the listing hits the cap.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: new `ServerDeps.ColumnLineageStore oms.ColumnLineageStore` field wired from the uncached `*PGRepository` in the PG bootstrap (same pattern as `LineageStore`). The lineage handler call gets `lineageHandler.SetColumnLineageStore(deps.ColumnLineageStore)` before `RegisterRoutes`. The OMSHandler builder gets a parallel `omsHandler.SetColumnLineageStore(deps.ColumnLineageStore)` so the binding-write hook fires when the store is wired.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: full OpenAPI 3 entries for both new GET routes (request/response shapes, 200/400/401/404/500 responses, tags=[Lineage]). `cmd/server/contract_test.go::TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` enforces the chi-walk → openapi.paths cross-reference, so the embedded copy was synced byte-for-byte.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000088_lineage_column_edges.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000088_lineage_column_edges.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/column_lineage.go` (new — interface, model, derivation helper)

- 4/4 binding-hook integration tests in `pkg/oms/us377_binding_hook_test.go` pass: `TestUS377_BindingHook_CreateProducesColumnEdges`, `TestUS377_BindingHook_UpdateReplacesEdges` (PRD: re-derive on update; dropped properties yield zero edges), `TestUS377_BindingHook_DeleteCascadesEdges`, `TestUS377_BindingHook_StoreUnwiredIsHarmless` (Create succeeds in degraded mode without panic).
- 6/6 HTTP handler tests in `pkg/lineage/us377_handler_test.go` pass: `TestUS377_GetPropertyLineage_HappyPath` (PRD route 1), `TestUS377_GetPropertyLineage_StoreUnwired_Returns404`, `TestUS377_GetPropertyLineage_EmptyResults` (Upstream is non-nil empty slice for SDK consumers), `TestUS377_GetDatasetColumnImpact_HappyPath` (PRD route 2: reverse-impact), `TestUS377_GetDatasetColumnImpact_MissingParams`, `TestUS377_GetDatasetColumnImpact_StoreUnwired_Returns404`.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `column_mapping` JSON has *no enforced wire shape* anywhere in the codebase — `models.go` types it as `json.RawMessage`, the SPA types it as `unknown`. The PRD wording ("dst_property_rid (within object type)") implies a property-API-name-keyed mapping, but neither the docs nor any existing test fixtures pin a shape. I accepted BOTH the flat-object form AND the array form so future SDK callers that pick either convention work transparently. If a future US wants to lock the shape, do it in the binding *write* validator (admin_handlers.go) — `DeriveColumnLineageEdges` is the canonical parser the validator should call.
- Properties referenced in the mapping but absent from `properties` are silently skipped, NOT rejected. Rationale: the binding `column_mapping` is allowed to drift ahead of (or behind) the property schema during multi-step refactors. A binding write that names a not-yet-created property simply emits no edge for it; the next refresh after the property lands will pick it up. The same direction-tolerance lives in `LineageStore` (an upstream RID that doesn't exist is just a string).
- The admin routes for `datasource_bindings` were stripped in US-006 (`cmd/server/wire_us006_test.go::134-138` asserts the routes return 404). The handler methods on `OMSHandler` are still present and still the canonical write surface — production deployments today have no caller, but tests can still exercise the hook by mounting the handlers on a chi router directly. The store wiring in `cmd/server/main.go` is therefore "ready when admin routes return" and consumes zero PG IO until then.
- For the US-377 binding-hook integration tests I had to wrap the existing `mockRepo` (which stubs `CreateDatasourceBinding`/`UpdateDatasourceBinding` as no-ops) with a `us377BindingRepo` overlay that map-backs the bindings by RID. The base mockRepo's stubs would otherwise yield a 404 on the Update path. Pattern: **embed mockRepo, override the methods that need real persistence behaviour, leave the rest as base stubs**. Don't modify the base mockRepo struct — many other tests rely on the stub behaviour.
- `Replace`-by-binding is the right write semantics for the column-edge graph (NOT append-only like `lineage_edges`). The reason: a binding update can DROP a property from the mapping, and the lineage view must reflect that drop synchronously. An append-only history would force every reader to filter by "latest binding revision", which is both more complex AND racier. The PG implementation handles atomicity via `BeginTx`/`Commit` so concurrent readers never see a partial replace.
- Output sorting in `DeriveColumnLineageEdges` is deterministic (lexicographic by api_name, then src_column) on purpose. The replacement loop in `ReplaceColumnLineageForBinding` allocates new ids in input order; with deterministic input ordering, two binding writes with identical mapping payloads produce identical edge sets (modulo ts), which is the cheapest possible change-detection signal for downstream tools (e.g. an automation rule that fires when "binding lineage actually changed").
- The contract-test enforcement (`TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented`) hits *every* chi route — even routes mounted under `r.Group(func(api chi.Router))` with auth middleware. Both new lineage routes had to land in `api/openapi.yaml` AND the byte-identical `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`. The PG-gated AIP routes pattern from US-373 / US-374 (which skip OpenAPI because their store is nil in the contract test) does NOT apply here — the lineage handler is mounted unconditionally, and the contract test sees the routes.
- `defaultColumnLineageListLimit = 200` mirrors `defaultLineageListLimit`; `columnPageLimit = 200` mirrors `pageLimit`. Keep these in sync — the operational mental model "200 rows per call" is the same for both lineage surfaces. If a future US widens this to support paginated cursors, do so for both at once.
- Wiring location for the OMSHandler setter (`omsHandler.SetColumnLineageStore`) sits right next to `SetNotificationBulkStore` and `SetFunctionExecutionStore` — the grouping is "optional stores wired only when the corresponding `deps.X != nil`". The lineage handler setter (`lineageHandler.SetColumnLineageStore`) sits *before* `lineageHandler.RegisterRoutes` — the order matters because `SetColumnLineageStore` is a plain field assignment and not a route-mounting call.

## 2026-05-03 - US-378 - Pipeline 增量构建 + schema evolution

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000089_pipeline_incremental.{up,down}.sql` adds three columns: `pipelines.mode TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (with CHECK constraint `('', 'FULL', 'APPEND')`), `pipelines.last_known_schema JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb` (the most-recent observed source schema for diff comparison), and `pipeline_runs.last_committed_offset BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (the high-water mark the run advanced the pipeline to).
- `pkg/pipeline/pipeline.go`: added `ModeFull`/`ModeAppend` constants + `IsKnownMode` predicate + `SchemaField{Name, Type}` struct. `Pipeline` extended with `Mode string` and `LastKnownSchema []SchemaField`; `PipelineUpdate` mirror with pointer fields. `Validate()` rejects unknown modes. `ClonePipeline` deep-copies `LastKnownSchema` via `cloneSchemaFields`.
- `pkg/pipeline/runs.go`: `PipelineRun` extended with `LastCommittedOffset int64`. New `MemoryStore.LatestCommittedOffset(ctx, pipelineID)` returns the max LastCommittedOffset across **successful** runs (failed/canceled runs do NOT advance the watermark — half-applied progress would corrupt downstream state on the next APPEND). `Store` interface widened with the new method.
- `pkg/pipeline/schema_evolution.go` (new): `SchemaDiff{AddedColumns, DroppedColumns, ConflictedColumns, MismatchedPriorTypes}` + `IsBreaking()` predicate. `DiffSchemas(prior, current)` compares case-sensitive on Name and case-insensitive+trimmed on Type (so `Long` / `long` / ` LONG ` are equivalent — schema sources frequently disagree on casing without semantic difference). `ResolveSchemaEvolution(prior, current)` returns `SchemaEvolutionResolution{MergedSchema, AddedColumns, Diff}` on success or `ErrSchemaBreakingChange` (with formatted dropped+conflict context) on rejection. `MergeSchema(prior, current)` preserves prior column order (Bleve facet positions don't shuffle on every run) and appends new columns in observed order.
- `pkg/pipeline/incremental.go` (new): `IncrementalRow{Offset int64, Data map[string]any}` is the wire shape. `IncrementalSource{Schema(ctx), ReadAfter(ctx, after)}` is the narrow connector contract — Schema returns the current source columns (used for the diff BEFORE any row is read so a breaking change short-circuits the run); ReadAfter returns rows with Offset > after. `IncrementalSink{ApplyAddedColumns(ctx, added), WriteRows(ctx, rows)}` is the optional downstream — pass nil for a "preview" run that resolves the diff + pulls the row delta without persisting. `RunIncremental(ctx, opts)` is the engine: schema → diff → readAfter → defensive offset filter → applyAddedColumns → writeRows → IncrementalRunResult{ProcessedRows, NewLastCommittedOffset, MergedSchema, AddedColumns}. **Critical contract:** `ResolveSchemaEvolution` runs BEFORE any source read; the sink-side schema-extension call runs BEFORE WriteRows so partial writes never land on a stale index shape.
- `pkg/apierror/error.go`: new `NewPipelineBreakingChange` constructor (wire code `WEAVE_PIPELINE_BREAKING_CHANGE`, HTTP 422). Mirrors the US-371 / US-370 named-code constructors. Tests cover the constructor at `pkg/apierror/error_test.go::TestNewPipelineBreakingChange_Code`.
- `cmd/server/pipeline_store.go`: PG store INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT/SCAN paths now round-trip `mode` + `last_known_schema` (via `coalesceSchemaFields`) on the pipeline row AND `last_committed_offset` on the run row. New `pgPipelineStore.LatestCommittedOffset(ctx, pipelineID)` runs `SELECT MAX(COALESCE(last_committed_offset, 0)) FROM pipeline_runs WHERE pipeline_id=$1 AND status='success'`. `COALESCE(...)` defaults keep legacy rows (pre-migration) reading cleanly.
- `pkg/pipeline/store.go::MemoryStore.UpdatePipeline`: round-trips Mode + LastKnownSchema via the new pointer fields.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000089_pipeline_incremental.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000089_pipeline_incremental.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/pipeline/pipeline.go` (Mode constants + SchemaField + Pipeline/PipelineUpdate fields + Validate mode check + ClonePipeline schema deep-copy)
- `pkg/pipeline/runs.go` (PipelineRun.LastCommittedOffset + MemoryStore.LatestCommittedOffset)
- `pkg/pipeline/store.go` (Store interface gains LatestCommittedOffset; MemoryStore.UpdatePipeline round-trips Mode + LastKnownSchema)
- `pkg/pipeline/schema_evolution.go` (new — SchemaDiff + DiffSchemas + MergeSchema + ResolveSchemaEvolution + ErrSchemaBreakingChange)
