**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The merge endpoint's 409 body is the project's first non-`ApiError`-shaped error response. The standard `request()` helper would discard the `conflicts` / `unresolved` arrays because it parses errors into `ApiRequestError` (which only carries `errorCode` / `errorName` / `parameters`). Solution: bypass `request()` for the merge call and read the JSON body directly, branching on `status === 409 && parsed.errorCode === 'MERGE_CONFLICT'` to throw a typed `MergeBranchConflictError` carrying the structured payload. Other failures still flow through `ApiRequestError` so call sites that don't care about the conflict shape behave identically. Pattern: when an endpoint returns a structured error envelope that doesn't match the standard `ApiError` shape, define a typed `Error` subclass per endpoint and short-circuit the response parsing for it; don't try to widen `ApiRequestError` because that would force every consumer to handle the new fields.
- React-i18next `t()` returns the raw key (e.g. `"branchReconcile.merging"`) when the i18n instance is not initialised in tests. The existing test setup (`web/src/test/setup.ts`) does NOT bootstrap i18n, so RTL tests must NOT assert on translated strings — assert on `data-testid` markers, props (`disabled`), or the dynamic data values that flow through the component (e.g. branch ids, `apiName` strings). The BranchPicker test follows the same convention and is the canonical reference. If a story genuinely needs translated copy under test, set up an `i18next.init({lng:'en', resources})` in a per-test wrapper rather than mutating the shared setup file.
- For test data attributes that include colons (e.g. `data-testid="reconcile-conflict-${resolutionKey}"` where the key is `actionType:updateOrder`), `screen.getByTestId('reconcile-conflict-actionType:updateOrder')` works directly — RTL's testid lookup uses string equality, not CSS selector parsing, so colons are not interpreted. This is the right shape because `resolutionKey` is the conflict's stable identity (`<entityType>:<apiName>`) and matching it 1:1 in the testid lets E2E tests select an exact conflict row without index gymnastics.
- The reconcile page deliberately re-renders the conflict list using `serverConflicts` (set from the 409 body) rather than re-fetching the diff. This is faster (no extra round trip), keeps the user's existing radio selections intact for the still-conflicting subset, and matches the backend's behaviour of computing conflicts on every merge call (so a re-fetch of `postBranchDiff` would return the same list anyway). The `useMergeBranch` mutation also invalidates the `branchDiffPost` query key on the *successful* path so a navigation-back later reflects the merged state.
- The `mergeBranch` helper in `ontologies.ts` re-exports the static `withActiveBranch` and `ApiRequestError` from `./client` rather than dynamic-importing them. Dynamic imports inside an async handler work but make TypeScript inference and test mocking harder. Static imports at the top of `ontologies.ts` are the canonical pattern and match what every other API helper in this file does.
- Backend `MergeBranch` rejects branches with `status != "open"` (409 BranchNotOpen) BEFORE inspecting the body, so a closed-branch user hitting "Merge" surfaces as an `ApiRequestError` with `errorCode: "BranchNotOpen"`. The page also pre-disables the merge button when `branch.status !== 'open'` so the user gets immediate visual feedback instead of relying on the round-trip 409. Keep both guards: client-side disable for UX, server-side reject for correctness.
- The "Open Reconcile UI" link from `BranchDiffPage` is the discoverability bridge — without it users would have no way to find the new surface unless they typed the URL. Adding the link in the header (rather than the body) keeps it visible regardless of how many diff entries the page renders. When introducing a new sibling page that supersedes an existing read-only surface, mirror this pattern: keep the legacy page operational, add a link from its header to the new page, and note both in the route table.

## 2026-05-03 - US-388 - Dataset Transaction Chain (rollback)

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.{up,down}.sql` adds the rollback audit overlay to `dataset_transactions`: nullable `rolled_back_at TIMESTAMPTZ` + `rolled_back_to_tx_id TEXT`, plus a partial index `idx_dataset_transactions_rolled_back ON (ontology_api_name, rolled_back_at) WHERE rolled_back_at IS NOT NULL` so a "show me undone txs" lookup stays cheap. Loose TEXT (no FK) on `rolled_back_to_tx_id` because the rollback bookkeeping row legitimately self-points to its own TxID and an FK would create an awkward "constraint deferred to commit" pattern.
- `pkg/oms/dataset_transaction.go`:
  - `DatasetTransaction` gains `RolledBackAt time.Time` + `RolledBackToTxID string` (both `omitempty`). Zero-value time means "not rolled back" — matches the SQL NULL semantics without an extra `*time.Time` indirection.
  - New `AffectedKey{ObjectTypeRID, PrimaryKey}` struct surfaces the per-PK replay set the rollback handler needs.
  - `DatasetTransactionStore` interface widened with `ListAfterCommittedAt(ctx, ontologyAPIName, after)` (oldest-first chain entries strictly newer than `after`) and `MarkRolledBack(ctx, txID, rolledBackToTxID, rolledBackAt)` (idempotent overwrite).
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository_dataset_transaction.go`:
  - All five existing read methods (`GetDatasetTransaction`, `LatestForOntology`, `ListByOntology`) round-trip the new `rolled_back_at` / `rolled_back_to_tx_id` columns via `*time.Time` / `*string` scan dests — same pattern as `parent_tx_id` / `user_id`.
  - New `ListAfterCommittedAt` selects rows with `committed_at > $2` ordered ASC for chronological replay; rolled-back rows are still surfaced (caller's choice whether to skip).
  - New `ListAffectedKeysSince` joins `object_history` to `object_types` so the affected-keys lookup is scoped by `ontology_rid`. `DISTINCT (object_type_rid, primary_key)` so a PK touched by N batches surfaces once.
  - New `MarkRolledBack` rejects an empty `txID` / zero `rolledBackAt` up front, then `UPDATE` with `nilIfEmpty(rolledBackToTxID)` so a self-pointing bookkeeping row is still expressible via a non-empty string. Returns `ErrNotFound` on zero rows-affected.
- `cmd/server/dataset_rollback_handler.go` (new):
  - `datasetTransactionWriter` interface composes `datasetTransactionLister` (the existing read-only contract from `dataset_transaction_handler.go`) with `RecordDatasetTransaction` + `ListAfterCommittedAt` + `MarkRolledBack`. Same shape pattern as `oms.DatasetTransactionStore` minus the funnel consumer's recorder overlap so a degraded-mode test router can plug a fake.
  - `datasetRollbackOntologyRepo` is the OMS subset (`GetOntology` + `GetObjectType`) used for URL → ontology resolution AND per-PK replay's ObjectType lookup.
  - `datasetRollbackAffectedStore` (`ListAffectedKeysSince`) and `datasetRollbackSnapshot` (`SnapshotObjectsAt`) are the two replay-side hooks; nil any of them and the rollback degrades to a metadata-only audit overlay.
  - `indexWriter` is the `*index.Manager` subset (`IndexDocument`/`DeleteDocument`) the replay path mutates.
  - `CreateTransaction` (`POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions`): looks up ontology → reads chain head via `LatestForOntology` → mints a `tx-<32-hex>` id via `crypto/rand` (16 bytes = 128 bits) → records with `parent_tx_id = chain head`. Optional body `{userId?, editsCount?}` with `DisallowUnknownFields`. Returns `201` JSON of the freshly stamped row. Empty chain → `parentTxId` blank.
  - `Rollback` (`POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=tx-...`): the AC chokepoint. (1) validate `?to=` → starts with `tx-` (`InvalidRollbackTarget` 400 if not), (2) lookup ontology + target tx, reject `RollbackTargetWrongOntology` cross-ontology, `RollbackTargetNotFound` 404 missing, (3) `ListAfterCommittedAt` newer txs, (4) replay each `(objectTypeRID, primaryKey)` against `SnapshotObjectsAt(targetTx.CommittedAt)` — restored if snapshot row exists, deleted from the live Bleve index otherwise, (5) `MarkRolledBack` each newer tx, (6) record a fresh "rollback" tx whose `parent_tx_id = target.TxID` AND `rolled_back_to_tx_id = target.TxID` (self-pointer audit) so the chain head moves coherently.
  - Replay degrades cleanly: nil affected/history/index → `RestoredObjects=0, DeletedObjects=0` but the audit overlay still flips and the bookkeeping row still lands.
- `cmd/server/main.go`:
  - `ServerDeps` gains `DatasetAffectedStore datasetRollbackAffectedStore` for the affected-keys lookup. Wired off the uncached `*PGRepository` in the PG bootstrap (the `ListAffectedKeysSince` method joins `object_history`, not on the cached metadata Repository).
  - Routes `POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions` + `POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback` mounted unconditionally so OpenAPI / contract tests always discover them. The `datasetTxWriter` is acquired via type assertion on `deps.DatasetTransactionStore` (the same `*PGRepository`) so a degraded-mode router that left it unset still gets `DatasetRollbackUnavailable` 400 cleanly.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: documented both new POST routes (`createDatasetTransaction` + `rollbackDataset`) including the `?to=` query, `RollbackTargetNotFound` / `RollbackTargetWrongOntology` / `DatasetRollbackUnavailable` error envelopes, and the response wire shape (`rolledBackTxIds`, `restoredObjects`, `deletedObjects`, `newTransaction`, `targetTx`). Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/dataset_transaction.go` (RolledBackAt/RolledBackToTxID + AffectedKey + 2 store-interface methods)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across the full ~70-package surface (cached on second run; uncached cmd/server completes in 2.6s)

  - `TestRollback_DegradedNoReplayInfra` — nil affected/history/index → metadata-only rollback (counts=0, audit overlay flipped, bookkeeping row stamped)
  - `TestRollback_NoNewerTxs_NoOp` — target IS chain head → empty RolledBackTxIDs but bookkeeping row still recorded

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The acceptance criterion has THREE parts that look like one feature: (a) `dataset_transactions` table with `parent_tx_id` (already built in US-379, migration 000090), (b) `POST /transactions` for explicit checkpoints, (c) `POST /rollback?to=tx-...` for the actual time-travel effect. The first part is reused as-is; the second is an empty-edits checkpoint stamper; the third is the surgical part. Don't conflate them — the rollback is the only one that touches user-visible state, the checkpoint is metadata-only.
- The rollback's "write isolation" semantics (PRD: "写入按 tx 隔离") translate to: every `POST /rollback` records a fresh bookkeeping tx whose `parent_tx_id = target.TxID`, NOT the prior chain head. This intentionally branches the chain visually — a future `?asOf=tx-...` read past the rollback resolves correctly because the rollback's CommittedAt > target's CommittedAt and `committed_at`-ordered scans still find the right snapshot. The audit overlay (`rolled_back_at` / `rolled_back_to_tx_id` on the abandoned txs) lets a future history view render the "deleted" branch with strikethrough rather than dropping it.
- Self-pointer convention: the bookkeeping row sets `rolled_back_to_tx_id = target.TxID` (NOT its own TxID). This makes the audit semantics clean — every row with a non-NULL `rolled_back_to_tx_id` either (a) was rolled back (its own state is stale, points at the rollback target), or (b) IS a rollback bookkeeping row (its state is canonical, points at the rollback target). The schema doesn't disambiguate the two cases — the join with `parent_tx_id` makes (b) detectable (rollback rows have parent = the same target).
- Replay degrades, marks don't. The handler returns 200 even with nil affected/history/index — the chain audit overlay always flips, but `RestoredObjects` / `DeletedObjects` stay 0. This matches the PRD's "rollback is a metadata-first operation" semantics: a system without a replay infrastructure (e.g. a fresh Bleve cluster being rebuilt) should still be able to flip the chain. Conversely, a `MarkRolledBack` failure DOES abort the handler — a half-marked chain is genuinely incoherent and would surface as a stale `?asOf=tx-...` read returning the rolled-back state.
- The replay walks `affectedStore` keys, NOT `ListAfterCommittedAt`'s tx list. The two views differ on purpose: the tx list is the AUDIT axis ("which txs to mark"); the affected-keys list is the OBJECT axis ("which docs to replay"). A single tx can touch hundreds of PKs; conversely a long sequence of compensating edits can re-touch the same PK across N txs but the replay only needs the latest snapshot at target's CommittedAt — one re-index per affected (objectType, pk) is sufficient.
- Bucketing affected keys by `ObjectTypeRID` BEFORE iterating cuts the snapshot fetches from O(affected_keys) to O(distinct_object_types). For a typical rollback that touches N rows of M ObjectTypes (N >> M), this is the difference between minutes of round-trips and milliseconds. Done by `byOT := make(map[string][]string)` in `replayObjects`.
- `crypto/rand` for the tx id (16 bytes = 128 bits) is overkill for a single-machine deployment but matches the rest of the project's RID minting and avoids the time-precision ambiguity of `tx-20260503T...`. The fallback to a time-based id when `crypto/rand` errors is a defensive hedge that keeps the handler making forward progress on platforms where `getrandom(2)` is mis-configured.
- The `datasetTransactionWriter` interface composes `datasetTransactionLister` rather than re-declaring the read methods. This means a future story that adds a new read method to the lister (e.g. `ListByUser` for an audit drill-down) automatically picks it up in the writer surface and no per-method ceremony is needed in the rollback handler. Same composition pattern as `oms.DatasetTransactionStore` extending a hypothetical reader interface.
- `nilIfEmpty(rolledBackToTxID)` in the `MarkRolledBack` UPDATE is the canonical PG nullability shim: empty Go string → SQL NULL, non-empty string → TEXT value. Used throughout the repo for `parent_tx_id` / `user_id` / etc. — keep using it for any new nullable column to avoid the "stored empty string vs NULL" ambiguity that breaks `WHERE col IS NULL` queries.
- The cmd/server `historySnapshotStore` interface (in `history_snapshot_provider.go`) and the new `datasetRollbackSnapshot` interface (in `dataset_rollback_handler.go`) have IDENTICAL method shapes — both expect `SnapshotObjectsAt(ctx, objectTypeRID, asOf) ([]oms.LatestObjectState, error)`. They're declared independently so each consumer can stub its own minimal subset; `*PGRepository` satisfies both. Don't try to merge them into one shared interface — the duplication is intentional and keeps test fakes narrow.
- The `recordingIndex` test fake in `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go` captures Index/Delete calls into nested maps keyed by scoped key + PK so tests can assert exactly what the rollback wrote. This is the canonical pattern for asserting on "what side effects landed on the index manager" — same shape used by other index-touching tests in the repo. The `index.ScopedKey(ontologyAPIName, objectTypeAPIName)` formatter is the single source of truth for the `{ontology}__{objectType}` key shape — never hardcode the separator.
---

## 2026-05-03 - US-389 - Branch on Function (Goja)

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::CreateFunctionRequest` gains an optional `BranchID string \`json:"branchId,omitempty"\`` body field. The publish handler reads the branch in this priority order: `?branch=` query (authoritative, mirrors the read-side `WithBranchScope` stamping convention from US-384) → `req.BranchID` (legacy/SDK fallback) → `NormalizeBranchID("") → "main"`. The resolved branch is stamped onto `fn.BranchID` BEFORE `fn.Validate()` so a future schema constraint on the field surfaces cleanly, and BEFORE `detectFunctionCallCycle` so the cycle scan walks the branch's overlay (a branch publish that adds A→B should resolve B against the branch first, falling back to main).
- `cycleCtx := WithBranchScope(r.Context(), branchID)` is the chokepoint for branch-aware cycle detection. The repository overlay (`functionCallGraphRepoOverlay`) honours the ctx because the underlying `repo.GetFunctionByName` already pulls branch from `BranchScopeFromContext` (US-384). No changes were needed to the call-graph machinery itself — wiring the ctx is sufficient.
- 409 conflict envelope on `ErrDuplicate` now carries `branchId` alongside `name` and `version` so SDK callers can render "function 'compute' v1.0.0 already exists on branch 'feature-x'" instead of the ambiguous "already exists in this ontology". Required because the (ontology, name, version, branch_id) UNIQUE constraint widened in US-384 means duplicates are now branch-scoped, and a clear error message is the difference between a 5-second fix and a 5-minute hunt.
- `pkg/oms/mock_functions_test.go::CreateFunction` uniqueness key now includes `BranchID` so the in-memory mock matches the PG UNIQUE constraint shape. `GetFunctionByName`, `GetFunctionByNameVersion`, `ListFunctions`, and `ListFunctionVersionsByName` are now branch-aware — they consult `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` and route to branch-specific rows when present, falling back to main otherwise. New `mockPreferBranchFunctions` / `mockPreferBranchFunctionsByName` mirror the `pkg/oms.preferBranchFunctions` / `preferBranchFunctionsByName` helpers so the in-memory test harness reproduces the production overlay/inheritance semantics. This is what lets handler-level unit tests verify branch routing through the full request → handler → repo chain.
- `api/openapi.yaml` / `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: documented the new `?branch=` query parameter and `branchId` body field on the `createFunction` operation; updated the version description to note the new (ontology, name, version, branchId) UNIQUE shape. Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

- `diff api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` — empty (contract test gate clean)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- US-384 already shipped the persistence + read-side branch dispatch for Functions (column, UNIQUE constraint widening, `BranchScopeFromContext`-aware repo methods, ExecuteFunction stamping `?branch=` onto the request context). US-389 closed the publish-side gap: until this story, the `BranchID` field on the wire model was effectively read-only because `CreateFunction` never populated it from the request, so every newly-published row landed on main regardless. The fix was small-scoped — request struct + handler resolution + cycle ctx + 409 envelope — exactly because the storage/read story was already done. Pattern: when a feature splits into "branch-aware reads" and "branch-aware writes" stories, expect the writes story to be the much smaller one if reads landed first.
- The `?branch=` query vs `branchId` body precedence rule is "query wins" because the read path uses query exclusively (`r.URL.Query().Get("branch")` → `WithBranchScope`). Honouring both inputs with query taking precedence keeps the wire shape symmetric across publish and read while still letting SDK builders that can't easily mutate the URL pass the branch in the body. Don't surface this ambiguity as a 400 — that would break SDKs that do both for belt-and-braces and is a footgun for no real benefit.
- `WithBranchScope(r.Context(), branchID)` MUST wrap the cycle-detection call site even though the ctx is not threaded into the persistence call below it. Reason: `detectFunctionCallCycle` walks the call graph through `repo.GetFunctionByName` and `GetFunctionByNameVersion`, both of which read `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)`. Without the wrap, a branch publish whose new code calls `weave.callFunction("helper")` would resolve `helper` against main instead of the branch's overlay, and a cycle that exists on the branch overlay (A→B → branch publish B' → B→A) could slip through validation. The persistence call below it doesn't need the same ctx because the row's own `BranchID` field is the source of truth at INSERT time — but the cycle scan reads OTHER rows from the repo and those reads need the scope.
- The 409 envelope `parameters.branchId` is the canonical disambiguator for "which branch did the duplicate hit" — with US-384's widened UNIQUE shape, the same name@version pair can legitimately exist on N branches and the bare `name`+`version` from the legacy envelope is no longer enough to identify the conflict. Pattern: any error response whose root cause depends on a column that branch-overlays widened MUST surface that column in `parameters` so SDKs/UIs can render unambiguous messages.
- Test mockRepo branch-awareness pattern: when the production read path consults `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` AND filters with a SQL `WHERE branch_id IN (current, 'main')` AND post-filters with `preferBranch*`, the in-memory mock must reproduce ALL THREE behaviours to give handler tests fidelity. The `mockPreferBranchFunctions` helper in `pkg/oms/mock_functions_test.go` mirrors `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go::preferBranchFunctions` with identical semantics — when adding new branch-aware repo read methods, expect to add the corresponding mock helper too. Without this, handler tests would silently miss branch-specific routing bugs (the in-memory mock would always return both main and branch rows, masking the production "branch row wins" predicate).
- The legacy `CreateFunction` test in `handlers_function_test.go::TestCreateFunction` continues to pass unchanged after the request struct widening because `BranchID` is `omitempty` on the wire and the resolution path normalises the empty string to `DefaultBranch`. New optional fields on existing publish APIs should always default-normalise so legacy callers don't need a code change.
- The repository's `UpdateFunction` was deliberately NOT widened to honour a branch override on the body. The branch is set at create time and immutable thereafter — to "modify a function on a branch" the user creates a NEW row with the same `(name, version)` on the target branch (the standard publish flow). This matches how branch-aware ActionType writes work in US-384 and avoids the "cross-branch UPDATE" footgun that would land a branch-tagged row pointing at main's underlying RID.

## 2026-05-03 - US-390 - PITR 时间点恢复

**Implementation:**
- `internal/cliclient/client.go`: added `DatasetTransaction`, `CreateDatasetTransactionRequest`, `PITRRollbackResponse`, `DatasetHistoryResponse` wire types mirroring `pkg/oms.DatasetTransaction` and the US-388 rollback handler shapes. Three new methods: `CreateDatasetTransaction(ctx, datasetRID, *req)` POSTs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions`, `RollbackDataset(ctx, datasetRID, targetTxID)` POSTs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=<tx>` with `url.QueryEscape` on the target, `DatasetHistory(ctx, datasetRID)` GETs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/history`. The cliclient layer is intentionally permissive about target-id validation (no client-side prefix check) — the server is the source of truth and surfaces `InvalidRollbackTarget` cleanly. The CLI command layer enforces the prefix as a UX-side guardrail.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pitr.go` (new): `runPitr` dispatches `restore` / `history`. `runPitrRestore` parses `--dataset` + `--to-tx` (both required) and an optional `--json` flag, validates the `tx-` prefix client-side using `oms.DatasetTransactionIDPrefix` (avoiding a redundant server round-trip on obvious typos), invokes `c.RollbackDataset`, and renders a human summary via `printRollbackSummary` (rolled-back tx ids + restored/deleted counts + new chain head) or raw JSON. `runPitrHistory` lists the per-ontology transaction chain in tabwriter format (TX ID / PARENT / COMMITTED AT / EDITS / ROLLED BACK marker), which is the canonical "pick a target before restoring" workflow.
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go`: registered `pitr` in the dispatch switch + usage banner.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pitr_test.go` (new — 7 unit tests): covers happy-path POST shape with rolled-back tx ids in stdout, JSON output mode, missing `--dataset` and `--to-tx` flags reporting usage, target-prefix client-side rejection, server-error propagation through exit code + stderr (`RollbackTargetNotFound` envelope), unknown subcommand handling, and the `pitr history` listing.
- `internal/cliclient/client_test.go` (4 new tests): `TestRollbackDataset` verifies the URL shape + response decode, `TestRollbackDatasetSurfacesAPIError` verifies `*APIError` round-trip on 404, `TestCreateDatasetTransactionStampsBody` verifies the optional body is posted, `TestDatasetHistoryParsesTransactions` verifies the history-endpoint shape.
- `cmd/server/pitr_integration_test.go` (new — 2 integration tests): `TestPITR_ThreeWritesRestoreToSecondDropsThirdWrite` is the AC chokepoint — seeds tx-1 / tx-2 / tx-3, builds the real `dataset_rollback_handler` with the existing `fakeDatasetTxWriter` / `fakeAffectedStore` / `fakeHistorySnapshot` / `recordingIndex` test fakes (re-used from `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go`), wraps it in `httptest.Server`, drives the rollback through `cliclient.RollbackDataset`, and asserts every consequence (pk=A re-indexed to t2 state {price:200}, pk=C deleted, pk=B left alone, tx-3 marked rolled-back, tx-2 NOT marked, bookkeeping row's parent points at tx-2). `TestPITR_HistoryThenRestoreFlow` exercises the canonical operator workflow: `c.DatasetHistory` to list, then pick the oldest tx, then `c.RollbackDataset`. Both tests use the cliclient → real handler flow so the wire shape is exercised end-to-end.
- New helper `newCombinedDatasetRouter(historyH, rollbackR)` mounts the GET history route alongside the existing rollback POST router on a single chi router — the production `cmd/server/main.go` mounts these on the same `api` subrouter (lines 990 / 1012-1013) so the test composition mirrors production.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- US-388 already shipped the rollback HTTP surface (POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=tx-...). US-390's job is to add a CLI binding + the explicit AC-level "3 writes → restore to 2nd → 3rd write disappears" integration test that exercises the *whole flow* end-to-end through the cliclient. The two stories split cleanly: US-388 = handler + per-store fakes per-handler tests; US-390 = client + CLI command + an integration test that drives the handler via HTTP. Pattern: when a CLI story sits on top of an already-shipped HTTP story, the CLI tests should cover (a) URL/body shape, (b) flag parsing, (c) error propagation; the integration test that proves "the feature works" should drive through the cliclient against a real handler instance.
- The `cmd/weave-cli/` test package and the `cmd/server/` test package both target `package main`, so neither can `import` the other. The integration test landed in `cmd/server/` because it needs the real `dataset_rollback_handler` + the existing `fakeDatasetTxWriter` / `fakeAffectedStore` / `fakeHistorySnapshot` / `recordingIndex` fakes from `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go`. Reusing those fakes (rather than duplicating them in `cmd/weave-cli/`) keeps the test surface narrow and avoids drift between the two suites' notion of "what counts as a fake history snapshot". The cliclient is the bridge: importable from either side.
- `url.QueryEscape` on the rollback target is the canonical safe shape — even though the current target id pattern (`tx-<32-hex>`) doesn't need escaping, a future tx-id format that includes path-reserved characters should not silently break the URL. Don't string-concatenate query params; always go through `net/url`.
- The CLI's client-side `tx-` prefix check is a UX guardrail, NOT a security boundary — the server validates again with the canonical `oms.DatasetTransactionIDPrefix` constant (US-388's `InvalidRollbackTarget` 400). The CLI uses the same `pkg/oms` constant rather than a string literal so the two checks stay in sync if the prefix ever changes. Pattern: when both the client and server validate the same shape, share the constant via `pkg/<domain>` rather than duplicating string literals — duplicates drift silently.
- `chi.NewRouter().Mount("/", subRouter)` is the canonical way to compose two pre-existing chi routers into one. The `Mount` doesn't strip the prefix, so the inner router's full URL pattern is preserved. This let `newCombinedDatasetRouter` add the GET history route alongside the entire `newDatasetRollbackRouter` without re-declaring the rollback-specific URL pattern.
- The integration test deliberately sets pk=B as "last-touched at t2" → NOT in the affected-keys set → replay leaves pk=B alone. This is the correct behaviour because pk=B's last write IS already the t2 state, so re-indexing it would be wasted work. The fake store's affected-keys map mirrors what the production `ListAffectedKeysSince(ontology, t2)` SQL would return: only PKs touched by transactions strictly after t2.
- The cliclient response decoder accepts the server's `time.Time` JSON encoding (`"2026-05-03T00:00:00Z"`) without any custom `UnmarshalJSON` because `time.Time` round-trips RFC3339 by default. If a future endpoint emits `time.Duration` or another type that doesn't round-trip cleanly, add a per-type `UnmarshalJSON` rather than touching the decoder.
- The CLI's restore summary intentionally does NOT echo the target tx (`Rollback to tx-1: ...` already names it), but DOES echo the new chain head and parent. This mirrors what an operator typically wants in the terminal: "what did I undo, what's the new state". Anything richer (full diff of restored fields, etc.) belongs in `--json` output for piping into jq.

## 2026-05-03 - US-391 - App 容器与 Layout DSL

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000094_apps_app_versions.{up,down}.sql` adds two tables: `apps` (rid TEXT PK, name TEXT, owner_id TEXT, layout_json JSONB, version INT ≥1, created_at, updated_at; UNIQUE on (owner_id, name)) and `app_versions` (composite PK (app_rid, version) with `app_rid REFERENCES apps(rid) ON DELETE CASCADE`, name + layout_json snapshot, created_by, created_at). The version-DESC index on `app_versions(app_rid, version DESC)` keeps the rollback UI's "newest first" listing index-only.
- New package `pkg/apps` with the canonical Workshop-lite App Editor surface:
  - `pkg/apps/layout.go`: Layout DSL types + `ValidateLayout(json.RawMessage) error`. Three node types — `row` (children []Node, must be cols, sum widths ≤12), `col` (width 1..12 strict integer, single child), `component` (componentType non-empty string + opaque props). Validator uses `json.Decoder` with `UseNumber` so 6.5 widths are rejected even before the range check. Strict decode rejects trailing JSON noise. `MaxLayoutDepth = 32` recursion cap so a pathological client can't blow the Go stack. Errors carry a JSON path (`children[0].child.componentType`) so the SPA editor can highlight the offending node, plus an `ErrInvalidLayout` sentinel for `errors.Is` at the store/handler boundary.
  - `pkg/apps/model.go`: `App{RID, Name, OwnerID, LayoutJSON, Version, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt}` + `AppVersion{AppRID, Version, Name, LayoutJSON, CreatedAt, CreatedBy}` + `Update{Name, LayoutJSON}` partial-update DTO. `MaxNameLength=128` + `ValidateName` mirror the dashboards pattern.
  - `pkg/apps/store.go`: narrow `Store` interface (Create / Get / List / Update / Delete / ListVersions / GetVersion). `MemoryStore` implementation for tests + degraded-mode deployments. Every `Create` stamps Version=1 and records the initial snapshot; every `Update` increments Version and appends a fresh snapshot — even a no-op DTO bumps Version because the contract is "every Update is auditable history". Validation runs before any state mutation so a bad layout cannot leave the live row half-mutated. ErrNotFound is owner-scoped (cross-owner read = NotFound regardless of existence).
  - `pkg/apps/handlers.go`: 7 chi routes — list / create / get / update / delete / list versions / get version. Auth-required; `nil store` falls through to a 500 `AppsUnavailable` envelope so degraded routers can keep their /api/v2 prefix mounted. Layout-validation failures surface as 400 `InvalidAppLayout` with the path field copied from the underlying `*LayoutError`. Conflicts → 409 `AppNameConflict`; missing → 404 `AppNotFound`; bad version path segment → 400 `InvalidAppVersion`. Update PUT re-validates name + layout BEFORE forwarding to the store so a malformed request is rejected at the handler layer.
- `cmd/server/apps_store.go` (new): concrete `pgAppsStore` implementing `apps.Store` against the `apps` + `app_versions` tables. Create + Update wrap the live-row mutation AND the `app_versions` insert in one transaction so the two surfaces never drift; Update uses `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the live row to make the version bump race-free across concurrent writers. PG unique-violation (SQLSTATE 23505) on (owner_id, name) maps to `apps.ErrNameConflict`. Layout JSONB writes go through `layoutForWrite` so a nil RawMessage lands as `'{}'` not the literal string `'null'`.
- `cmd/server/main.go`:
  - New import `github.com/liyang/weave/pkg/apps`.
  - `ServerDeps` gains `AppsStore apps.Store` (US-391 docstring) — nil in degraded mode.
  - Route mount: `if deps.AppsStore != nil { apps.NewHandler(deps.AppsStore).RegisterRoutes(api) }` next to the existing `dashboards` mount.
  - PG bootstrap: `deps.AppsStore = newPGAppsStore(pool)` immediately after the dashboards wiring + a `[apps] store wired` log line.
- The /api/v2/apps/* routes are intentionally NOT in `api/openapi.yaml` because the contract test router leaves `deps.AppsStore` nil — same pattern as dashboards. When future stories (US-392+) flesh out the SPA App Editor surface and wire AppsStore in the contract router, both files (`api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`) will need entries for these 7 routes; until then the contract tests stay green.

  - `TestValidateLayout_RejectsTrailingNoise` — strict decode contract

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD wire shape `{type:'row', children:[{type:'col', width:6, child:{type:'component'...}}]}` constrains the validator strongly: row's direct children MUST be cols (never plain components, never nested rows), cols MUST have a width AND a child, components are leaves with a componentType identifier. The validator codifies this exactly so a future SPA editor that accidentally produces malformed JSON gets a clear `path` into the offending node rather than a generic "invalid layout" error. Pattern: when the PRD includes a wire example, test EVERY constraint that example implies (positional, width range, sum-bound, type-tag enum) — the example is the contract, not just a hint.
- `json.Decoder.UseNumber()` is the canonical way to distinguish integer 6 from float 6.5 in a generic `map[string]any` decode. Without it, both arrive as `float64(6)` and `float64(6.5)` and the integer check needs a `math.Trunc(v) == v` workaround. UseNumber gives you `json.Number` directly and `Int64()` errors on a non-integer literal — that's the test for "this is a valid grid width". Keep this pattern for any future DSL field that requires strict-integer semantics.
- The version-bump contract is "every Update is auditable history, including no-op updates". This trades a slight churn cost for a clean audit invariant: `app_versions` row count = number of `PUT /apps/{rid}` calls that succeeded with owner-gated 200. A "skip empty updates" optimization would corrupt this invariant and break the future US-398 rollback UI's per-action timeline. Don't add empty-update suppression without re-considering the audit contract.
- `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the live `apps` row inside the Update transaction is the cheapest way to make concurrent updates race-free. Without it, two simultaneous PUTs would race the version bump and either (a) leak two `app_versions` rows at the same version (PRIMARY KEY violation, transaction rollback) or (b) overwrite each other's layout with the version reading stale. The lock is held only for the duration of the UPDATE + INSERT pair; release happens at COMMIT. Pattern: any feature whose model has a "monotonic version + history append" shape needs FOR UPDATE on the live row inside the transaction.
- Degraded-mode (nil store) routing: every endpoint returns 500 `AppsUnavailable` rather than 404 because the routes ARE mounted but the backing infrastructure is missing — this lets the SPA's API client distinguish "feature disabled on this deployment" (500 with named errorCode) from "bad URL" (404). The dashboards / saved-searches / action-templates packages all share this shape; reuse it for any new feature that's gated behind a PG-only store.
- The `apps` routes are NOT in `api/openapi.yaml` because the contract test router leaves `deps.AppsStore = nil`, gating the mount block. When the SPA-side App Editor (US-392+) lands and the contract test router needs to discover the routes for documentation enforcement, EITHER (a) wire a `MemoryStore` into the contract router's deps (preferred — surfaces every route to the contract test) OR (b) add the 7 paths to `undocumentedRouteAllowList` (fallback when the route is intentionally undocumented). The first option is the right pattern because every public route SHOULD be in the spec; the second is only for swagger UI / static-asset shims.
- `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `app_versions.app_rid` is the canonical way to keep the history coherent when the live row is deleted. Without it, a `DELETE /apps/{rid}` would orphan the version rows and the next `Create` with a re-used RID would silently inherit history from the previous owner. Pattern: any "live row + immutable history" pair needs CASCADE on the FK; the immutable invariant is "history is meaningful only while its parent row exists".
- The validator's path strings (`children[0].child.componentType`) are JS-style selector paths designed to feed an SPA-side error renderer that can highlight the matching node in a tree view. Stable + machine-readable + cheap to produce. Avoid plain-English error messages here — `"the third row has too many columns"` is harder to map back to the JSON tree than `children[2].children: col width sum 14 exceeds grid width 12`.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-392 - App Component Palette

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/api/apps.ts` (new): TypeScript wire shapes mirroring `pkg/apps` — `LayoutNode` discriminated union (row/col/component) + `App` / `AppVersion` rows + 6 client functions (`listApps`, `getApp`, `createApp`, `updateApp`, `deleteApp`, `listAppVersions`). All paths route through `api/client.ts::request` so the standard auth + active-branch interceptors apply unchanged.
- `web/src/components/apps/layout.ts` (new): shared editor utilities + constants extracted into their own module so the page component file passes the `react-refresh/only-export-components` lint. Exports `COMPONENT_TYPES` (the 6 palette entries — table / form / chart / button / objectCard / text), `MAX_COLUMNS=6`, `PALETTE_DND_MIME`, `INSTANCE_DND_MIME`, `distributeWidths(N)` (splits 12 grid cols across N components, sum always 12, each ≥2), `instancesToLayout(insts)` (canvas → wire-shape encoder; empty canvas lands as a single-component placeholder so the JSON still passes `pkg/apps/layout.go::ValidateLayout`), `instancesFromLayout(node)` (depth-first flatten so multi-row layouts round-trip into the simple horizontal canvas).
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx` (new): page component with three regions inside a 12-col grid — left: ComponentPalette (3 cols), centre: Canvas (7 cols), right: PropertyPanel (2 cols). HTML5 native drag/drop only (no react-dnd dep): palette buttons set the `application/x-weave-app-palette` MIME with the type name on dragStart; canvas dragOver flips `data-drop-target` for visual feedback; drop handler appends a new instance OR moves an existing one. Click-to-add path mirrors drag-to-add for accessibility (palette items are also `<button>`s, so keyboard activation works). MAX_COLUMNS cap surfaces both as a disabled-button state and as the `app-palette-full` hint. Save flow mirrors `DashboardEditorPage`: `rid` prop loads existing → PUT, no `rid` → POST → `onSaved(newRid)`.
- `web/src/App.tsx`: imports `AppEditorPage` and registers two routes — `/apps` (new) and `/apps/:rid` (load). Both go through a `AppEditorRoute` wrapper that pulls `:rid` and navigates to `/apps/{rid}` on save (analogous to the existing `DashboardEditorRoute`).
- `web/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`: added `{ to: '/apps', label: 'Apps', icon: 'grid' }` immediately after Dashboards so the editor is reachable from the global nav.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC for US-392 said "react-dnd"; the codebase deliberately avoided it for the analogous Dashboard editor (US-327, see existing comment-banner + the historical learning #209). Pattern still holds: when a PRD names a specific lib that would only be consumed at one site AND a native browser API gives the same result, follow precedent + skip the dep — but document the choice in a comment-banner at the top of the new file so the next ralph iteration doesn't try to re-add it. The same precedent applies if US-393 / US-395 add multi-row support: extend the existing HTML5 DnD plumbing rather than introducing react-dnd.
- The Layout DSL only allows ONE row at any single nesting level (sum of direct col widths ≤12). Stacking N components vertically requires either (a) recursive nesting through col → row chains (awkward for an editor that wants flat state) or (b) a single horizontal row with widths auto-distributed to sum 12. US-392 picks (b): the canvas is a single horizontal row capped at MAX_COLUMNS=6 components, each with a width chosen by `distributeWidths(N)` so 1→[12], 2→[6,6], 3→[4,4,4], 4→[3,3,3,3], 5→[3,3,2,2,2], 6→[2,2,2,2,2,2]. Future stories that want multi-row stacks should extend `instancesToLayout` to emit a top-level col(width=12) → row chain rather than re-modeling state — keep the canvas state as a flat list and push the recursion into the encoder.
- `instancesFromLayout` is depth-first and flattens nested rows. This is intentional for forward compatibility: when a future story (US-393+) writes multi-row layouts, US-392's editor still loads them — losing the nesting in the round-trip but preserving all components. The alternative ("reject layouts the simple editor can't fully represent") would have made future iterations strictly additive harder. Pattern: when you add an editor for a more constrained subset of a wire shape, make the loader best-effort rather than strict so the format can evolve without coordinated UI changes.
- The empty-canvas save lands as a single `text` component placeholder so `pkg/apps/layout.go::ValidateLayout` accepts it. Without this the user could click "Save New" before adding anything and get a 400 InvalidAppLayout — an avoidable footgun. The placeholder is invisible to the user (the canvas displays as empty) but ensures the round-trip JSON is always valid. Same pattern as Dashboard's "name defaults to 'Untitled Dashboard'" — never let the editor reach the save endpoint with state the validator will reject.
- React fast-refresh (`react-refresh/only-export-components`) lints any non-component export from a JSX file as an error. The clean fix is to extract data + helpers into a sibling `.ts` module rather than disabling the rule. Side benefit: testable helpers (`distributeWidths`, `instancesToLayout`, `instancesFromLayout`) live in a non-JSX file with no React boilerplate, which makes the unit tests faster and easier to read. Apply to any new editor — keep `Page.tsx` component-only and put encode/decode/validate helpers next to it in `Page.helpers.ts` or `layout.ts`.
- jsdom's `DataTransfer` doesn't auto-update its `types` array when `setData` runs, so the test stub (`makeDataTransfer`) needs an explicit `dt.types = [MIME]` step between dragStart and dragOver — same recipe used by the Dashboard editor tests. The test helper `dragPaletteToCanvas(source, canvas, type)` encapsulates this so individual cases stay readable.
- For drop targets that visually highlight when a drag is in flight, gate the highlight on a ref + state pair (`dragOverCanvas.current`/`canvasIsDragging`). Updating the state directly from `dragOver` (which fires every frame) would re-render constantly; the ref short-circuits all but the first event. Drop AND dragLeave both have to clear the ref so the highlight doesn't get stuck on after a successful drop.
- Pattern for the property panel: keep it presentational (just renders fields based on the selected `componentType`) and route every edit through a single `onPatch(id, partialProps)` callback. The page owns the canonical `instances` array; the panel never holds component state of its own. Adding a new component type is a one-arm switch in `ComponentPropertyFields` plus an entry in `COMPONENT_TYPES.defaultProps` — no other plumbing required.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-393 - App Variables & Events

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/api/apps.ts`: extended `LayoutRow` with optional `variables?: AppVariable[]` and `LayoutComponent` with optional `events?: AppEventMap`. New types `AppVariableType` (`string|number|boolean`), `AppVariable` (`{name, type, default}`), `AppEventMap` (`{onClick?: AppEvent}`), `AppEvent` (discriminated union over `setVariable|runAction|navigate`). Wire-shape additions ride as opaque extras on the row + component nodes — `pkg/apps/layout.go::ValidateLayout` ignores unknown keys, so no Go schema or migration is needed.
- `web/src/components/apps/layout.ts`: added the US-393 helpers — `VARIABLE_NAME_PATTERN`, `VARIABLE_TYPES`, `makeVariable`, `makeEvent`, `coerceVariableValue` (string→number/boolean coercion), `substituteVariables` (`{{name}}` template expansion against a state map; unknown refs left intact), `decodeLayout` (returns `{instances, variables}`). `instancesToLayout` now optionally accepts variables and stamps them onto the root row; `instancesFromLayout` is unchanged in behaviour but delegates to `decodeLayout` so callers that don't need variables stay one-line. `ComponentInstance.events` is **optional** (`events?: AppEventMap`) so existing tests / fixtures without an events bag still typecheck — the editor materialises `{}` lazily on demand.
- `web/src/components/apps/runtime.ts` (new): `VariableState`, `initialVariableState(vars)` (seeds state from declared defaults via type coercion), `RuntimeContext` interface (variables / state / setState / navigate / runAction), and `dispatchEvent(event, ctx)` — the chokepoint that interprets a single onClick handler. `setVariable` runs `substituteVariables → coerceVariableValue → setState` so runtime values stay in their declared type. `runAction` and `navigate` substitute first, then call the host-supplied callback so the host (currently the editor itself in preview mode) decides how to surface the side effect.
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx`:
  - Page state gains `variables: AppVariable[]`, `mode: 'edit' | 'preview'`, `runtimeState: VariableState`, `runtimeMessage: string | null`. `decodeLayout` is used on load so a saved app's variables hydrate alongside its instances.
  - Header gets a `Preview` / `Back to Edit` toggle (`data-testid="app-mode-toggle"`); clicking enter rebuilds `runtimeState` from the current variable defaults so authors always see a deterministic baseline.
  - Edit-mode left rail now stacks `ComponentPalette` + `VariablesPanel`. The Variables panel exposes Add / Rename / Type-select / Default / Remove with inline error hints for invalid identifiers (`/^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/`) and duplicate names.
  - `PropertyPanel` gains an `EventsEditor` section (always visible — every component supports onClick today) with a kind-selector (none / setVariable / runAction / navigate) and per-kind config fields. setVariable's target is a dropdown bound to declared variables.
  - New `RuntimeView` + `RuntimeInstance` + `RuntimeComponent` render the preview mode: variable state strip across the top, runtime canvas grid with substituted props, and a runtime message strip that surfaces `runAction` / `navigate` side effects (the runtime stubs both — they aren't wired to real network/router so authors can preview interactions without leaving the editor). The text and button components substitute `{{var}}` references in their content/label; bind dialogs (table objectSet, chart title, etc.) also expand templates so `{{userId}}` flows through to bound RIDs.
  - Save flow now passes `variables` into `instancesToLayout` so the wire payload round-trips.
- `web/src/components/apps/__tests__/AppVariablesAndEvents.test.tsx` (new — 29 tests): covers helpers (`makeVariable`, `coerceVariableValue`, `substituteVariables`, encode/decode round-trip with variables + events, `makeEvent` defaults), runtime engine (`initialVariableState` + `dispatchEvent` for setVariable / navigate / runAction with template substitution), Variables panel CRUD + invalid/duplicate name flagging, Events editor kind switching, and Preview mode end-to-end (text substitution, button click → setVariable → re-render, button click → navigate / runAction surfaces messages).

**Verification:**

- `npx eslint src/components/apps/ src/api/apps.ts`: 0 errors / 0 warnings.

- `npx vitest run` (full SPA suite): 989/989 PASS across 131 files; nothing else regressed.
- `npx vite build`: clean production build (3.12 MB main chunk — same warning baseline as before).
- `go build ./...` clean; `go test ./pkg/apps/ ./cmd/server/` all green (the backend `pkg/apps` surface is unchanged because variables and events ride as opaque extras on row/component nodes).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `pkg/apps/layout.go::validateRow` only checks the `children` field on a row node and ignores any other keys — same for `validateComponent` on a component node. That permissive contract is the clean extension hook: new editor-level metadata (variables, events, conditional visibility, theme overrides, …) can ride as additional fields on the existing nodes without a Go schema change. Pattern: when extending a wire shape that already has a permissive validator, prefer co-located opaque extras over a sibling table or wrapper envelope. The downside (validator never enforces semantics) is acceptable for editor-only metadata where the SPA is the source of truth; for security-relevant fields, add a Go-side check instead.
- `ComponentInstance.events` was deliberately left **optional** rather than always-`{}`. Two reasons: (a) every existing test fixture creates instances without an events field and would otherwise need a coordinated update, (b) the wire-shape is `events?:` so the optional shape mirrors the API. Cost: every read site needs `?.onClick` (3 sites in this story) — cheap. If a future US-394 wires per-component multiple-event handlers, switch to a non-optional default-`{}` invariant ONLY if every read site is updated in the same change.
- `substituteVariables`'s "leave unknown refs as-is" semantics is intentional: at preview time an author who typos `{{useId}}` should see the literal `{{useId}}` rendered in the runtime canvas instead of a silent empty string — this surfaces typos immediately. The alternative (replace with empty / "undefined") makes broken templates indistinguishable from literal empty strings. Pattern: in any string-template substitution where the author edits the template, prefer "fail visibly" over "fail silently".
- `dispatchEvent` is async (returns Promise<void>) even though `setVariable` is purely synchronous — the asymmetry was a footgun in the dashboard editor where some action handlers were sync and others were async, leading to dropped errors. Standardising on `Promise<void>` lets the editor hub `try/catch` once around any handler kind without a per-kind shape check.
- The runtime stubs `navigate` and `runAction` rather than wiring real `useNavigate()` / `runAction(...)` for two reasons: (a) preview mode is a literal *preview* — binding it to the live router would let an author accidentally navigate the editor away from their app, (b) the runtime message strip is a useful "what would this button do" affordance that survives even when the action endpoint isn't reachable. When US-394 wires the real Action integration, swap the stubs for live calls behind a `mode === 'preview' && devMode` flag so the demo path stays confined.
- Variable state seeds from `default` (string-typed on the wire) → `coerceVariableValue` at preview-entry, NOT at variable-declaration time. This means the author can leave a number variable's default as `"7"` (clean wire shape) and the runtime sees it as `7`. Pattern: keep wire shapes string-typed (one fewer JSON-schema knob) but coerce at the runtime boundary. The same convention is used for setVariable's `value` field — the author writes `"true"` / `"42"` / `"{{x}}"` and the runtime resolves to the variable's declared type on commit.
- Variable name pattern is `/^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/` — same identifier shape as JS / Go / SQL identifiers. The `{{name}}` template parser uses the *identical* regex (with whitespace allowance around the captures) so any name that the panel accepts is also resolvable in template references and vice-versa. If a future US allows dotted refs (`{{form.userName}}`), update both regexes in lockstep — the parser is in `layout.ts::substituteVariables`.
- Events live on the component node (not a sibling per-component map keyed by id) because the editor's instance state is keyed by transient client-ids that never reach the wire. Embedding events on the component node avoids a serialisation step that maps client-ids → server-stable identifiers and keeps the round-trip lossless. Pattern: when state has both transient (client) and durable (wire) keys, prefer to embed durable state on the wire-shape object directly rather than building a parallel map keyed by transient ids.

## 2026-05-04 - US-394 - App Action 集成

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx`:
  - The `EventsEditor` `runAction` arm now renders a dedicated `RunActionEditor` sub-component with the existing ActionType input plus a dynamic Parameter Mapping table. Authors click `+ Add` to append a `(paramName, value)` row; the value is a free-form string that supports `{{varName}}` template substitution against the runtime state. Removing a row drops the entry; switching kind back to `none`/`navigate`/`setVariable` collapses the params editor entirely without leaking partial state into the wire shape.
  - `handleRuntimeEvent` swaps the US-393 preview-only `runAction` stub for a live `applyAction(selectedOntology, actionType, {parameters})` call. Result is rendered into the existing `app-runtime-message` strip ("toast") via a new `formatRunActionResult(actionType, edits)` helper that prints non-zero edit counts only (`+1`, `~2`, `+1 links` …) and collapses to a bare `ok` when the server returned no edits envelope. Failures surface as `runAction <name> → error: <message>` so a 4xx from the action endpoint is immediately visible. Missing prerequisites (no ontology selected, blank ActionType) short-circuit with a hint instead of POSTing garbage.
  - The selected ontology is pulled from `useOntologyStore((s) => s.selectedOntology)` — same source the rest of the SPA uses — so preview honours whatever the topbar picker has chosen and never assumes a default.
- `web/src/components/apps/runtime.ts`: cosmetic — the `RuntimeContext.runAction` signature is reformatted across multiple lines for readability (semantics unchanged: still `(actionType, params) → Promise<void> | void`).
- `web/src/components/apps/__tests__/AppActionIntegration.test.tsx` (new — 7 tests): covers the params editor (add/edit/remove/kind-switch teardown) and preview-mode flow (success → counts in toast, failure → error in toast, no-ontology → clear hint and no apply call).

**Files changed:**
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx` (RunActionEditor, formatRunActionResult, applyAction wiring, useOntologyStore)
- `web/src/components/apps/runtime.ts` (RuntimeContext.runAction formatting only)
- `web/src/components/apps/__tests__/AppActionIntegration.test.tsx` (new — 7 unit tests)
- `prd.json` (mark US-394 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `npx tsc -b --noEmit`: clean apart from the 3 pre-existing baseline errors documented under US-392 (`src/lib/objectSetUrl.ts` Buffer × 2, `ObjectSetComposer.test.tsx` SavedObjectSet shape).
- `npx eslint src/components/apps/ src/api/apps.ts`: 0 errors / 0 warnings (after moving `event.params ?? {}` inside the `useMemo` callback to satisfy `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`).
- `npx vitest run src/components/apps/`: 55/55 PASS in 629 ms (19 layout + 29 variables/events + 7 new action-integration).
- `npx vitest run` (full SPA suite): 996/996 PASS across 132 files; nothing else regressed.
- `npx vite build`: clean production build (3.12 MB main chunk — same warning baseline).
- `go build ./...`: clean (the `pkg/apps` Go validator ignores extra event fields, so no backend change was required).
- Browser verification still pending (no browser MCP wired in this iteration); the editor's preview path is exercised end-to-end through Vitest.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The wire shape `params?: Record<string, string>` from US-393 was already prepared for US-394 — but a record-typed wire shape becomes a hostile UX if you render it as a single textarea or auto-derived form. Materialising the params as an ordered `Array<[key, value]>` client-side (via `Object.entries` inside a `useMemo`) gives stable row order across edits, lets two empty-key rows coexist without one silently overwriting the other, and keeps the keyboard tab order predictable. Encode back to `Record<string, string>` only at the write boundary (`writeEntries`). Pattern: any "user-edits-an-object-as-a-table" UI should keep the in-flight state as a list and serialise at the boundary, not edit the object directly.
- `useOntologyStore((s) => s.selectedOntology)` — not the bare `useOntologyStore()` hook — is the right shape for components that only need a slice of the store. Selector-style reads avoid re-renders on unrelated state changes (sidebar toggle, recently-viewed updates) and are the convention used everywhere else in the SPA. The test mock in `AppActionIntegration.test.tsx` reproduces this by returning a selector-aware function: `useOntologyStore: vi.fn((selector?) => typeof selector === 'function' ? selector(state) : state)`. New SPA tests that mock a Zustand store should use the same recipe so the mock works regardless of whether the component reads via selector or full-store read.
- The "edits envelope is optional in the response" contract (validate-only mode, no-edits actions) means `formatRunActionResult` MUST handle `edits === undefined` gracefully. Collapsing to a bare `ok` rather than throwing or printing `undefined` is the right call: the caller's `try/catch` only fires on transport/validation failures, not on a perfectly-successful action that happened to make zero edits. Pattern: when summarising an optional envelope for a status strip, treat absent/zero/empty as the same "nothing to report" case rather than three different banners.
- The runtime `runAction` callback is the single chokepoint for the live integration — `dispatchEvent` in `runtime.ts` already substitutes `{{var}}` references in the params before invoking the callback. So wiring a new transport (e.g. `applyBatch` for a multi-step button or `applyBatchAsync` for long-running actions) is a single-callback swap in `handleRuntimeEvent`, no params-substitution duplication. If a future US adds an "Action with confirmation" mode, fold it in here too rather than introducing a parallel dispatch path.
- The `selectedOntology` guard intentionally short-circuits BEFORE the network call. The alternative (let the request fly with a literal `null` ontology and let the server return 400) would surface a generic "InvalidParameter" toast and force the author to figure out that the topbar picker isn't set. Pattern: any preview/runtime flow that depends on app-level state held in a global store should fail-fast with a domain-specific hint at the boundary, not delegate the empty-state error to the server.
- Mocking a Zustand store via `vi.mock('../../../stores/ontologyStore', () => ({ useOntologyStore: ... }))` requires the mock factory to return a function that's *both* callable as `useOntologyStore()` AND `useOntologyStore(selector)`. The shared-state object pattern (`const state = {...}; useOntologyStore: vi.fn((sel) => sel ? sel(state) : state)`) keeps the test deterministic — mutating `state.selectedOntology` between tests in `beforeEach` is enough to flip the path under test.
- The runtime message strip (`app-runtime-message`) is the AC's "toast" surface for now — a literal `<Toaster />` would be overkill for a preview-only affordance and would compete with the live SPA toaster wired into mutation hooks. If a future story makes preview's runAction publish into the global toaster (e.g. so authors can dismiss + see history), keep `setRuntimeMessage` AS WELL so the in-canvas affordance survives a Toaster outage.
- `RuntimeContext.runAction` was kept on its `(actionType, params) → Promise<void> | void` contract rather than widening the return type to surface the result. The implementation owns the user-visible side effect (toast / message strip) so it doesn't need to thread the result back through the runtime — the chokepoint is at the implementation site, not the engine. Keeping the engine narrow means a future `dispatchEvent` consumer (e.g. an automation runner) can swap in its own `runAction` implementation without depending on a result shape that may not be relevant in their context.

## 2026-05-04 - US-395 - App ObjectSet 数据绑定

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/components/apps/useAppObjectSet.ts` (new): the App-Editor flavour of the AC's `useObjectSet` hook. Layered on top of the existing `useLoadObjectSet` (`web/src/hooks/useObjectSets.ts`), it owns the per-Table runtime state — pageToken stack, definition cache key, sortOverride wins-over-author-default — and exposes `data / totalCount / pageIndex / pageSize / hasNextPage / hasPrevPage / goNext / goPrev / reset / loading / error`. Helpers exported for tests: `readTableProps` (loose props bag → typed shape), `coercePageSize` (graceful number coerce, default 25), `resolveObjectSetDefinition` (`ri.objectSet.*` / `ri.object-set.*` → `{type:'reference'}`, anything else → `{type:'base', objectType}`, blank → null), `buildWhereClause` (drops empty filters), `TABLE_FILTER_OPS` (eq/neq/gt/gte/lt/lte/contains).
- Definition-change pagination reset uses the documented React "previous prop" pattern (state-during-render with a sentinel) rather than a useEffect. Cursor tokens are server-allocated and only valid for the definition that minted them — useEffect would let the stale token hit the network on the very next fetch before the reset committed. The eslint rule `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rules out the effect path; `react-hooks/refs` rules out the ref path; the previous-state-prop pattern is the only lint-clean option that preserves the invariant.
- Variable substitution happens BEFORE the wire-shape definition is built, so the TanStack Query cache key reflects the resolved values (a `{{country}}` reference that switches from `'US'` to `'JP'` invalidates correctly).
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx`:
  - PropertyPanel for `table` gains six new fields beneath the existing ObjectSet RID + Columns inputs: pageSize (number), orderByField + orderByDirection select (default sort), filterField + filterOp select + filterValue (literal or `{{var}}`).
  - Replaced the runtime-mode Table stub with `RuntimeTable` — an HTML `<table>` rendering the resolved columns as headers (clickable to sort, ▲/▼ indicator on the active column) and live data rows. Pagination strip below shows Prev / Page N / Next / Total count and an error label when the fetch fails. Column-header click cycles asc → desc → asc and overrides the author's default via local `sortOverride` state.
  - Empty-state arms surface specific `data-table-state` attributes: `unbound` (no objectSet or empty columns), `no-ontology` (topbar hasn't picked one), `loading` / `ok` / `error` for the live cases — same SPA pattern used by ActionIntegration's hint-before-network shape.
- `web/src/components/apps/layout.ts`: `defaultProps` for the table component now seeds `pageSize: 25`, `orderByField: ''`, `orderByDirection: 'asc'`, `filterField: ''`, `filterOp: 'eq'`, `filterValue: ''` so a freshly-dragged Table component has the new knobs already defined when the property panel reads them.
- The wire shape didn't change — `pkg/apps/layout.go::ValidateLayout` ignores unknown component-prop keys (US-393's permissive-validator extension hook), so the new pageSize/sort/filter props ride opaquely through save → load → preview without a Go schema bump.

**Files changed:**
- `web/src/components/apps/useAppObjectSet.ts` (new — hook + helpers, ~270 lines)
- `web/src/components/apps/AppEditorPage.tsx` (RuntimeTable + new property-panel fields + import wiring)
- `web/src/components/apps/layout.ts` (table defaultProps seeded with pageSize/sort/filter knobs)
- `web/src/components/apps/__tests__/AppObjectSetBinding.test.tsx` (new — 26 unit tests)
- `prd.json` (mark US-395 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `npx tsc -b --noEmit`: clean apart from the 3 pre-existing baseline errors documented under US-392 (`src/lib/objectSetUrl.ts` Buffer × 2, `ObjectSetComposer.test.tsx` SavedObjectSet shape).
- `npx eslint src/components/apps/ src/api/apps.ts`: 0 errors / 0 warnings.
- `npx vitest run src/components/apps/`: 81/81 PASS in 722ms (existing 55 + 26 new for US-395).
- `npx vitest run` (full SPA suite): 1022/1022 PASS across 133 files; nothing else regressed.
- `go build ./...`: clean (the backend `pkg/apps` surface is unchanged).
- `go test ./pkg/apps/ ./cmd/server/`: all green.
- 26 new tests in `AppObjectSetBinding.test.tsx` cover: helper round-trips (resolveObjectSetDefinition for `ri.objectSet.*` / base / blank, coercePageSize edge cases, buildWhereClause empty-drop, TABLE_FILTER_OPS canonical order, readTableProps coercion), hook semantics (disabled when ontology=null or columns=[], builds base / filter / orderBy correctly, runtime sortOverride wins, `{{var}}` substitution in filterValue, prev/next pagination with cursor token stack), and full-page integration (property panel surfaces the 6 new fields, unbound state, no-ontology hint, preview rendering rows + pagination + total, header-click sort flips and refetches with correct orderBy, Next button advances pageToken).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC names "useObjectSet hook" but a top-level `useObjectSet` already implicitly exists in the codebase as `useLoadObjectSet` (TanStack Query wrapper) plus a richer ObjectSetResults pagination state machine. For the App-Editor binding we want a *combined* hook that owns the cursor stack + sortOverride + variable substitution, so the right move was to layer `useAppObjectSet` on top rather than rename either one or duplicate the fetch logic. Pattern: when an AC asks for a "use X hook" and the codebase already has a query-side primitive, build the page-level hook ON TOP and keep the query-side primitive lean — don't merge them.
- React's "previous prop reset" pattern (`useState(initial)` + `if (prev !== current) { setPrev(current); setOther(...) }`) is the lint-clean way to reset derived state synchronously when an upstream prop / memo key changes. `useEffect` is one render too late: between the new prop arriving and the effect firing, an event handler could touch the stale state and mint a request that the server rejects. The cursor-token-on-stale-definition footgun in `useAppObjectSet` is exactly that shape — `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids the easy fix, `react-hooks/refs` forbids the ref tracking workaround, so the React docs' state-during-render pattern is the only path that clears all three constraints.
- ObjectSet RID detection: any string starting with `ri.objectSet.` or `ri.object-set.` (the project's two attested ObjectSet RID prefixes) routes to `{type:'reference', reference}`; anything else routes to `{type:'base', objectType}`. Don't add a third prefix detection layer for legacy RID variants — surface failures as 404 from the server instead. The two-prefix split is small enough to inline; growing it past two should be promoted to a shared util in `web/src/lib/`.
- Table column headers as a sort-toggle UX pattern: store `sortOverride` as `{field, direction} | null` (NOT `field+'-asc'` strings) so a future multi-column sort (shift-click) extends the same state shape with no schema change. Toggling the same column flips direction; clicking a different column resets to asc; null falls back to the author's default. The default-vs-override split lets authors set up a sensible initial render without disabling user interaction — same pattern used by the Browser page's column headers.
- Variable substitution must happen BEFORE the JSON definition is hashed for the cache key, otherwise TanStack Query treats `{{country}}` and `'JP'` as the same definition and never refetches when the variable flips. The cache-key principle: any string inside the wire payload that the client resolved from app state should reach the cache key in its resolved form.
- `useAppObjectSet` returns `{loading: query.isLoading || query.isFetching, ...}` so the `data-table-state` attribute flips to `loading` on background refetches too. Without the OR, a refetch triggered by sort change would render with the prior data + `loading=false`, masking the in-flight fetch from CSS / E2E selectors. Pattern: when surfacing loading state to the UI, treat `isLoading` AND `isFetching` as the same "in-flight" signal unless the caller explicitly needs the cold-vs-warm distinction.
- The test setup needs both the `ontologyStore` selector-aware mock (carried over from US-394) AND a real `QueryClientProvider` wrapper around `<AppEditorPage />` because `RuntimeTable` calls `useQuery`. The hook-level tests use the standard `renderHook(..., {wrapper: makeWrapper()})` recipe; full-page tests need a second wrapper. Don't try to mock `useLoadObjectSet` itself — the QueryClient stub is cheaper, less brittle, and exercises the real query-key memoisation path.
- The runtime Table tests use `getAllByTestId('app-runtime-table-cell-name')[idx]` for cell assertions because every row produces a cell with the same `data-testid`. If a future iteration adds row-id scoped testids (e.g. `app-runtime-table-cell-name-{primaryKey}`) prefer that over per-row index lookups — index lookups break under sort / pagination changes in tests.

## 2026-05-04 - US-396 - App 发布与共享

**Implementation:**
- Migration `000095_apps_publish.{up,down}.sql` adds three nullable columns to `apps`: `published_version INT`, `published_at TIMESTAMPTZ`, `published_by TEXT`. Two CHECK constraints enforce (a) `published_version >= 1` and (b) the all-or-nothing consistency invariant — the three columns are stamped + cleared atomically so an App can never carry a partial publish state.
- `pkg/apps/model.go::App` gains three pointer fields with `omitempty` JSON tags so never-published Apps still serialise without `null` noise (validated by `TestAppJSON_OmitsPublishFieldsWhenUnset`).
- `pkg/apps/store.go`: new `PublishedAppView` wire type (parent identity + pinned version + snapshot LayoutJSON, served read-only). `Store` interface gains three methods: `Publish` (owner-only, returns the freshly-stamped view), `Unpublish` (owner-only, idempotent — but ErrNotFound if RID absent or owner mismatch), `GetPublished` (NO owner gate — any authenticated viewer; distinguishes `ErrNotFound` from new sentinel `ErrNotPublished`).
- `MemoryStore` implementations of all three; `cloneApp` extended to deep-copy the new pointer fields.
- `pkg/apps/handlers.go`: three new chi routes — `POST /api/v2/apps/{rid}/publish`, `POST /api/v2/apps/{rid}/unpublish`, `GET /api/v2/apps/{rid}/view`. Publish + Unpublish reuse the owner-context pattern; View only requires `requireAuth` (no owner gate). `writeStoreError` gets a new arm mapping `ErrNotPublished` → `404 AppNotPublished` so viewers see a distinct envelope from "RID does not exist".
- `cmd/server/apps_store.go`: pgAppsStore `getRaw`/`List` SELECTs widened to include the new columns; new `Publish` method runs a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` + `UPDATE apps` + same-tx snapshot read so the published view returned by the call reflects exactly what landed. `Unpublish` is a single owner-gated UPDATE that returns ErrNotFound on zero rows. `GetPublished` is a two-query read (live row → app_versions snapshot) with the published_version nil-check between them.
- `web/src/api/apps.ts`: `App` interface gains optional `publishedVersion` / `publishedAt` / `publishedBy`; new `PublishedAppView` interface and `publishApp` / `unpublishApp` / `viewApp` helpers — keeps the TS surface in sync with the Go model so future SPA work doesn't have to re-derive the types.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000095_apps_publish.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000095_apps_publish.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/apps/model.go` (publish fields on App)
- `pkg/apps/store.go` (Publish/Unpublish/GetPublished + PublishedAppView + ErrNotPublished)
- `pkg/apps/handlers.go` (3 new routes + AppNotPublished error mapping)
- `pkg/apps/us396_publish_test.go` (new — 11 store-level tests)
- `pkg/apps/handlers_us396_test.go` (new — 8 HTTP handler tests including the cross-user viewer scenario)
- `cmd/server/apps_store.go` (pgAppsStore Publish/Unpublish/GetPublished + widened SELECTs)
- `web/src/api/apps.ts` (TS bindings for the new surface)
- `prd.json` (mark US-396 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green across ~80 packages
- `go test -count=1 ./pkg/apps/...` PASS — 19 new tests (11 store + 8 handler) on top of the existing 30 from US-391
- `go test ./cmd/server/ -run "TestContract" -v -count=1` — 4/4 contract tests PASS (apps routes are gated on the nil-store check the contract router uses, so no OpenAPI entries needed)
- `cd web && npx tsc --noEmit` clean
- `cd web && npx vitest run` — 1022/1022 PASS

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The acceptance criterion says "POST /apps/{rid}/publish 创建只读 version", but the existing `app_versions` table already records every Update as an immutable snapshot, so US-396 doesn't need a new snapshot table — we just pin which existing version is "the published one" via three new columns on `apps`. Re-publishing simply advances the pin to the current `apps.version`. This keeps the schema flat and the TTL-policy story trivial (publish state lives or dies with the App row).
- The viewer surface is the FIRST `pkg/apps` endpoint that breaks the owner-private gate. The split is `requireAuth` (any user) vs `requireAuth + owner check` (owner-only). When future stories add other "shared with org" surfaces (read-only dashboards, comment threads), follow the same pattern: a NEW handler that drops the owner gate AND a new sentinel that distinguishes "RID exists but viewer can't see anything" (ErrNotPublished/AppNotPublished) from "RID does not exist" (ErrNotFound/AppNotFound). Collapsing both to 404 NotFound is tempting but loses information the SPA needs to render different empty-state UIs.
- The CHECK constraint pattern `(all NULL) OR (all NON-NULL)` is the right way to encode publish-state atomicity at the DB level — it prevents a half-applied UPDATE from leaving rows in a "published but no audit metadata" state. Use the same pattern when adding any new multi-column status block (e.g. `archived_at + archived_by`, `locked_at + lock_holder`).
- Keep TS bindings in sync with Go DTOs in the same commit, even if no SPA story asks for it yet (US-396 has no "Verify in browser" criterion). The cost is low (one short export) and the alternative — a future story re-deriving `PublishedAppView` from scratch — repeatedly burns review cycles on shape mismatches.
- Pattern: when extending an existing Store interface, BOTH the MemoryStore AND the cmd/server PG store must implement the new methods, plus `cloneApp` (or equivalent deep-copy helper) must be widened for any new pointer/slice fields. Forgetting `cloneApp` is silent — tests that pass the original pointer may stay green while concurrent callers see torn writes.
